# Papers and theorem statements

114 distinct primary references · 2157 Lean declarations · 6 published results with incomplete formalizations · 3 literature hypotheses.

[Interactive theorem atlas](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/) · [Review selection and coverage](https://github.com/the-matter-lab/fqchem/blob/main/docs/theorems/Surveys.md)

## Aaronson & Ambainis (2014) · [The Need for Structure in Quantum Speedups](https://doi.org/10.4086/toc.2014.v010a006)

- **Paper result**. Turturean, Corollary 1.2, proves the Aaronson–Ambainis influence conjecture on the uniform Boolean cube. For a [0,1]-valued polynomial of degree at most d and variance at least ε > 0, some squared-flip influence is at least (ε/d)⁷. [aaronson_ambainis](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#AaronsonAmbainisPort.Main/AaronsonAmbainis.aaronson_ambainis)
- **Derived result**. The pinned proof converts Turturean’s Theorem 1.1 to the {0,1} cube and the unnormalized squared flip difference. The bound has coefficient 8√15/(125π⁴) multiplying ε^(5/2)/d⁶. [aaronson_ambainis_explicit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#AaronsonAmbainisPort.Main/AaronsonAmbainis.aaronson_ambainis_explicit)
- **Special case**. Applies the proved Boolean influence bound to the acceptance probability of a T-query phase circuit on independent uniform input bits, assuming positive variance at least ε. [acceptance_influential](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainis/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.acceptance_influential)
- **Special case**. Applies the explicit squared-flip influence bound with degree 2T to a chosen orbital population after T binary onsite pulses, assuming uniform independent inputs and variance at least ε. [orbitalPopulation_influential](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainis/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.orbitalPopulation_influential)
- **Supporting lemma**. Computes the uniform mean of a function on one Boolean bit for the two-orbital witness. [mean_oneBit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainis/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.mean_oneBit)
- **Derived result**. For the specific one-bit orbital probe, the Bernoulli antibonding readout has variance 1/4 and squared-flip influence 1. [antibondingInfluence](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainis/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.antibondingInfluence) · [antibondingVariance](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainis/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.antibondingVariance)
- **Derived result**. Instantiates the explicit influence inequality for a single binary pulse, the antibonding readout, and variance 1/4. [antibonding_influential](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainis/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.antibonding_influential)

## Abrams & Lloyd (1999) · [Quantum Algorithm Providing Exponential Speed Increase for Finding Eigenvalues and Eigenvectors](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevLett.83.5162)

- **Special case**. Finite Fourier orthogonality yields unit readout amplitude on the exact eigenphase grid point and zero at every other grid point. Off-grid phase error is a separate analysis. [exactGridQPE](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.PhaseEstimation/FQChem.Algorithms.exactGridQPE)
- **Supporting lemma**. The normalized finite Fourier kernel is one at the target, vanishes on other exact grid phases, and has magnitude ≤2/(Nδ) for a unit phase at chord distance at least δ from the target. [kernel_at_distinct_grid_phase](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.PhaseEstimationFilter/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.PhaseEstimationFilter.kernel_at_distinct_grid_phase) · [kernel_at_target](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.PhaseEstimationFilter/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.PhaseEstimationFilter.kernel_at_target) · [kernel_norm_le_one](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.PhaseEstimationFilter/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.PhaseEstimationFilter.kernel_norm_le_one) · [kernel_suppression](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.PhaseEstimationFilter/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.PhaseEstimationFilter.kernel_suppression)
- **Supporting lemma**. Normalizing the postselected Fourier-filter branch gives the kernel-weighted amplitudes. A positive initial ground overlap keeps the normalizer positive. [branchWeight](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.PhaseEstimationFilter/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.PhaseEstimationFilter.branchWeight) · [branch_normalized](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.PhaseEstimationFilter/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.PhaseEstimationFilter.branch_normalized) · [conditional_amplitudes](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.PhaseEstimationFilter/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.PhaseEstimationFilter.conditional_amplitudes) · [heralding_pos](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.PhaseEstimationFilter/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.PhaseEstimationFilter.heralding_pos) · [leakage_nonneg](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.PhaseEstimationFilter/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.PhaseEstimationFilter.leakage_nonneg)
- **Special case**. The conditional ground-sector weight is initial ground mass divided by branch weight. A normalized input makes the heralding probability positive and at most one. [fidelity_eq](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.PhaseEstimationFilter/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.PhaseEstimationFilter.fidelity_eq) · [heralding_probability](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.PhaseEstimationFilter/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.PhaseEstimationFilter.heralding_probability)
- **Special case**. If every excited phase is an Nth root of unity distinct from the target, the ideal target readout eliminates all excited amplitudes. The success weight is the initial ground mass. [exact_grid_preparation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.PhaseEstimationFilter/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.PhaseEstimationFilter.exact_grid_preparation)
- **Derived result**. With positive initial ground mass and all excited phases separated by chord distance δ, the finite N-point filter bounds the heralding probability and gives an explicit conditional-fidelity lower bound. [separated_preparation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.PhaseEstimationFilter/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.PhaseEstimationFilter.separated_preparation)

## Anderson (1975) · [A random-walk simulation of the Schrödinger equation: H₃⁺](https://doi.org/10.1063/1.431514)

- **Supporting lemma**. In a finite eigenbasis with nonzero initial ground overlap, normalization gives explicit Born weights. The infidelity is the excited mass divided by total filtered mass. [bornWeight](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.GroundStateProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.bornWeight) · [excited_nonneg](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.GroundStateProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.excited_nonneg) · [infidelity](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.GroundStateProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.infidelity) · [normalization](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.GroundStateProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.normalization) · [total_pos](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.GroundStateProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.total_pos)
- **Supporting lemma**. A positive lower bound on excitation energies exponentially suppresses the unnormalized excited mass, and the resulting exponential envelope tends to zero. [boundTendstoZero](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.GroundStateProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.boundTendstoZero) · [excitedBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.GroundStateProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.excitedBound)
- **Special case**. For nonnegative imaginary time, initial ground mass greater than zero and a finite gapped spectrum, infidelity is bounded by the initial excited-to-ground mass ratio times exp(−2Δτ), so ground-sector fidelity tends to one. Evolution here uses the exact diagonal filter. [projectionBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.GroundStateProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.projectionBound) · [projectionLimit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.GroundStateProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.projectionLimit)
- **Derived result**. The normalized energy excess lies between zero and the spectral width times the infidelity envelope. Finiteness of the spectrum gives an energy excess tending to zero. [energyBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.GroundStateProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.energyBound) · [energyLimit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.GroundStateProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.energyLimit)

## Andersson, Malmqvist & Roos (1992) · [Second-order perturbation theory with a complete active space self-consistent field reference function](https://doi.org/10.1063/1.462209)

- **Derived result**. For finitely many couplings and strictly positive diagonal gaps, the sum of negative squared couplings divided by their gaps is nonpositive. CASPT2 in the cited work also treats nondiagonal blocks. [mrpt2Shift](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.PerturbationTheory.Multireference/FQChem.VariationalMethods.mrpt2Shift)

## Assaraf & Caffarel (2003) · [Zero-Variance Zero-Bias Principle for Observables in Quantum Monte Carlo: Application to Forces](https://arxiv.org/abs/physics/0310035)

- **Special case**. The squared residual at a real reference energy equals the Born-weighted centered second moment plus omitted-node contributions. Eigenstates have zero moment. The converse requires Hψ to vanish at every node of ψ. [eigenstateZeroVariance](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VariationalMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VariationalMonteCarlo.eigenstateZeroVariance) · [residualDecomposition](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VariationalMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VariationalMonteCarlo.residualDecomposition) · [zeroVarianceIff](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VariationalMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VariationalMonteCarlo.zeroVarianceIff)

## Atia & Aharonov (2017) · [Fast-forwarding of Hamiltonians and exponentially precise measurements](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-017-01637-7)

- **Supporting lemma**. A word of unitary gates acts in execution order, and concatenating k copies multiplies its gate count by exactly k. These identities implement the composition in the fast-forwarding lemma. [copies_length](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FastForwarding/FQChem.Complexity.FastForwarding.copies_length) · [run_append](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FastForwarding/FQChem.Complexity.FastForwarding.run_append)
- **Supporting lemma**. A hybrid argument accumulates at most kα state-vector distance on k segments. The one-step error is assumed only for normalized inputs with a clean ancilla, while the simulated intermediate states may leak out of that subspace. [repeat_error](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FastForwarding/FQChem.Complexity.FastForwarding.repeat_error)
- **Special case**. Supplementary Lemma 2 (arXiv v2, Lemma 3). Atia–Aharonov, arXiv v2, Lemma 3. For every time t up to kT, k copies of a word for t/k simulate bounded self-adjoint evolution on clean inputs with error ≤kα and gate count ≤kB. B bounds the shorter circuits in the chosen unitary gate alphabet. [byConcatenation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FastForwarding/FQChem.Complexity.FastForwarding.byConcatenation)

## Avron & Elgart (1999) · [Adiabatic Theorem without a Gap Condition](https://doi.org/10.1007/s002200050620)

- **Paper result**. Theorem 1, bounded self-adjoint Hamiltonians on a complex Hilbert space. For a smooth finite-rank spectral projection, Schrödinger evolution of any normalized initial vector in its range has distance tending to zero uniformly over scaled time in [0,1]. The proof works for a continuous Hamiltonian path and a continuously differentiable projection, without a spectral gap or a universal rate. [gaplessAdiabaticDistance](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.GaplessAdiabatic/FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.gaplessAdiabaticDistance)
- **Paper result**. Uniform leakage in the equivalent norm-of-the-orthogonal-residual formulation, with the finite spectral projection specified by its eigenbasis. [gaplessAdiabatic](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.GaplessAdiabatic/FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.gaplessAdiabatic)
- **Derived result**. Finite rank and inclusion of every selected eigenspace in full place the projection tangent in the norm closure of commutators with the Hamiltonian. The pointwise proof uses the orthogonal complement of the kernel and finite sums of rank-one operators. [approximateSpectralCommutator](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.GaplessAdiabatic/FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.approximateSpectralCommutator)
- **Supporting lemma**. Finite spectral projection identities, a rank-one commutator formula and norm-closure arguments used to construct approximate solutions. [band_commutes](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.GaplessAdiabatic/FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.band_commutes) · [band_idempotent](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.GaplessAdiabatic/FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.band_idempotent) · [band_selfAdjoint](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.GaplessAdiabatic/FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.band_selfAdjoint) · [comm_rankOne](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.GaplessAdiabatic/FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.comm_rankOne) · [rankOne_comm_closure](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.GaplessAdiabatic/FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.rankOne_comm_closure) · [star_comm_closure](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.GaplessAdiabatic/FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.star_comm_closure)
- **Derived result**. An approximate commutator on one interval gives error bounded by its residual times the interval length plus an endpoint correction. A finite partition gives a uniform adiabatic limit from pointwise approximate solutions. [approximateCommutatorTransport](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.ApproximateCommutator/FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.approximateCommutatorTransport) · [interval_defect](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.ApproximateCommutator/FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.interval_defect)
- **Supporting lemma**. Product differentiation and a norm estimate control a transported observable. For a contractive idempotent initial projection, the intertwining error bounds leakage. [leakage_le_intertwining](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.AdiabaticTheorem/FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.leakage_le_intertwining) · [sandwichBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.AdiabaticTheorem/FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.sandwichBound) · [transportedDerivative](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.AdiabaticTheorem/FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.transportedDerivative)
- **Derived result**. For a supplied slow propagator and a bounded differentiable solution of the exact commutator equation, the intertwining defect is at most (2B+sD)/T. Avron–Elgart’s general gapless theorem allows an approximate commutator and gives convergence without a universal rate. [adiabaticIntertwining](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.AdiabaticTheorem/FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.adiabaticIntertwining)
- **Derived result**. Leakage is at most (4L/g+sD)/T when a reduced inverse of the shifted generator and a differentiable commutator correction obey the stated uniform bounds. [reducedResolventPreparation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.AdiabaticTheorem/FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.reducedResolventPreparation)
- **Special case**. A two-band generator a(s)I+gap·P(s) with fixed nonzero splitting, contractive idempotents, bounded first and second projection derivatives and supplied slow evolutions has O(1/T) leakage. The limit is at s=1. [gappedBandPreparation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.AdiabaticTheorem/FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.gappedBandPreparation) · [gappedBandPreparation_limit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.AdiabaticTheorem/FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.gappedBandPreparation_limit)

## Barenco et al. (1995) · [Elementary gates for quantum computation](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevA.52.3457)

- **Derived result**. Cuccaro majority/unmajority ripple carry lowered to actual Qatlib legal one-/two-qubit words, counting each Toffoli as 15. The k paired low bits plus outgoing carry cost at most 34k+1 primitive instructions. [circuit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedRipple/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedRipple.circuit) · [finish_action](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedRipple/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedRipple.finish_action) · [prepare_action](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedRipple/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedRipple.prepare_action)
- **Derived result**. Gidney §2.10, implemented with a borrowed-carry ripple variant. Equal register halves exchange workspace and an odd width peels its high-order controlled flip. Any k distinct addressed wires can be incremented modulo 2ᵏ with one borrowed wire in ≤720(k+3) legal one-/two-qubit Clifford+T instructions. [evenHalves](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.HalvedIncrement/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.HalvedIncrement.evenHalves) · [evenWidth](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.HalvedIncrement/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.HalvedIncrement.evenWidth) · [oneDirty](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.HalvedIncrement/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.HalvedIncrement.oneDirty)
- **Supporting lemma**. Composition and primitive Clifford+T words preserve the exact basis action and resource bounds. A Toffoli is expanded into fifteen local gates. [bit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls.bit) · [compose](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls.compose) · [controlledBit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls.controlledBit) · [toff](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls.toff) · [weaken](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls.weaken)
- **Supporting lemma**. A toggle through borrowed binary registers has a computable change in its final control predicate. Running the chain in reverse restores every temporary register. [allFlip](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls.allFlip) · [carryFlip](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls.carryFlip) · [carry_congr](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls.carry_congr) · [carry_xor](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls.carry_xor) · [readCarry](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls.readCarry) · [restore](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls.restore) · [toggleDifference](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls.toggleDifference) · [zipNames](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls.zipNames)
- **Derived result**. The borrowed-wire ladder realizes a many-controlled NOT using linear-size local gate words, following Lemma 7.2. [many](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls.many) · [manyFromPath](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls.manyFromPath)
- **Paper result**. The balanced split of Barenco et al., Lemma 7.3 and Corollary 7.4, implements a k-controlled NOT with one borrowed qubit and at most 120(k+3) addressed one- and two-qubit gates. The matrix action includes arbitrary superpositions and restores the borrowed wire. [borrowedMCX](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls.borrowedMCX) · [coherentMCX](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.BorrowedControls.coherentMCX)
- **Supporting lemma**. The two-qubit controlled NOT realizes its classical action on every computational basis ket. [applyGate_cnotGate_ket](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.GateVocabulary.LocalReflectionGates/Qatlib.Concepts.GateVocabulary.LocalReflectionGates.applyGate_cnotGate_ket)
- **Supporting lemma**. For the coherent outer control required by QSP, an arbitrary q-instruction binary decoder circuit expands to at most 415440q one- and two-qubit gates on the addressed data and signal registers. The bound is independent of all input entries. [decoder](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseControl/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseControl.decoder)
- **Supporting lemma**. On the fixed physical register, each controlled CNOT and each controlled two-bit diagonal can be placed on at most three specified bits. Three-wire exact synthesis gives a constant number of elementary gates; repeated phase indices require only two bits. [controlledCnot](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseControl/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseControl.controlledCnot) · [phase](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseControl/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseControl.phase)
- **Supporting lemma**. Gate-word certificates for placing, composing and taking adjoints of the sparse decoder instructions and for embedding each of its legal gates. These lemmas implement the local resource account. [adj](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseControl/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseControl.adj) · [code](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseControl/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseControl.code) · [concat](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseControl/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseControl.concat) · [ctrl_mul](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseControl/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseControl.ctrl_mul) · [ctrl_one](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseControl/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseControl.ctrl_one) · [ctrl_star](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseControl/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseControl.ctrl_star) · [empty](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseControl/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseControl.empty) · [localGate](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseControl/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseControl.localGate) · [phase_unit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseControl/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseControl.phase_unit) · [placed](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseControl/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseControl.placed) · [single](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseControl/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseControl.single) · [toff_invol](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseControl/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseControl.toff_invol) · [weaken](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseControl/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseControl.weaken)
- **Supporting lemma**. Explicit diagonal and permutation formulas for operations on selected wires. The two signal flags, the row, the column and every data bit have distinct physical addresses. Untouched wires retain their bit values. [place_diag](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparsePlacement/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseWires.place_diag) · [place_perm](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparsePlacement/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseWires.place_perm) · [raw_control_diag](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparsePlacement/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseWires.raw_control_diag) · [raw_control_perm](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparsePlacement/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseWires.raw_control_perm) · [raw_mul](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparsePlacement/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseWires.raw_mul) · [raw_star](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparsePlacement/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseWires.raw_star) · [triple_range](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparsePlacement/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseWires.triple_range)
- **Supporting lemma**. A controlled exchange of two n-bit address registers factors into n Fredkin gates. The proof traces every control, data and spectator wire and compiles each three-wire factor via exact elementary synthesis. [fredkin_inv](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseSwap/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseControl.fredkin_inv) · [insert_swap](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseSwap/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseControl.insert_swap) · [perm_comp](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseSwap/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseControl.perm_comp) · [swapGate](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseSwap/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseControl.swapGate) · [swapOne](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseSwap/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseControl.swapOne) · [swapSubset](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseSwap/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseControl.swapSubset)
- **Special case**. Every unitary on three specified bits admits an exact decomposition with at most 415440 elementary gates, acting identically on all other bit values. The proof applies the pinned Barenco-style Gray-code synthesis bound at n=3. [three](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseWires/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseWires.three)
- **Supporting lemma**. Explicit restriction to selected physical bits and identity on complementary wires respects products, identity and adjoints. Lifting an exact word preserves its gate count. [built_adj](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseWires/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseWires.built_adj) · [built_mul](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseWires/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseWires.built_mul) · [built_weaken](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseWires/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseWires.built_weaken) · [place_adjoint](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseWires/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseWires.place_adjoint) · [place_apply](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseWires/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseWires.place_apply) · [place_mul](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseWires/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseWires.place_mul) · [place_one](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseWires/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseWires.place_one) · [reflection_built](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseWires/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseWires.reflection_built)
- **Paper result**. Pinned exact Gray-code synthesis of any k≥1 qubit unitary by single-qubit operations and CNOT gates, with no oracle calls and an explicit bound on word length. The sparse construction applies the bound for exactly three qubits. [section8GrayUniversality](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.GateTables.GrayUniversal.Section8GrayUniversalityElem/Qatlib.Concepts.GateTables.GrayUniversal.section8GrayUniversality)
- **Supporting lemma**. Pinned local-gate semantics. The T and inverse T gates are unitary, and the compiled reflection word applies its matrix product to every input state. [run_reflectionCompile](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.GateVocabulary.LocalReflectionGates/Qatlib.Concepts.GateVocabulary.LocalReflectionGates.run_reflectionCompile) · [tGateAdj_mem_unitary](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.GateVocabulary.LocalReflectionGates/Qatlib.Concepts.GateVocabulary.LocalReflectionGates.tGateAdj_mem_unitary) · [tGate_mem_unitary](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.GateVocabulary.LocalReflectionGates/Qatlib.Concepts.GateVocabulary.LocalReflectionGates.tGate_mem_unitary)

## Beals et al. (2001) · [Quantum Lower Bounds by Polynomials](https://arxiv.org/abs/quant-ph/9802049)

- **Special case**. Applies the proved Boolean influence bound to the acceptance probability of a T-query phase circuit on independent uniform input bits, assuming positive variance at least ε. [acceptance_influential](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainis/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.acceptance_influential)
- **Special case**. Applies the explicit squared-flip influence bound with degree 2T to a chosen orbital population after T binary onsite pulses, assuming uniform independent inputs and variance at least ε. [orbitalPopulation_influential](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainis/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.orbitalPopulation_influential)
- **Supporting lemma**. Represents a binary phase oracle or coherent bypass by a degree-at-most-one real factor. Multiplying an amplitude by it adds at most one to the degree. [phaseScale_degree](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainisQuery/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.phaseScale_degree) · [phaseScale_value](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainisQuery/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.phaseScale_value) · [phase_degree](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainisQuery/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.phase_degree) · [phase_eval](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainisQuery/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.phase_eval)
- **Supporting lemma**. A fixed complex mixing matrix acts linearly on the real and imaginary polynomial components and preserves the common degree bound. [mix_degree](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainisQuery/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.mix_degree) · [mix_value](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainisQuery/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.mix_value)
- **Special case**. Beals et al., Lemma 4.1, specialized to phase queries. After T queries the real and imaginary amplitude polynomials evaluate correctly and have total degree at most T. Boolean multilinear reduction is unnecessary for these bounds. [amplitudes_degree](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainisQuery/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.amplitudes_degree) · [amplitudes_value](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainisQuery/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.amplitudes_value)
- **Supporting lemma**. Relates squared amplitudes to a complex inner product, preserves mass under phase queries and fixed unitaries, and bounds the probability of a finite basis event by [0,1]. [acceptance_mem_unitInterval](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainisQuery/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.acceptance_mem_unitInterval) · [amplitudes_normalized](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainisQuery/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.amplitudes_normalized) · [sqnorm_eq](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainisQuery/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.sqnorm_eq) · [unitary_preserves](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainisQuery/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.unitary_preserves)
- **Special case**. Beals et al., Lemma 4.2, for a T-query phase circuit. The constructed real polynomial agrees with the event probability on Boolean inputs and has total degree at most 2T. Lean uses ordinary polynomials. [acceptancePolynomial_degree](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainisQuery/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.acceptancePolynomial_degree) · [acceptancePolynomial_eval](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainisQuery/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.acceptancePolynomial_eval)
- **Derived result**. The diagonal one-particle Hamiltonian with entries 0 or π is Hermitian, and its unit-time exponential implements the sign oracle on the addressed orbitals. [onsiteHamiltonian_isHermitian](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainisOrbitals/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.onsiteHamiltonian_isHermitian) · [onsite_evolution](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainisOrbitals/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.onsite_evolution)
- **Derived result**. Amplitude and probability identities identify alternating orbital mixing and binary onsite evolution with the phase-query circuit in the polynomial method. [orbitalEvolution_eq](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainisOrbitals/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.orbitalEvolution_eq) · [orbitalPopulation_eq](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainisOrbitals/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.orbitalPopulation_eq)
- **Supporting lemma**. The one-bit interference example uses 1/√2, a normalized bonding state, and a unitary bonding/antibonding readout. [bondingNormalized](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainisOrbitals/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.bondingNormalized) · [bondingReadout_unitary](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainisOrbitals/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.bondingReadout_unitary) · [halfAmplitude_sq](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainisOrbitals/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.halfAmplitude_sq)
- **Derived result**. Calculates a two-orbital realization of a single-bit query. The antibonding probability equals the encoded bit. [antibondingPopulation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainisOrbitals/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.antibondingPopulation)
- **Supporting lemma**. The product of Boolean signs equals ±1 and is orthogonal under the cube sum to every polynomial of total degree strictly below the number of bits. [parity_cases](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrbitalSimulation/FQChem.Complexity.OrbitalSimulation.parity_cases) · [parity_orthogonal](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrbitalSimulation/FQChem.Complexity.OrbitalSimulation.parity_orthogonal)
- **Derived result**. In the library’s two-orbital model, the diagonal onsite energy is bounded by π. Evolving for time N implements a relative parity sign, which determines the antibonding population for N > 0. [evolution](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrbitalSimulation/FQChem.Complexity.OrbitalSimulation.evolution) · [hermitian](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrbitalSimulation/FQChem.Complexity.OrbitalSimulation.hermitian) · [onsite_bounds](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrbitalSimulation/FQChem.Complexity.OrbitalSimulation.onsite_bounds) · [population_parity](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrbitalSimulation/FQChem.Complexity.OrbitalSimulation.population_parity)
- **Derived result**. For individual Boolean potential-term queries, uniform additive error < 1/2 on the parity-encoding population at time N forces N ≤ 2T. Berry et al., Theorem 3, uses sparse matrix row queries and trace distance for a different Hamiltonian family. [simulation_query_lower_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrbitalSimulation/FQChem.Complexity.OrbitalSimulation.simulation_query_lower_bound)
- **Supporting lemma**. Sequential composition is list concatenation in Qatlib’s query-word semantics. A unitary oracle and unitary gate steps preserve normalization. [normalized_run](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Foundations.QueryWordCore.Circuit/Qatlib.Foundations.QueryWordCore.Circuit.IsUnitaryCircuit.normalized_run) · [run_append](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Foundations.QueryWordCore.Circuit/Qatlib.Foundations.QueryWordCore.Circuit.run_append)
- **Paper result**. Each coordinate of a q-query circuit’s output amplitude has real and imaginary polynomial representatives of degree at most q (Lemma 4.1). [amplitudePolynomials](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainisQuery/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.amplitudePolynomials)
- **Derived result**. Distinguishing the two parity classes around any threshold requires N/4 coherent whole-row queries, even when the separation is arbitrarily small. [separatedParity](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries.separatedParity)
- **Derived result**. A single phase oracle encodes the mean of N signs. The parity Fourier coefficient of its evolution can be evaluated exactly, while it vanishes in any compressed circuit amplitude with fewer than N queries. [blockEncoding](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.BlockSimulationPrecision/FQChem.Complexity.BlockSimulationPrecision.blockEncoding) · [parityCoefficient](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.BlockSimulationPrecision/FQChem.Complexity.BlockSimulationPrecision.parityCoefficient) · [zeroCoefficient](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.BlockSimulationPrecision/FQChem.Complexity.BlockSimulationPrecision.zeroCoefficient)

## Begušić & Chan (2025) · [Real-Time Operator Evolution in Two and Three Dimensions via Sparse Pauli Dynamics](https://doi.org/10.1103/PRXQuantum.6.020302)

- **Supporting lemma**. A cyclic trace identity exchanges propagation of a finite matrix state with propagation of the observable. This is a mathematical prerequisite for the Heisenberg-picture algorithm. [pictureDuality](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.RealTimePropagation.HeisenbergPicture/FQChem.Dynamics.pictureDuality)
- **Special case**. Conjugating an anticommuting string Q by a rotation generated by P with P²=I gives two terms in Q and PQ. The trigonometric rotation follows by choosing its half-angle coefficients. [pauliRotation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.RealTimePropagation.HeisenbergPicture/FQChem.Dynamics.pauliRotation)

## Bennett et al. (1997) · [Strengths and Weaknesses of Quantum Computing](https://arxiv.org/abs/quant-ph/9701001)

- **Derived result**. For the complementary projector Hamiltonian I−P, the kernel is the marked subspace and the normalized energy equals one minus its population. [energy_eq_failure](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.GroundStateSearch/FQChem.Complexity.GroundStateSearch.energy_eq_failure) · [groundSpace](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.GroundStateSearch/FQChem.Complexity.GroundStateSearch.groundSpace)
- **Supporting lemma**. Relates the marking oracle to a subspace reflection and checks normalization for both the queried trajectory and the query-free reference trajectory. [mark_eq_neg_reflection](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.GroundStateSearch/FQChem.Complexity.GroundStateSearch.mark_eq_neg_reflection) · [queried_norm](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.GroundStateSearch/FQChem.Complexity.GroundStateSearch.queried_norm) · [reference_norm](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.GroundStateSearch/FQChem.Complexity.GroundStateSearch.reference_norm)
- **Supporting lemma**. Bessel’s inequality supplies the address bound for orthonormal candidates. The sum of projection amplitudes for a normalized reference is at most √N. [orthonormal_addresses](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.GroundStateSearch/FQChem.Complexity.GroundStateSearch.orthonormal_addresses) · [sum_amplitudes_le](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.GroundStateSearch/FQChem.Complexity.GroundStateSearch.sum_amplitudes_le)
- **Special case**. BBBV hybrid argument for reflection queries to subspaces satisfying the address bound. Summed distance from the reference after T queries is at most 2T√N. [hybrid_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.GroundStateSearch/FQChem.Complexity.GroundStateSearch.hybrid_bound) · [hybrid_step](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.GroundStateSearch/FQChem.Complexity.GroundStateSearch.hybrid_step)
- **Derived result**. Converts the hybrid estimate into a success-probability or complementary-projector energy bound for uniform performance across marked subspaces. For orthonormal candidates, energy error ε implies N√(1−ε) ≤ (2T+1)√N. [energy_query_lower_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.GroundStateSearch/FQChem.Complexity.GroundStateSearch.energy_query_lower_bound) · [orthonormal_energy_lower_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.GroundStateSearch/FQChem.Complexity.GroundStateSearch.orthonormal_energy_lower_bound) · [query_lower_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.GroundStateSearch/FQChem.Complexity.GroundStateSearch.query_lower_bound)

## Berry et al. (2006) · [Efficient Quantum Algorithms for Simulating Sparse Hamiltonians](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00220-006-0150-x)

- **Derived result**. For individual Boolean potential-term queries, uniform additive error < 1/2 on the parity-encoding population at time N forces N ≤ 2T. Berry et al., Theorem 3, uses sparse matrix row queries and trace distance for a different Hamiltonian family. [simulation_query_lower_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrbitalSimulation/FQChem.Complexity.OrbitalSimulation.simulation_query_lower_bound)
- **Supporting lemma**. The Hermiticity and row sparsity hypotheses in Berry et al., Theorem 3, hold for the weighted paths joined by bit-controlled crossings. Each row has at most two nonzero entries, including at the endpoints. [hermitian](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityPath/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityPath.hermitian) · [twoSparse](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityPath/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityPath.twoSparse)
- **Supporting lemma**. If two input strings agree on the bits incident to a given level, their entire Hamiltonian rows there coincide. Thus the crossing data for that row depends on at most two bit values. [row_from_adjacent_bits](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityPath/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityPath.row_from_adjacent_bits)
- **Supporting lemma**. Theorem 3’s two-track graph, expressed in coordinates labeled by accumulated input parity, has two identical copies of the bare spin chain. The Lean identities identify every matrix entry of this relabeling. [crossed_eq_lift](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityPath/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityPath.crossed_eq_lift) · [lift_entry](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityPath/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityPath.lift_entry)
- **Special case**. The parity-path step in the proof of Berry et al., Theorem 3. At every physical time the crossed propagator is exactly the lift of the uncrossed propagator. From the initial zero-track endpoint the far-end amplitude vanishes on the wrong parity track and equals the bare path amplitude on the correct one. [endpoint_amplitude](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityPath/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityPath.endpoint_amplitude) · [evolution_lifts](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityPath/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityPath.evolution_lifts)
- **Supporting lemma**. Binomial recursion gives the two weighted nearest-neighbor terms in the action of the bare tridiagonal matrix. The formulas also cover the two end levels. [couplings](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SpinPathEvolution/FQChem.Complexity.SpinPathEvolution.couplings) · [row_action](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SpinPathEvolution/FQChem.Complexity.SpinPathEvolution.row_action)
- **Supporting lemma**. The positive vector with entries √(N choose j) has eigenvalue N/2 on the spin path. This supplies the norm-attaining vector for the crossed Hamiltonians. [binomial_eigenvector](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SpinPathEvolution/FQChem.Complexity.SpinPathEvolution.binomial_eigenvector)
- **Supporting lemma**. The binomial spin amplitudes start at the zero endpoint and satisfy the constant-coefficient Schrödinger equation for the actual path matrix. [state_deriv](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SpinPathEvolution/FQChem.Complexity.SpinPathEvolution.state_deriv) · [state_zero](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SpinPathEvolution/FQChem.Complexity.SpinPathEvolution.state_zero)
- **Supporting lemma**. A differentiable finite vector obeying v′ = Av evolves by the matrix exponential. The integrating factor exp((t−z)A) proves this auxiliary uniqueness statement. [exp_column](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SpinPathEvolution/FQChem.Complexity.SpinPathEvolution.exp_column)
- **Derived result**. Explicit all-time solution underlying Berry et al., Theorem 3. The level-j amplitude is √(N choose j) cos(t/2)^(N−j)(−i sin(t/2))^j. The far-end matrix entry is (−i sin(t/2))^N. [evolution_column](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SpinPathEvolution/FQChem.Complexity.SpinPathEvolution.evolution_column) · [transfer_amplitude](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SpinPathEvolution/FQChem.Complexity.SpinPathEvolution.transfer_amplitude)
- **Paper result**. At physical time π the spin path transfers the zero endpoint to the opposite endpoint with unit amplitude magnitude, as used in the proof of Berry et al., Theorem 3. The exact ket phase is (−i)^N. [perfect_transfer](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SpinPathEvolution/FQChem.Complexity.SpinPathEvolution.perfect_transfer)
- **Supporting lemma**. The Fourier character of a reversible two-bit XOR row answer factors into the two phase queries for the crossing bits; missing end edges and inactive answer-mask positions contribute phase one. [row_character](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries.row_character) · [xor_character](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries.xor_character)
- **Supporting lemma**. Given the row vertex and the two incident edge bits, the decoder reconstructs every complex entry of that row, including endpoints. [response_decodes](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries.response_decodes)
- **Supporting lemma**. The normalized two-bit Walsh transform is an involutive unitary. With coherent row addressing, conjugating the phase query by this fixed transform gives the computational-basis permutation that XORs both incident bits into the answer. [basis_unitary](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries.basis_unitary) · [coherent_lookup](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries.coherent_lookup) · [walsh_adjoint](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries.walsh_adjoint) · [walsh_lookup](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries.walsh_lookup) · [walsh_square](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries.walsh_square)
- **Supporting lemma**. Input-independent unitary composition and closure of the row-query circuit under a final fixed gate preserve the query budget. The construction also handles the initial state. [apply_gate](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries.apply_gate) · [unitary_product](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries.unitary_product)
- **Supporting lemma**. A conventional reversible XOR lookup compiles into one phase-basis row call. Each such call compiles into two input-bit phase queries. The amplitudes agree for all inputs and arbitrary fixed interleaving unitaries. [compile](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries.compile) · [xor_step](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries.xor_step)
- **Supporting lemma**. The sign of the final crossed-path prefix parity equals the product of the N input-bit signs in the polynomial query lower bound. [parity_eq](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries.parity_eq) · [sign_prefixParity](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries.sign_prefixParity)
- **Paper result**. In the Berry parity construction, recovering the final track with bounded error needs at least N/4 full row queries. The endpoint version reads the Born probability of the physical evolution at π with additive error at most 1/4. [endpoint_query_lower_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries.endpoint_query_lower_bound) · [parity_query_lower_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries.parity_query_lower_bound)
- **Supporting lemma**. The two tracks carry a strictly positive binomial eigenvector with eigenvalue N/2. The crossed entries are real and nonnegative, giving exact weighted row sums. [eigen](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityNorm/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityNorm.eigen) · [positive_entries](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityNorm/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityNorm.positive_entries) · [row_mass](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityNorm/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityNorm.row_mass) · [w_pos](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityNorm/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityNorm.w_pos)
- **Supporting lemma**. At a coordinate maximizing |uᵢ|/wᵢ, the weighted row-sum identity bounds the absolute value of any eigenvalue of the crossed Hamiltonian by N/2. [eigenvalue_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityNorm/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityNorm.eigenvalue_bound)
- **Paper result**. For every input string the physical Hilbert-space operator norm of the Hermitian two-track matrix is exactly N/2. At time π its scaled simulation time is πN/2. [spectral_norm](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityNorm/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityNorm.spectral_norm)
- **Supporting lemma**. For a traceless finite Hermitian matrix, the absolute real part of any diagonal entry is at most half the sum of absolute eigenvalues. This bounds a basis-outcome probability error by the trace distance. [diagonal_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseNoFastForwarding/FQChem.Complexity.SparseNoFastForwarding.diagonal_bound)
- **Supporting lemma**. Tracing out finite simulator workspace gives a Hermitian output with trace equal to the squared norm of the global state. A diagonal entry sums the workspace probabilities for that output. [difference_hermitian](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseNoFastForwarding/FQChem.Complexity.SparseNoFastForwarding.difference_hermitian) · [readout](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseNoFastForwarding/FQChem.Complexity.SparseNoFastForwarding.readout) · [reduced_hermitian](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseNoFastForwarding/FQChem.Complexity.SparseNoFastForwarding.reduced_hermitian) · [trace_reduced](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseNoFastForwarding/FQChem.Complexity.SparseNoFastForwarding.trace_reduced)
- **Paper result**. Physical evolution by the crossed Hamiltonian for time π places the initial endpoint exactly on the far-end parity track, with global phase (−i)^N. Its density matrix is the projector onto this endpoint. [evolved_density](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseNoFastForwarding/FQChem.Complexity.SparseNoFastForwarding.evolved_density) · [evolved_state](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseNoFastForwarding/FQChem.Complexity.SparseNoFastForwarding.evolved_state)
- **Paper result**. Berry et al., Theorem 3. For every positive N the crossed row-computable Hamiltonians are 2-sparse with scaled time ‖H‖₂π = πN/2. A finite coherent whole-row query algorithm achieving trace distance ≤1/4 on all inputs after discarding any finite workspace uses at least N/4 queries. The reversible row answer contains both incident bits. Its address determines the neighboring levels and edge weights. [no_fast_forwarding](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseNoFastForwarding/FQChem.Complexity.SparseNoFastForwarding.no_fast_forwarding)

## Berry et al. (2015) · [Simulating Hamiltonian Dynamics with a Truncated Taylor Series](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevLett.114.090502)

- **Supporting lemma**. Finite matrix identities for the controlled SELECT operation and its conjugation by a unitary ancilla preparation. They certify unitarity and one joint matrix element. [circuit_unitary](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.LinearCombinationOfUnitaries/FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.LinearCombinationOfUnitaries.circuit_unitary) · [select_prepare](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.LinearCombinationOfUnitaries/FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.LinearCombinationOfUnitaries.select_prepare) · [select_unitary](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.LinearCombinationOfUnitaries/FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.LinearCombinationOfUnitaries.select_unitary)
- **Paper result**. In Eqs. (7)–(11), the reference ancilla block of PREPARE† SELECT PREPARE equals the sum of term unitaries weighted by prepared probabilities. Probabilities cₖ/α give H/α, as in the paper’s LCU construction. [blockEncodes](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.LinearCombinationOfUnitaries/FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.LinearCombinationOfUnitaries.blockEncodes) · [referenceBlock](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.LinearCombinationOfUnitaries/FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.LinearCombinationOfUnitaries.referenceBlock)
- **Derived result**. The squared weight in the reference ancilla sector is ‖Hψ‖²/α². This is a success probability for a unit system input and is derived from the circuit block. [postselectionWeight](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.LinearCombinationOfUnitaries/FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.LinearCombinationOfUnitaries.postselectionWeight)
- **Supporting lemma**. The first k exponential Taylor terms have norm error at most eˢsᵏ/k! at s = ‖x‖, and converge in a complete normed algebra. These estimates support the segment analysis. [segment_error](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.TruncatedTaylor/FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.TruncatedTaylor.segment_error) · [segment_tendsto](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.TruncatedTaylor/FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.TruncatedTaylor.segment_tendsto)
- **Supporting lemma**. The proof controls the perturbation of repeated powers near a contraction and identifies the norm of the scaled generator −ihH. [generator_norm](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.TruncatedTaylor/FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.TruncatedTaylor.generator_norm) · [power_error](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.TruncatedTaylor/FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.TruncatedTaylor.power_error)
- **Special case**. For a bounded Hamiltonian the one-segment truncation has a factorial remainder. For self-adjoint H and r positive equal segments the operator error is bounded by (1+δ)ʳ−1, with a corresponding bound on input vectors. Circuit synthesis and query cost require additional results. [realTime_segment](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.TruncatedTaylor/FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.TruncatedTaylor.realTime_segment) · [realTime_segmented](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.TruncatedTaylor/FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.TruncatedTaylor.realTime_segmented) · [realTime_state_error](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.TruncatedTaylor/FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.TruncatedTaylor.realTime_state_error)

## Booth, Thom & Alavi (2009) · [Fermion Monte Carlo without fixed nodes: A game of life, death, and annihilation in Slater determinant space](https://doi.org/10.1063/1.3193710)

- **Supporting lemma**. Randomized integer rounding has nonnegative normalized masses and the intended first moment. The two independent roundings implement spawning and diagonal death or cloning. [roundedCounts](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FCIQMC/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FCIQMC.roundedCounts) · [roundingFirstMoment](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FCIQMC/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FCIQMC.roundingFirstMoment) · [roundingMass_nonneg](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FCIQMC/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FCIQMC.roundingMass_nonneg) · [roundingMass_sum](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FCIQMC/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FCIQMC.roundingMass_sum)
- **Supporting lemma**. A normalized proposal and normalized rounding laws define finite one-walker and independent-population outcome laws. Summing over other walkers recovers the selected marginal. [outcomeMass_nonneg](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FCIQMC/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FCIQMC.outcomeMass_nonneg) · [outcomeMass_sum](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FCIQMC/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FCIQMC.outcomeMass_sum) · [populationMass_probability](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FCIQMC/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FCIQMC.populationMass_probability) · [productMarginal](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FCIQMC/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FCIQMC.productMarginal)
- **Special case**. Local cancellation leaves at most one sign at each determinant and preserves the net signed walker count exactly. [annihilationPreserves](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FCIQMC/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FCIQMC.annihilationPreserves) · [survivorsSignedCount](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FCIQMC/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FCIQMC.survivorsSignedCount)
- **Special case**. For a supported spawning proposal and a fixed shift, the expected one-step signed counts give the Euler imaginary-time CI update, for one walker and for a finite population. This is a first-moment identity. [populationFirstMoment](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FCIQMC/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FCIQMC.populationFirstMoment) · [walkerFirstMoment](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FCIQMC/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FCIQMC.walkerFirstMoment)

## Born & Oppenheimer (1927) · [Zur Quantentheorie der Molekeln](https://doi.org/10.1002/andp.19273892002)

- **Derived result**. For one nuclear coordinate on a differentiable potential surface, Newton’s law implies zero instantaneous derivative of kinetic plus potential energy. Existence and accuracy of a molecular adiabatic approximation are additional questions. [bornOppenheimerEnergy](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.RealTimePropagation.BornOppenheimer/FQChem.Dynamics.bornOppenheimerEnergy)
- **Derived result**. On a one-coordinate Newton trajectory driven by the eigenbranch force and both MM forces, kinetic plus layered electronic potential energy has zero derivative. [layeredBornOppenheimerEnergy](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Multiscale.StitchingSolutions.LayeredQM/FQChem.Multiscale.layeredBornOppenheimerEnergy)

## Born (1926) · [Zur Quantenmechanik der Stoßvorgänge](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01397477)

- **Supporting lemma**. Qatlib sums squared amplitudes over a finite basis event. For a normalized state the probability is ≤1, and arbitrary complex rescaling multiplies it by the scalar’s squared norm. [measureProb_le_one](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Foundations.BornMetrics.EventProbability/Qatlib.Foundations.BornMetrics.EventProbability.measureProb_le_one) · [measureProb_smul](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Foundations.BornMetrics.EventProbability/Qatlib.Foundations.BornMetrics.EventProbability.measureProb_smul)
- **Supporting lemma**. Qatlib’s finite-state primitives expand the complex inner product, normalize each basis ket and give nonnegative event weights from squared amplitudes. [inner_apply](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.Qubit/Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.inner_apply) · [measureProb_nonneg](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.Qubit/Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.measureProb_nonneg) · [normalized_ket](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.Qubit/Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.normalized_ket)
- **Derived result**. Squared branch norms ‖Kᵢψ‖² are nonnegative and sum to one for a normalized input under Kraus completeness. This extends the probability rule to a finite generalized measurement. [bornCompleteness](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.MeasurementTheory/FQChem.Properties.MeasurementTheory.bornCompleteness)

## Boys (1950) · [Electronic wave functions - I. A general method of calculation for the stationary states of any molecular system](https://doi.org/10.1098/rspa.1950.0036)

- **Derived result**. A bounded Hamiltonian and nested spans of determinants give nonincreasing Ritz energy whenever the smaller span contains a nonzero trial determinant. [selectedCI](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.SubspaceDiagonalization.ConfigurationInteraction/FQChem.VariationalMethods.selectedCI)

## Brassard et al. (2002) · [Quantum amplitude amplification and estimation](https://doi.org/10.1090/conm/305/05215)

- **Special case**. Theorem 12, k=1, M≥2. The counter Born distribution for a unitary preparation has the BHMT amplitude-error guarantee with success at least 8/π². The expanded circuit counts M−1 controlled marking calls plus initialization. The paper counts M evaluations of its function. [amplitudeEstimation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.AmplitudeEstimation/FQChemQatlib.Properties.AmplitudeEstimation.amplitudeEstimation)
- **Special case**. Theorem 12 for k=1 and M≥2. The Born law meets the BHMT error window with probability at least 8/π². Probability-one endpoint outcomes hold for a=0 and for a=1 with even M. [prepared_ae_bhmt_error_and_endpoints](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.AmplitudeCore.PreparedReadout.PreparedAmplitudeEstimation/Qatlib.Concepts.AmplitudeCore.PreparedReadout.prepared_ae_bhmt_error_and_endpoints)
- **Supporting lemma**. Counts the expanded implementation’s M−1 marking calls, gates, preparation accesses and source reflections in its explicit circuit vocabulary. The preparation budget is separate from the marking-query convention. [prepared_ae_access_ledger](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.AmplitudeCore.PreparedReadout.PreparedAmplitudeEstimation/Qatlib.Concepts.AmplitudeCore.PreparedReadout.prepared_ae_access_ledger)
- **Special case**. Theorem 12, M≥2 and all integer windows k≥2. The actual prepared circuit satisfies the k-dependent amplitude error bound with success strictly above 1−1/[2(k−1)]. [amplitudeEstimationTail](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.AmplitudeEstimation/FQChemQatlib.Properties.AmplitudeEstimation.amplitudeEstimationTail)
- **Special case**. The prepared Born distribution satisfies the strict k≥2 amplitude tail of Theorem 12 for M≥2. The proof handles zero and unit amplitude and otherwise uses the two-eigenphase mixture. [preparedAeWindow_strict](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.AEMeanReadout.LocalAEBackend.ComparativeStrictBounds/Qatlib.Concepts.AEMeanReadout.LocalAEBackend.preparedAeWindow_strict)
- **Derived result**. Unknown-success amplitude amplification of this signed solver succeeds with probability at least 1/8 averaged over the chosen depths. Its conditional solution is preserved up to global phase, and the solver and inverse calls are counted. [amplifiedSignedSolution](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineAmplification/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.amplifiedSignedSolution)
- **Derived result**. For 4d ≤ |λ| ≤ 1/4, the signed solver attains vector error ε and constant success with at most 2/d input-preparation or inverse calls and 640/(d²ε) exact controlled evolutions. Elementary sparse runtime requires additional synthesis. [signedOracleResources](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineQueries/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.signedOracleResources)
- **Supporting lemma**. A randomized window of Grover depths bounds the mean success probability and preserves the selected output up to global phase. [amplified_postMeasure_phase](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.Postselection.RationalHHLNoiseConsumers/Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.Postselection.HHLUnknown.Probe.amplified_postMeasure_phase) · [mixed_window_avg](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.Postselection.RationalHHLNoiseConsumers/Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.Postselection.HHLUnknown.Probe.mixed_window_avg)
- **Supporting lemma**. The amplification word separately counts its preparation and adjoint uses. [prepared_aa_separate_access_word](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.LchsSimulation.StaticLchs.PreparedAmplitudeAmplification/Qatlib.Concepts.LchsSimulation.StaticLchs.prepared_aa_separate_access_word)
- **Supporting lemma**. In the plane of unit good and bad components, the projected amplitude is sin θ, the state has unit norm and the inner product is the cosine of the angle difference. [inner_states](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.AmplitudeAmplification/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.AmplitudeAmplification.inner_states) · [project_state](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.AmplitudeAmplification/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.AmplitudeAmplification.project_state) · [state_normalized](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.AmplitudeAmplification/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.AmplitudeAmplification.state_normalized)
- **Supporting lemma**. The good-subspace sign reflection negates the angle in the two-component representation. [mark_state](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.AmplitudeAmplification/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.AmplitudeAmplification.mark_state)
- **Paper result**. The two reflections rotate by 2θ. Starting at angle θ, k ideal iterations give angle (2k+1)θ and good-outcome probability sin²((2k+1)θ), as in the amplitude-amplification analysis. [iterate_state](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.AmplitudeAmplification/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.AmplitudeAmplification.iterate_state) · [step_rotates](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.AmplitudeAmplification/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.AmplitudeAmplification.step_rotates) · [success_law](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.AmplitudeAmplification/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.AmplitudeAmplification.success_law)
- **Special case**. For known a=sin²θ with 0<θ≤π/2, choosing the integer iteration count gives success at least 1−a using at most π/(4√a) marking reflections. State preparation and its inverse are separate operations. [inverse_sqrt_queries](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.AmplitudeAmplification/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.AmplitudeAmplification.inverse_sqrt_queries) · [rounded_success](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.AmplitudeAmplification/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.AmplitudeAmplification.rounded_success)

## Cai et al. (2020) · [Quantum computation of molecular response properties](https://arxiv.org/abs/2001.03406)

- **Derived result**. Positive damping gives a unique finite-basis response vector at every real frequency, including resonance. The residual norm divided by the damping bounds the error in the vector. [dampingIdentity](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.LinearSystems.MolecularResponse/FQChem.Properties.MolecularResponse.dampingIdentity) · [residualError](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.LinearSystems.MolecularResponse/FQChem.Properties.MolecularResponse.residualError) · [uniqueResponse](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.LinearSystems.MolecularResponse/FQChem.Properties.MolecularResponse.uniqueResponse)
- **Paper result**. The doubled system in Eq. (12) is Hermitian. A vector supported on the second block solves it exactly when its nonzero component solves the original response equation. [hermitianResponseSystem](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.LinearSystems.MolecularResponse/FQChem.Properties.MolecularResponse.hermitianResponseSystem)
- **Special case**. Reciprocal eigenvalue weighting solves the damped response equation in the supplied energy eigenbasis. [spectralResponse](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.LinearSystems.MolecularResponse/FQChem.Properties.MolecularResponse.spectralResponse)
- **Paper result**. Summing the two frequency branches gives the excited-state sum for dipole polarizability in Eq. (3). The permanent ground-state dipole cancels. [polarizability](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.LinearSystems.MolecularResponse/FQChem.Properties.MolecularResponse.polarizability)

## Cao et al. (2013) · [Quantum algorithm and circuit design solving the Poisson equation](https://doi.org/10.1088/1367-2630/15/1/013021)

- **Special case**. For a 1D uniform grid with homogeneous Dirichlet boundaries and mode index 1 through N, the sine mode is nonzero and diagonalizes the second-difference stencil with its explicit eigenvalue. [dirichletPoissonMode](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.LinearSystems.DirichletPoisson/FQChem.Algorithms.dirichletPoissonMode)

## Carleo et al. (2012) · [Localization and Glassy Dynamics Of Many-Body Quantum Systems](https://doi.org/10.1038/srep00243)

- **Derived result**. A finite weighted sum of outer products of centered real log-derivative samples has nonnegative quadratic form when the weights are nonnegative. This supplies a sampled variational metric property. [sampledTangentMetric](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.RealTimePropagation.VariationalEvolution/FQChem.Dynamics.sampledTangentMetric)

## Chmiela et al. (2017) · [Machine learning of accurate energy-conserving molecular force fields](https://doi.org/10.1126/sciadv.1603015)

- **Derived result**. Differentiating energy invariance under a linear isometry gives the directional force transformation at a differentiable configuration. The cited work develops a gradient-domain force-field model. [invariantDirectionalForce](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.LearnedSurrogates/FQChem.Dynamics.invariantDirectionalForce)

## Choi (1975) · [Completely positive linear maps on complex matrices](https://doi.org/10.1016/0024-3795%2875%2990075-0)

- **Supporting lemma**. The matrix amplification acting on system blocks equals conjugation and summation with the ancilla-extended Kraus matrices. [lifted_operation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.OpenSystems.Kraus/FQChem.Dynamics.OpenSystems.Kraus.lifted_operation)
- **Special case**. The finite Kraus representation gives a complex linear map preserving positivity at every finite ancilla size. This is the representation-to-complete-positivity direction of Choi’s characterization. [completePositivity](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.OpenSystems.Kraus/FQChem.Dynamics.OpenSystems.Kraus.completePositivity) · [complexLinearity](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.OpenSystems.Kraus/FQChem.Dynamics.OpenSystems.Kraus.complexLinearity)
- **Derived result**. Kraus completeness preserves trace with an arbitrary finite ancilla. Combined with complete positivity it preserves normalized positive joint states. [jointDensityPreservation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.OpenSystems.Kraus/FQChem.Dynamics.OpenSystems.Kraus.jointDensityPreservation) · [tracePreservation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.OpenSystems.Kraus/FQChem.Dynamics.OpenSystems.Kraus.tracePreservation)

## Condon (1930) · [The Theory of Complex Spectra](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRev.36.1121)

- **Supporting lemma**. A coordinate move has support on its source mode. Multilinearity expands a pair of occupied-slot replacements, and permuting the two moves preserves their joint two-body operator. [absent_move](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction.absent_move) · [ket_update_pair](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction.ket_update_pair) · [rankOneBasis](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction.rankOneBasis) · [twoSymm](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction.twoSymm)
- **Derived result**. Condon, §4. Acting on an ordered occupation determinant replaces one occupied source or two distinct occupied sources. Attempting to annihilate an absent source or a repeated source gives zero. Exterior antisymmetry supplies the signs upon reordering. [oneAbsent](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction.oneAbsent) · [oneAction](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction.oneAction) · [twoAbsent](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction.twoAbsent) · [twoAction](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction.twoAction) · [twoRepeated](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction.twoRepeated)

## Craig & Manolopoulos (2004) · [Quantum statistics and classical mechanics: Real time correlation functions from ring polymer molecular dynamics](https://doi.org/10.1063/1.1777575)

- **Derived result**. For a finite permutation of beads, the sum of harmonic nearest-neighbor spring forces vanishes. This is internal-force cancellation in the ring-polymer model. [ringPolymerSpringForce](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.RealTimePropagation.RingPolymer/FQChem.Dynamics.ringPolymerSpringForce)

- **Special case**. The lower bound in Hangleiter et al., Lemma 2, is proved for normalized pure states and extended to a possibly degenerate ground sector.
