## Gilyén et al. (2019) · [Quantum singular value transformation and beyond: exponential improvements for quantum matrix arithmetics](https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.01838)

- **Paper result**. Theorem 17, Eq. (32). For a finite-dimensional projected unitary encoding and a phase list realizing P, compressing the alternating word gives the odd or even singular-value transform of the encoded matrix. Orthonormal frames represent the two projector ranges. The result includes the empty phase word. [singularValueTransformationOfPhases](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.SingularValueTransformation/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.SingularValueTransformation.singularValueTransformationOfPhases)
- **Paper result**. Corollary 8 and Theorem 17, Eq. (32). For a complex polynomial of positive degree d satisfying the paper’s parity and magnitude conditions, d real phases realize P exactly and its alternating unitary word implements the singular-value transform on the specified input and output spaces. [polynomialSingularValueTransformation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.SingularValueTransformation/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.SingularValueTransformation.polynomialSingularValueTransformation)
- **Paper result**. The two cases of Theorem 17, Eq. (32). The compressed alternating unitary word transforms the singular values by the response polynomial of a prescribed odd or even phase list. The even word uses the adjoint oracle and both outer projections onto the input space. [source_corner_even_eq](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QsvtCore.QsVtMain.ConventionBridge/Qatlib.Concepts.QsvtCore.QsVtMain.source_corner_even_eq) · [source_corner_odd_eq](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QsvtCore.QsVtMain.ConventionBridge/Qatlib.Concepts.QsvtCore.QsVtMain.source_corner_odd_eq)
- **Paper result**. Corollary 8, Eq. (14). The response polynomial of a length-d list of real phases equals a prescribed positive-degree complex polynomial under the parity and magnitude hypotheses. Qatlib also proves minimal length and parity for any realizing list. [corollary8ComplexPhaseExistence](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QsvtCore.PhaseExistence.Corollary8ComplexPhaseExistenceGily/Qatlib.Concepts.QsvtCore.PhaseExistence.corollary8ComplexPhaseExistence)

## Hangleiter et al. (2017) · [Direct certification of a class of quantum simulations](https://doi.org/10.1088/2058-9565/2/1/015004)

- **Supporting lemma**. In a finite orthonormal eigenbasis, the Hermitian expectation is the sum of energies weighted by squared overlaps. [spectralExpectation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.GapCertificate/FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.spectralExpectation)
- **Special case**. The lower bound in Hangleiter et al., Lemma 2, is proved for normalized pure states and extended to a possibly degenerate ground sector. A gap Δ and energy error ε certify ground-sector weight ≥1−ε/Δ. [gapCertificate](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.GapCertificate/FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.gapCertificate)

## Harrow, Hassidim & Lloyd (2009) · [Quantum Algorithm for Linear Systems of Equations](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevLett.103.150502)

- **Paper result**. The doubled system in Eq. (12) is Hermitian. A vector supported on the second block solves it exactly when its nonzero component solves the original response equation. [hermitianResponseSystem](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.LinearSystems.MolecularResponse/FQChem.Properties.MolecularResponse.hermitianResponseSystem)
- **Special case**. Reciprocal eigenvalue weighting solves the damped response equation in the supplied energy eigenbasis. [spectralResponse](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.LinearSystems.MolecularResponse/FQChem.Properties.MolecularResponse.spectralResponse)
- **Special case**. Given finitely many eigenvectors with nonzero eigenvalues, multiplying their reciprocally weighted superposition by A recovers the input superposition. HHL’s approximation, success probability and complexity need further proofs. [hhlSpectralInversion](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.LinearSystems.HHL/FQChem.Algorithms.hhlSpectralInversion)
- **Supporting lemma**. The implemented success coordinate agrees with the signed reciprocal on regular bins. The decoded phase distance bounds the relative error even across a sign change. [decodedDistance](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.decodedDistance) · [regularGain](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.regularGain) · [relativeGain](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.relativeGain) · [selectedGain](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.selectedGain)
- **Derived result**. The exact sine clock on either spectral sign has squared relative error of order 1/(dM)² for any superposition. For a normalized input the selected branch has mass at least 4d² and normalized vector error at most 160/(dM), preserved by unitary uncomputation. [normalizedBranch](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.normalizedBranch) · [signedRelativeClock](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.signedRelativeClock) · [superpositionError](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.superpositionError)
- **Supporting lemma**. Perturbation bound for normalized successful branches. [normalize_error_of_gap](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.BlockEncoding.Postselection/Qatlib.Concepts.BlockEncoding.Postselection.normalize_error_of_gap)
- **Supporting lemma**. Legality of the canonical signed filter and its action on an initialized flag qudit. [canonicalSignedFilter_consistent](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.SinEnvProgram.QlsHhl/Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.SinEnvProgram.canonicalSignedFilter_consistent) · [canonicalSignedProgram_rotation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.SinEnvProgram.QlsHhl/Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.SinEnvProgram.canonicalSignedProgram_rotation)
- **Supporting lemma**. The sine-clock distribution is normalized and satisfies a circular second-moment bound independent of the eigenphase. [sin_envelope_kernel_normalized](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.SinEnvFilter.SinEnvelopeHHL/Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.SinEnvFilter.SinEnvelopeHHL.sin_envelope_kernel_normalized) · [sin_envelope_kernel_second_moment](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.SinEnvFilter.SinEnvelopeHHL/Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.SinEnvFilter.SinEnvelopeHHL.sin_envelope_kernel_second_moment)
- **Derived result**. The inverse Fourier transform, controlled-time phases and sine-state preparation give the exact eigenphase kernel. Their adjoint uncomputes the reciprocal-weighted state to a zero clock. The signed solution branch has probability at least 4d² and normalized error at most 160/(dM). [cleanClock](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineUncompute/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.cleanClock) · [clockUnitary](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineUncompute/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.clockUnitary) · [forwardKernel](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineUncompute/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.forwardKernel) · [signedSolution](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineUncompute/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.signedSolution)
- **Supporting lemma**. The initialized sine-envelope clock has unit norm and is prepared by a unitary. [sin_envelope_state_normalized_and_prep](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.SinEnvFilter.SinEnvelopeHHL/Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.SinEnvFilter.SinEnvelopeHHL.sin_envelope_state_normalized_and_prep)
- **Supporting lemma**. The controlled-time phases and quantum Fourier transform are unitary. [norm_exp_two_pi_I](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.SpectralFilter.PhaseReadout.PhaseEstimation/Qatlib.Concepts.SpectralFilter.PhaseReadout.norm_exp_two_pi_I) · [qftMatrix_unitary](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.SpectralFilter.PhaseReadout.PhaseEstimation/Qatlib.Concepts.SpectralFilter.PhaseReadout.qftMatrix_unitary)
- **Supporting lemma**. The direct sum of unitary eigenphase-clock blocks is unitary on the combined register. [blockDiag_unitary](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QueryComplexity.DegreeMethod.LocalCapability/Qatlib.Concepts.QueryComplexity.DegreeMethod.blockDiag_unitary)
- **Supporting lemma**. The unitary composes the preparation, forward clock, signed reciprocal rotation and inverse clock. Its selected amplitudes and Born mass agree with the analyzed solution branch. [blockSlice](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineAmplification/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.blockSlice) · [solverMass](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineAmplification/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.solverMass) · [solverOutput](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineAmplification/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.solverOutput) · [solverUnitary](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineAmplification/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.solverUnitary) · [tensorSlice](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineAmplification/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.tensorSlice)
- **Derived result**. Unknown-success amplitude amplification of this signed solver succeeds with probability at least 1/8 averaged over the chosen depths. Its conditional solution is preserved up to global phase, and the solver and inverse calls are counted. [amplifiedSignedSolution](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineAmplification/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.amplifiedSignedSolution)
- **Supporting lemma**. The fixed forward and adjoint clock words use controlled evolutions and return their work bit on arbitrary input clock states. [clockUndo](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineQueries/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.clockUndo) · [forwardQueries](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineQueries/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.forwardQueries) · [inverseQueries](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineQueries/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.inverseQueries) · [powers](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineQueries/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.powers) · [unitaryOracle](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineQueries/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.unitaryOracle)
- **Derived result**. For 4d ≤ |λ| ≤ 1/4, the signed solver attains vector error ε and constant success with at most 2/d input-preparation or inverse calls and 640/(d²ε) exact controlled evolutions. Elementary sparse runtime requires additional synthesis. [signedOracleResources](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineQueries/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.signedOracleResources)
- **Supporting lemma**. The uniform forward and adjoint words query the evolution in the original Hermitian matrix basis. The eigenvector transformation is used to prove their action on every clock state. [clockAction](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineHermitian/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.clockAction) · [mixClock](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineHermitian/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.mixClock) · [mixPowers](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineHermitian/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.mixPowers) · [oracleConjugation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineHermitian/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.oracleConjugation) · [physicalForward](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineHermitian/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.physicalForward) · [physicalInverse](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineHermitian/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.physicalInverse)
- **Derived result**. For an invertible finite Hermitian matrix of either spectral sign in the stated bands, the clean-clock target is the ordinary inverse applied to the input in its original basis. At clock length M the normalized successful state has vector error at most 160/(dM) and unamplified probability at least 4d². [cleanInverse](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineHermitian/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.cleanInverse) · [inverseInBasis](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineHermitian/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.inverseInBasis) · [physicalSolution](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineHermitian/FQChemQatlib.Properties.SignedSineClock.physicalSolution)
- **Supporting lemma**. Taking an adjoint preserves the evolution query count. [adjointQueryCount_adjointCircuit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.CoherentSim.CoherentOAA.AdjointWord/Qatlib.Concepts.CoherentSim.CoherentOAA.adjointQueryCount_adjointCircuit)
- **Supporting lemma**. A randomized window of Grover depths bounds the mean success probability and preserves the selected output up to global phase. [amplified_postMeasure_phase](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.Postselection.RationalHHLNoiseConsumers/Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.Postselection.HHLUnknown.Probe.amplified_postMeasure_phase) · [mixed_window_avg](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.Postselection.RationalHHLNoiseConsumers/Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.Postselection.HHLUnknown.Probe.mixed_window_avg)
- **Supporting lemma**. Conjugating the diagonal eigenphase evolution gives a unitary input oracle. [continuous_hhl_oracle_unitary](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.SinEnvProgram.NearGridHHLOutput/Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.SinEnvProgram.continuous_hhl_oracle_unitary)
- **Supporting lemma**. The sine-clock word is unitary and makes exactly M−1 controlled-evolution calls. [sin_envelope_forward_word_counts](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.SinEnvProgram.SinEnvelopeHHLConsumers/Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.SinEnvProgram.SinEnvelopeHHLConsumers.sin_envelope_forward_word_counts) · [sin_envelope_phase_circuit_unitary](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.SinEnvProgram.SinEnvelopeHHLConsumers/Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.SinEnvProgram.SinEnvelopeHHLConsumers.sin_envelope_phase_circuit_unitary)
- **Supporting lemma**. Operational execution and matrix evaluation agree for the forward word and its unitary adjoint. [run_eq_evaluate](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.NativeNumerics.LayeredMean.CountedHHLProgram/Qatlib.Concepts.NativeNumerics.LayeredMean.run_eq_evaluate) · [wellFormedAdjointCircuit_stepToAdjoint_reverse](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.NativeNumerics.LayeredMean.CountedHHLProgram/Qatlib.Concepts.NativeNumerics.LayeredMean.wellFormedAdjointCircuit_stepToAdjoint_reverse)
- **Supporting lemma**. The clock threshold loop applies each bin’s controlled power and restores the work bit. [phaseFlagEmbed_phaseGate](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.SpectralReadout.OffGridPhaseReadout/Qatlib.Concepts.SpectralReadout.OffGridPhaseReadout.phaseFlagEmbed_phaseGate) · [phaseThresholdLoop_action](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.SpectralReadout.OffGridPhaseReadout/Qatlib.Concepts.SpectralReadout.OffGridPhaseReadout.phaseThresholdLoop_action)
- **Derived result**. Appendix A, Theorem 1(3), with a uniform-clock phase-estimation variant. For a Hermitian A with all eigenvalue magnitudes in [d,1/4], a single run with clock M=max(2,⌈65536/(d⁶ε⁴)⌉) succeeds with probability at least d²/4. The postselected state is within ε of normalized A⁻¹b in the clean success registers. Its circuit uses 2(M−1) exact spectral-oracle queries and 4(M−1)+6 fixed-matrix gates. [signedInverseState](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.LinearSystems/FQChemQatlib.Properties.LinearSystems.signedInverseState)
- **Derived result**. For signed gapped spectral data the uniform-clock reciprocal filter has Born success at least d²/4 and normalized pseudoinverse-state error at most 2η/d when M ≥ 4096/(d²η⁴) and η ≤ d/2. These are variant-specific precision bounds. [uniform_offgrid_signed_hhl_born_and_solution](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.UniformHHL.OffGridSignedHHL/Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.UniformHHL.uniform_offgrid_signed_hhl_born_and_solution)
- **Supporting lemma**. Counts the forward and inverse phase-estimation parts of the uniform-clock signed HHL variant. For a legal preparation and M≥2 the word is well formed and uses 2(M−1) oracle calls and 4(M−1)+6 fixed-matrix gates. [uniform_hhl_actual_legal_forward_inverse_ledger](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.UniformHHL.UniformHHLFilter/Qatlib.Concepts.HhlAlgorithms.UniformHHL.uniform_hhl_actual_legal_forward_inverse_ledger)
- **Supporting lemma**. Eq. (1) of Subaşı, Somma and Orsucci and the HHL solution-state readout. For a unitary eigenbasis and nonzero real eigenvalues, the spectral Hermitian matrix’s pseudoinverse equals its ordinary inverse. [pseudoinverse_eq_inv_of_ne_zero](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Papers.QLinearSolver18.MainResults.Readout.PseudoinverseReadout/Qatlib.Papers.QLinearSolver18.MainResults.Readout.PseudoinverseReadout.pseudoinverse_eq_inv_of_ne_zero)
- **Proof in the literature · needs Lean proof**. [HHL solution-state accuracy and runtime](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#paper/hhl-linear-system-runtime). §II and supplementary Appendix A.1–A.2 · Theorem 1(3), Eq. (A6). Let A be an N-by-N s-sparse, efficiently row-computable Hermitian matrix with ‖A‖ ≤ 1 and ‖A⁻¹‖ ≤ κ, and let a unitary of cost Tᵦ prepare normalized |b⟩. With constant success HHL prepares a state within vector-norm error ε of A⁻¹|b⟩/‖A⁻¹|b⟩‖ in time Õ(κTᵦ + κ²s² log(N)/ε), suppressing the slowly growing simulation factors as in the paper. The output is a quantum state.
  - **Remaining Lean proof**. The signed, uncomputed solution and constant-success amplification have checked state-preparation and exact controlled-evolution query counts. Full sparse runtime still requires finite-precision simulation of those evolutions and elementary-gate costs for preparing the sine clock and applying the signed reciprocal filter.
  - [Qatlib · amplified success and oracle count for the positive-spectrum sine filter](https://github.com/quantum-atlas-ai/qatlib/blob/f76f71a7c5cd19a31c251d0feeab09c75a758d69/Qatlib/Papers/LinearSystems09/Runtime.lean#L63)

## Higgott, Wang & Brierley (2019) · [Variational Quantum Computation of Excited States](https://doi.org/10.22331/q-2019-07-01-156)

- **Special case**. With an exact common eigenbasis and diagonal penalties that lift every energy to at least the target while leaving the target unchanged, the minimum penalized expectation equals the target energy. Degeneracies are allowed. [quantumDeflation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.ExcitedStates/FQChem.VariationalMethods.quantumDeflation)

## Hirsch (1983) · [Discrete Hubbard-Stratonovich transformation for fermion lattice models](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevB.28.4059)

- **Supporting lemma**. For repulsive step coupling x≥0 a real nonnegative Hirsch parameter solves cosh γ=exp(x/2). [realHirschParameter](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC.realHirschParameter)
- **Paper result**. Hirsch’s discrete spin-field identity for the shifted Hubbard interaction is checked on all four local occupation pairs under cosh γ=exp(x/2). [hirschLocal](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC.hirschLocal)
- **Special case**. Each field weight separates into spin-up and spin-down factors, and independent binary fields decouple the product over finitely many sites. [hirschLattice](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC.hirschLattice) · [spinOrbitalFactorization](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC.spinOrbitalFactorization)
- **Derived result**. A fixed number of split kinetic/interaction steps equals the exact sum of all discrete Hubbard field histories. [hubbardFreeProjection](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC.hubbardFreeProjection)
- **Derived result**. The shifted lattice interaction step is its diagonal exponential. For β,U≥0 the exact sum over binary field histories realizes the m-step Lie product at time β. [hirschHistoryMean_eq_split](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.HubbardProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC.hirschHistoryMean_eq_split) · [interactionStep_eq_exp](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.HubbardProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC.interactionStep_eq_exp)
- **Special case**. At fixed β≥0 and repulsive U, the finite-dimensional, exactly summed field-history propagator tends to exp(−β(K+V)), including after application to a fixed state. The limit refines the time grid. [hirschHistoryMean_mulVec_tendsto](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.HubbardProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC.hirschHistoryMean_mulVec_tendsto) · [hirschHistoryMean_tendsto](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.HubbardProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC.hirschHistoryMean_tendsto)

## Hofacker (2026) · [Rigorous Lower Bounds for Atomic and Ionic Ground-State Energies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34480)

- **Literature hypothesis · needs Lean proof**. [Atomic energy convexity in electron number](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#paper/atomic-energy-convexity). Di Marino et al., Eq. (4) · Hofacker, Appendix C, Eq. (63). At fixed positive integer nuclear charge, twice the ground energy of the N-electron Coulomb atom should be at most the sum for N−1 and N+1 electrons. The variational definition includes both spin states and simultaneous fermionic exchange. The six-centre counterexample concerns a different external potential.
  - [Formal statement in Lean](https://github.com/the-matter-lab/fqchem/blob/6770a38071e5b621be99d9d04edbf8a8e41cbd6c/FQChem/Unproven/AtomicEnergy.lean#L50)

## Hohenberg & Kohn (1964) · [Inhomogeneous Electron Gas](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRev.136.B864)

- **Supporting lemma**. Scalar polynomial and quadratic-form variation identities for the real form-domain pairing, external coupling and symmetric internal energy used in the weak eigen-equation. [h0_expand](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.DensityFunctionals.Continuum/FQChem.DFT.h0_expand) · [integral_expand](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.DensityFunctionals.Continuum/FQChem.DFT.integral_expand) · [ip_expand](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.DensityFunctionals.Continuum/FQChem.DFT.ip_expand) · [lin_coeff_zero](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.DensityFunctionals.Continuum/FQChem.DFT.lin_coeff_zero) · [pf_expand](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.DensityFunctionals.Continuum/FQChem.DFT.pf_expand)
- **Supporting lemma**. A minimizer of the real-form Rayleigh quotient satisfies the weak eigen-equation against every vector of the assumed form domain. [euler_lagrange](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.DensityFunctionals.Continuum/FQChem.DFT.euler_lagrange)
- **Special case**. Two minimizing wavefunctions with matching squared norms and densities share a ground state for the two potentials, by the variational inequalities. [weak_hk](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.DensityFunctionals.Continuum/FQChem.DFT.weak_hk)
- **Special case**. In the real-form setting, equal densities of equal-norm ground states give potentials differing by a constant under test-function separation, rigidity of the one-body lift and almost-everywhere nonvanishing. The common-state lemmas start with a shared ground state and derive constant lift, then constant potential. [commonStateConstantLift](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.DensityFunctionals.Continuum/FQChem.DFT.commonStateConstantLift) · [hohenbergKohn](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.DensityFunctionals.Continuum/FQChem.DFT.hohenbergKohn) · [strong_hk_of_ucp](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.DensityFunctionals.Continuum/FQChem.DFT.strong_hk_of_ucp)
- **Supporting lemma**. For one or more three-dimensional coordinates, almost-everywhere constancy of the sum of copies of a one-body potential implies its almost-everywhere constancy. [oneBodyRigidity](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.DensityFunctionals.MolecularHohenbergKohn/FQChem.MolecularHK.oneBodyRigidity)
- **Special case**. For two finite point-nucleus potentials in the supplied molecular form setting, ground states with equal density and equal squared norm determine the potential difference up to an almost-everywhere constant, assuming separation and the almost-everywhere nonvanishing of one state. [molecularHohenbergKohn](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.DensityFunctionals.MolecularHohenbergKohn/FQChem.MolecularHK.molecularHohenbergKohn)

## Huang & Palsberg (2024) · [Compiling Conditional Quantum Gates without Using Helper Qubits](https://doi.org/10.1145/3656436)

- **Supporting lemma**. Columnwise Hadamard conjugation changes the phase on the all-ones control and target sector into a phase-free XOR of the target. Updating the target leaves its disjoint control predicate unchanged. [conjugate](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.AncillaFreeNot/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.AncillaFreePhase.conjugate) · [hadamardColumn](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.AncillaFreeNot/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.AncillaFreePhase.hadamardColumn) · [readControls](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.AncillaFreeNot/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.AncillaFreePhase.readControls)
- **Derived result**. An exact, clean- and dirty-ancilla-free k-controlled NOT on the given physical register costs at most 1442k+4323 arbitrary one-/two-qubit gates. The word uses a Hadamard on the target on each side of the already compiled phase of angle π. Equality of full matrices eliminates residual global and relative phases. [multiControlledNot](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.AncillaFreeNot/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.AncillaFreePhase.multiControlledNot)
- **Supporting lemma**. A list of phase gates addressed from least to most significant control contributes its binary numerical value times the angle step when the target is on. Composition adds the phases in execution order. [digitValue_full](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.SingletonPhaseGradient/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.SingletonPhaseGradient.digitValue_full) · [phase_add](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.SingletonPhaseGradient/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.SingletonPhaseGradient.phase_add) · [runGradient](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.SingletonPhaseGradient/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.SingletonPhaseGradient.runGradient) · [run_append](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.SingletonPhaseGradient/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.SingletonPhaseGradient.run_append)
- **Derived result**. Exact matrix of the phase-gradient and increment/decrement construction, on all basis columns. A separate target phase supplies the constant increment on ordinary inputs; wrapping the control value activates the desired angle. The modular permutations are still named instructions at this intermediate level. [matrixWord](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.SingletonPhaseGradient/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.SingletonPhaseGradient.matrixWord) · [runWord](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.SingletonPhaseGradient/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.SingletonPhaseGradient.runWord)
- **Supporting lemma**. Each gradient costs one two-qubit phase per control. The full word has 2k+1 one-/two-qubit phases and one forward and one backward modular increment, whose physical cost is proved separately. [gradientCost](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.SingletonPhaseGradient/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.SingletonPhaseGradient.gradientCost) · [wordCost](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.SingletonPhaseGradient/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.SingletonPhaseGradient.wordCost)
- **Derived result**. Gidney Fig. 21 and the phase-gradient method also studied by Huang–Palsberg. For k controls and an arbitrary angle, an exact phase on the marked basis sector needs ≤1442k+4321 one-/two-qubit gates and zero extra qubits. The proved increment uses the target as dirty workspace; arbitrary exact controlled phase rotations are allowed. [grad](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.AncillaFreePhase/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.AncillaFreePhase.grad) · [multiControlledPhase](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.AncillaFreePhase/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.AncillaFreePhase.multiControlledPhase)

## Huang et al. (2022) · [Quantum advantage in learning from experiments](https://doi.org/10.1126/science.abn7293)

- **Supporting lemma**. Supplement D.2.a, Eqs. (D1)–(D3). Each local Bell projector is an amplitude outer product. For every positive trace-one n-qubit density matrix the Born weights of the full product Bell measurement on two copies are nonnegative and sum to one. [projector_gram](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.BellConfidence/FQChem.Properties.BellConfidence.projector_gram) · [weight_nonneg](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.BellConfidence/FQChem.Properties.BellConfidence.weight_nonneg) · [weight_sum](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.BellConfidence/FQChem.Properties.BellConfidence.weight_sum)
- **Paper result**. Supplement D.2.b, Eqs. (D5)–(D6). The shared Bell measurement record gives a sign in {−1,1} with expectation |Tr(Pρ)|² for every subsequently specified Pauli P. [score_mean](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.BellConfidence/FQChem.Properties.BellConfidence.score_mean) · [score_sign](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.BellConfidence/FQChem.Properties.BellConfidence.score_sign)
- **Paper result**. Supplement D.2.b, Lemma 1, Eq. (D8). For N>0 independent two-copy Bell rounds on any density operator and each u>0, the chance of a squared-expectation error at least u is at most 2 exp(−Nu²/2). [meanFailure](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.BellConfidence/FQChem.Properties.BellConfidence.meanFailure)
- **Supporting lemma**. Supplement D.2.b, Eqs. (D9)–(D10). The squared error of the clipped square-root estimator for y≥0 is bounded by the absolute error in estimating y². [clipped_error](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.BellConfidence/FQChem.Properties.BellConfidence.clipped_error)
- **Paper result**. Supplement D.2.b, Corollary 1, Eq. (D11), the quantum upper half of Theorem 6. For every n-qubit state and each Pauli revealed after collection, 2,048 Bell rounds on 4,096 copies give additive magnitude error at most 1/4 with success probability at least 4/5. [predictMagnitude](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.BellConfidence/FQChem.Properties.BellConfidence.predictMagnitude)
- **Supporting lemma**. Supplement D.4, Task 1. Distinct Pauli words are Hilbert–Schmidt orthogonal. For the normalized state (I+aP)/2ⁿ, |a|≤1, the nonidentity query Q has expectation of magnitude |a| if Q=P and zero otherwise. [hiddenMagnitude](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.BellConfidence/FQChem.Properties.BellConfidence.hiddenMagnitude) · [traceDifferent](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.BellConfidence/FQChem.Properties.BellConfidence.traceDifferent)
- **Special case**. Supplement D.4, Task 1, Eq. (D39), quantum upper bound at failure probability 1/5. For every hidden sign and distinct nonidentity pair P,Q, 256 Bell rounds (512 copies) select the larger magnitude in both label orderings using the same record. Theorem 7 concerns the conventional lower bound. [selectHiddenWord](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.BellConfidence/FQChem.Properties.BellConfidence.selectHiddenWord)
- **Supporting lemma**. Appendix D.2.b, Eqs. (D2)–(D4). Each one-qubit Pauli tensor its own copy is diagonal in the Bell basis. The identity reconstructs its matrix entries by summing Bell projectors times their measured signs, including the sign for Y⊗Y. [bellResolution](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.BellSampling/FQChem.Properties.BellSampling.bellResolution)
- **Derived result**. The algebra underlying Appendix D.2.b, Eq. (D6), extends to any complex matrix ρ. Contracting the Bell readouts against ρ⊗ρ gives Tr(ρP)². This extension uses a complex square. [squaredPauliExpectation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.BellSampling/FQChem.Properties.BellSampling.squaredPauliExpectation)
- **Paper result**. Appendix D.2.b, Eq. (D6). For Hermitian ρ and any Pauli word P, the product of local Bell signs on two copies has Born-weighted sum |Tr(ρP)|². For a density operator this is the expectation under the Bell measurement. The measurement is independent of P. [magnitudeSquaredPauliExpectation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.BellSampling/FQChem.Properties.BellSampling.magnitudeSquaredPauliExpectation)
- **Supporting lemma**. The identity word is I. Each Pauli word is Hermitian and squares to I, and each nonidentity Pauli has zero trace. These facts justify the hard density matrices of Supplementary Definition 2. [identityMatrix](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.PauliPrediction/FQChem.Properties.PauliPrediction.identityMatrix) · [pauliHermitian](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.PauliPrediction/FQChem.Properties.PauliPrediction.pauliHermitian) · [pauliSquare](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.PauliPrediction/FQChem.Properties.PauliPrediction.pauliSquare) · [pauliTrace](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.PauliPrediction/FQChem.Properties.PauliPrediction.pauliTrace)
- **Supporting lemma**. The squared Pauli trace correlations of a Hermitian E sum to 2ⁿ Tr(E²). For a positive measurement effect E, its purity is bounded by Tr(E)². [parseval](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.PauliPrediction/FQChem.Properties.PauliPrediction.parseval) · [purityBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.PauliPrediction/FQChem.Properties.PauliPrediction.purityBound)
- **Supporting lemma**. There are (2ⁿ−1)(2ⁿ+1) nonidentity Pauli words on n qubits. [nonidentityCount](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.PauliPrediction/FQChem.Properties.PauliPrediction.nonidentityCount)
- **Derived result**. Eq. (D35) implies a uniform mean square of 1/(2ⁿ+1) for pure measurement effects. This proof by Pauli Parseval gives an upper bound of 1/(2ⁿ+1) for the normalized trace responses of effects of arbitrary rank when n>0. Zero effects have zero responses. [nonidentitySecondMoment](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.PauliPrediction/FQChem.Properties.PauliPrediction.nonidentitySecondMoment)
- **Supporting lemma**. Every normalized trace response of a positive measurement effect to a Pauli word lies in [−1,1]. [responseAbs](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.PauliPrediction/FQChem.Properties.PauliPrediction.responseAbs)
- **Supporting lemma**. Supplementary Definition 2. For nonidentity P and a real bias |a|≤1, ρ=(I+aP)/2ⁿ is a positive trace-one matrix with Tr(ρP)=a. The null, plus and minus hypotheses take a=0, +0.9, −0.9. [hardStatePositive](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.PauliPrediction/FQChem.Properties.PauliPrediction.hardStatePositive) · [hardStateTrace](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.PauliPrediction/FQChem.Properties.PauliPrediction.hardStateTrace) · [hardStateTracePairing](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.PauliPrediction/FQChem.Properties.PauliPrediction.hardStateTracePairing)
- **Supporting lemma**. Each node chooses a finite POVM. Its Born weights sum to one on any trace-one input and the products of the successive Born weights form a normalized law over complete adaptive transcripts. [bornSum](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.AdaptivePauliPrediction/FQChem.Properties.AdaptivePauliPrediction.bornSum) · [leafNormalization](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.AdaptivePauliPrediction/FQChem.Properties.AdaptivePauliPrediction.leafNormalization) · [pathPositive](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.AdaptivePauliPrediction/FQChem.Properties.AdaptivePauliPrediction.pathPositive)
- **Supporting lemma**. Eqs. (D14)–(D20). A hard-state outcome weight is its maximally mixed weight times 1+a·Tr(EP)/Tr(E), with the zero effect handled explicitly. Multiplication along the observed path gives the leaf likelihood. [bornHard](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.AdaptivePauliPrediction/FQChem.Properties.AdaptivePauliPrediction.bornHard) · [massRatio](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.AdaptivePauliPrediction/FQChem.Properties.AdaptivePauliPrediction.massRatio)
- **Supporting lemma**. All null and alternative leaf masses are nonnegative. The null law is independent of the Pauli revealed after acquisition. [alternativeNonneg](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.AdaptivePauliPrediction/FQChem.Properties.AdaptivePauliPrediction.alternativeNonneg) · [nullIndependent](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.AdaptivePauliPrediction/FQChem.Properties.AdaptivePauliPrediction.nullIndependent) · [nullNonnegative](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.AdaptivePauliPrediction/FQChem.Properties.AdaptivePauliPrediction.nullNonnegative)
- **Derived result**. A direct inequality for the fair-sign mixture replaces the logarithmic estimate in Eqs. (D21)–(D34). Its likelihood relative to the null law is at least one minus the sum of squared local biased responses along the leaf. [mixtureLower](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.AdaptivePauliPrediction/FQChem.Properties.AdaptivePauliPrediction.mixtureLower) · [productLoss](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.AdaptivePauliPrediction/FQChem.Properties.AdaptivePauliPrediction.productLoss) · [signMixture](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.AdaptivePauliPrediction/FQChem.Properties.AdaptivePauliPrediction.signMixture)
- **Supporting lemma**. At additive tolerance 1/4 the intervals around 0 and 0.9 are disjoint. For every prediction, the sum of the two hypothesis error masses is bounded below using the sign mixture. [leafError](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.AdaptivePauliPrediction/FQChem.Properties.AdaptivePauliPrediction.leafError)
- **Paper result**. Supplementary Theorem 6, conventional lower bound. Every finite-outcome adaptive single-copy POVM tree with a post-reveal prediction rule and error probability ≤1/5 under Definition 2 must use T≥20(2ⁿ+1)/27 copies, for n≥1. The proof gives its own constant. [copyLowerBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.AdaptivePauliPrediction/FQChem.Properties.AdaptivePauliPrediction.copyLowerBound)
- **Paper result**. The same exponential lower bound holds for randomized adaptive strategies, averaging over any independent probability law on classical seeds. Each seed may select both the entire POVM tree and the post-reveal rule. The averaged failure probability must be at most 1/5. [randomizedCopyLowerBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.AdaptivePauliPrediction/FQChem.Properties.AdaptivePauliPrediction.randomizedCopyLowerBound)
- **Paper result**. Theorem 1 and Supplementary Theorem 6. On the hard prior the two-copy Bell estimator uses 2048 pairs for error at most 1/4 with failure probability ≤1/5, uniformly in n. At the same accuracy, every randomized adaptive single-copy POVM learner needs at least 20(2ⁿ+1)/27 copies. The finite-outcome measurement and post-reveal access models match Supplementary Definitions 1–2. [separation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.QuantumMemorySeparation/FQChem.Properties.QuantumMemorySeparation.separation)

## Huang, Kueng & Preskill (2020) · [Predicting many properties of a quantum system from very few measurements](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41567-020-0932-7)

- **Supporting lemma**. For each local X, Y or Z measurement, adding its two eigenprojectors gives the identity. Weighting them by their signs recovers the measured Pauli operator. [projector_sum](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ClassicalShadows/FQChem.Properties.ClassicalShadows.projector_sum) · [signed_projector_sum](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ClassicalShadows/FQChem.Properties.ClassicalShadows.signed_projector_sum)
- **Supporting lemma**. For a fixed choice of local axes and an arbitrary matrix on the k-qubit support, finite summation factors a product statistic over outcomes into local signed-projector sums inside the Born trace. [weighted_sum](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ClassicalShadows/FQChem.Properties.ClassicalShadows.weighted_sum)
- **Special case**. For a weight-k Pauli string on its support, the single-shot local Pauli shadow equals zero unless all k axes match. Multiplying the matched outcome sign by 3ᵏ gives Born mean Tr(ρP). The trace identity extends linearly to arbitrary complex matrices. [unbiased](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ClassicalShadows/FQChem.Properties.ClassicalShadows.unbiased)
- **Special case**. Supplementary Lemma 3, Eq. (S50). For every trace-one matrix on the k-qubit support, the single-shot Pauli estimator has second moment exactly 3ᵏ. On reduced density matrices this is the state-independent moment underlying the paper’s squared shadow norm. [secondMoment](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ClassicalShadows/FQChem.Properties.ClassicalShadows.secondMoment)
- **Supporting lemma**. The single-shot Pauli readout is real because it is a real scale times a product of measured signs. This relates its real-valued random variable to the Born trace identities. [estimate_real](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowPrediction/FQChem.Properties.ClassicalShadows.estimate_real)
- **Special case**. For N independent uniformly chosen local Pauli shots with the Born marginal on a weight-k support, Chebyshev’s inequality gives probability at most 3ᵏ/(Nε²) that the block mean misses its target by ε or more. The proof uses unbiasedness and the exact second moment in Supplementary Lemma 3. [blockPrediction](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowPrediction/FQChem.Properties.ClassicalShadows.blockPrediction)
- **Supporting lemma**. The median is the middle order statistic. If an odd sample of 2r+1 values has median error at least ε, at least r+1 distinct values also have error at least ε. [median_witness](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowPrediction/FQChem.Properties.ClassicalShadows.median_witness)
- **Supporting lemma**. With mutually independent block estimates, each failing with probability at most 1/8, a union bound over subsets of r+1 bad blocks bounds the median’s failure probability by 1/(4·2ʳ). [median_failure](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowPrediction/FQChem.Properties.ClassicalShadows.median_failure)
- **Special case**. Supplementary Theorem 1 for finite families of Pauli words and uniform local Pauli measurements. Reusing N shots per block across M targets of possibly different supports, Nε²≥8·3ᵏ for every target of weight k gives simultaneous failure probability at most M/(4·2ʳ) with 2r+1 independent blocks. [simultaneousPrediction](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowPrediction/FQChem.Properties.ClassicalShadows.simultaneousPrediction)
- **Special case**. Supplementary Theorem 1 specialized to local Pauli shadows, with a different explicit constant choice. For δ,ε>0 use 2r+1 blocks where r is the ceiling binary logarithm of ⌈M/(4δ)⌉. The Pauli Born marginals, independent draws and Nε²≥8·3ᵏ on each target imply simultaneous failure probability at most δ. [predictionAtConfidence](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowPrediction/FQChem.Properties.ClassicalShadows.predictionAtConfidence)
- **Special case**. For a bijective finite unitary-basis measurement channel in positive finite dimension and any Hermitian A, the squared shadow norm of Eq. (S7) is attained as the largest Born-record second moment over density states. Qatlib proves this through the positive moment effect. [shadowNormSquaredMaximum](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.ClassicalShadows/FQChemQatlib.Properties.ClassicalShadows.shadowNormSquaredMaximum)
- **Special case**. Supplementary Theorem 1 for arbitrary finite families of Hermitian observables and finite, invertible unitary-basis measurement ensembles. Qatlib constructs the Born record law and inverse snapshots. With K independent blocks of L shared shots and Lε² at least four times every centered squared shadow norm, the simultaneous failure probability is at most M·3^⌊K/2⌋/2ᴷ. [simultaneousPrediction](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Properties.ClassicalShadows/FQChemQatlib.Properties.ClassicalShadows.simultaneousPrediction)
- **Special case**. The finite unitary ensemble version of Eq. (S7). In positive dimension, the positive moment effect attains the maximal second moment over density operators for any Hermitian observable. [finite_ensemble_second_moment_spectral_maximum](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.ShadowModels.ShadowChannel.FiniteEnsembleShadows/Qatlib.Concepts.ShadowModels.ShadowChannel.finite_ensemble_second_moment_spectral_maximum)
- **Special case**. Supplementary Theorem 1 for arbitrary Hermitian observables and finite unitary ensembles. The independent Born samples and inverse snapshots are explicit. A per-observable centered squared shadow norm at most Lε²/4 yields simultaneous failure probability at most M·3^⌊K/2⌋/2ᴷ. [finite_ensemble_same_records_many_observables](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.ShadowModels.ShadowChannel.FiniteEnsembleShadows/Qatlib.Concepts.ShadowModels.ShadowChannel.finite_ensemble_same_records_many_observables)
- **Supporting lemma**. A coordinate of the K-by-N product law has the original one-shot marginal; this constructs the independent sampling model in Supplementary Theorem 1. [productDraw](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowConfidence/FQChem.Properties.ShadowConfidence.productDraw)
- **Supporting lemma**. For independent shots with a common mean and variance at most V, Chebyshev bounds the batch failure probability by V/(Nε²). When Nε² ≥ 34V this gives the 1/34 bound used for median amplification. [blockChebyshev](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowConfidence/FQChem.Properties.ShadowConfidence.blockChebyshev) · [blockFailure](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowConfidence/FQChem.Properties.ShadowConfidence.blockFailure)
- **Supporting lemma**. An upper median that misses the target has at least ⌈K/2⌉ bad blocks. For independent block estimates with failure probabilities at most 1/34, the median fails with probability at most 2⁻ᴷ, for odd and even K. [medianFailure](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowConfidence/FQChem.Properties.ShadowConfidence.medianFailure) · [medianWitness](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowConfidence/FQChem.Properties.ShadowConfidence.medianWitness)
- **Supporting lemma**. Rounding K=2 ln(2M/δ) upward gives a positive block count for M≥1 and 0<δ≤1; M·2⁻ᴷ≤δ. [blocks_pos](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowConfidence/FQChem.Properties.ShadowConfidence.blocks_pos) · [confidence](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowConfidence/FQChem.Properties.ShadowConfidence.confidence)
- **Supporting lemma**. Median-of-means concentration in Supplementary Theorem 1 for arbitrary square-integrable real scores, sharing the same records across all targets. The means and variance bounds are parameters of this statistical lemma. [simultaneousConfidence](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowConfidence/FQChem.Properties.ShadowConfidence.simultaneousConfidence)
- **Supporting lemma**. Unitary-basis Born projectors are Hermitian, have trace one, and sum to the identity in each basis. Each unitary entry has norm at most one. [basisEntry](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowMeasurement/FQChem.Properties.ShadowMeasurement.Primitive.basisEntry) · [projectorHermitian](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowMeasurement/FQChem.Properties.ShadowMeasurement.Primitive.projectorHermitian) · [sumProjector](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowMeasurement/FQChem.Properties.ShadowMeasurement.Primitive.sumProjector) · [traceProjector](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowMeasurement/FQChem.Properties.ShadowMeasurement.Primitive.traceProjector)
- **Supporting lemma**. Entrywise measurability of the unitary matrices gives measurable trace scores; uniform entry bounds supply finite second moments for arbitrary probability measures on the bases. [pairingBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowMeasurement/FQChem.Properties.ShadowMeasurement.Primitive.pairingBound) · [pairingMeasurable](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowMeasurement/FQChem.Properties.ShadowMeasurement.Primitive.pairingMeasurable) · [projectorMeasurable](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowMeasurement/FQChem.Properties.ShadowMeasurement.Primitive.projectorMeasurable) · [scoreLp](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowMeasurement/FQChem.Properties.ShadowMeasurement.Primitive.scoreLp) · [scoreMeasurable](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowMeasurement/FQChem.Properties.ShadowMeasurement.Primitive.scoreMeasurable)
- **Supporting lemma**. The outcome-projector channel of Eq. (S2) is unital, symmetric under the trace pairing and adjoint preserving. Its inverse preserves Hermitian observables. [channelOne](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowMeasurement/FQChem.Properties.ShadowMeasurement.Primitive.channelOne) · [channelSymmetric](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowMeasurement/FQChem.Properties.ShadowMeasurement.Primitive.channelSymmetric) · [inverseHermitian](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowMeasurement/FQChem.Properties.ShadowMeasurement.Primitive.inverseHermitian) · [pairingAdjoint](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowMeasurement/FQChem.Properties.ShadowMeasurement.Primitive.pairingAdjoint)
- **Supporting lemma**. For Eq. (S3), applying the inverse channel to the rank-one snapshot or to the observable gives the same trace score. For Hermitian observables the score is real. [hermitianScore](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowMeasurement/FQChem.Properties.ShadowMeasurement.Primitive.hermitianScore) · [inverseSnapshotScore](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowMeasurement/FQChem.Properties.ShadowMeasurement.Primitive.inverseSnapshotScore)
- **Paper result**. Eq. (S5). The Born outcome law and invertibility of the channel imply the mean of the inverse-snapshot estimator is the desired trace expectation. [scoreMean](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowMeasurement/FQChem.Properties.ShadowMeasurement.Primitive.scoreMean)
- **Supporting lemma**. Eq. (S8). Subtracting the normalized trace of the observable shifts every individual score by that same scalar. [scoreCentered](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowMeasurement/FQChem.Properties.ShadowMeasurement.Primitive.scoreCentered)
- **Paper result**. Eq. (S7), before maximizing over states. The squared real score integrates to the ensemble average of the Born-weighted squared matrix element of the inverse observable, including continuous basis ensembles. [bornSecondMoment](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowMeasurement/FQChem.Properties.ShadowMeasurement.Primitive.bornSecondMoment)
- **Supporting lemma**. The one-state second moment is bounded by its supremum over all density states. Bounded scores and the normalization of every Born law give a finite upper bound. [moment_le](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowMeasurement/FQChem.Properties.ShadowMeasurement.Primitive.moment_le)
- **Paper result**. Supplementary Lemma 1, Eq. (S6). The variance of the actual one-shot score is bounded by the squared shadow norm after subtracting the observable’s scalar part. [centeredVariance](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowMeasurement/FQChem.Properties.ShadowMeasurement.Primitive.centeredVariance)
- **Paper result**. Supplementary Theorem 1 with the constants of Eq. (S13), on any probability space realizing the independent shared Born records. The basis ensemble may be continuous. Each block uses at least one shot when every centered norm is zero. [sampleComplexity](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowMeasurement/FQChem.Properties.ShadowMeasurement.Primitive.sampleComplexity)
- **Paper result**. Supplementary Theorem 1, Eqs. (S7), (S12)–(S14). From the NK-fold product of the Born law, the medians predict every Hermitian observable simultaneously with failure at most δ. K=⌈2 ln(2M/δ)⌉ and N=max(1,⌈34 maxᵢ‖Oᵢ−Tr(Oᵢ)I/d‖²shadow/ε²⌉). [iidSampleComplexity](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.ShadowMeasurement/FQChem.Properties.ShadowMeasurement.Primitive.iidSampleComplexity)

## Hubbard (1959) · [Calculation of Partition Functions](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevLett.3.77)

- **Supporting lemma**. The characteristic function of a standard real Gaussian gives the scalar phase-average identity for real v and τ≥0. [gaussianPhaseAverage](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.GaussianAuxiliaryField/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.GaussianAuxiliaryField.gaussianPhaseAverage)
- **Special case**. For a finite Hermitian channel V and τ≥0, the Gaussian average of exp(i√τ xV) exists and equals exp(−τV²/2). [gaussianHubbardStratonovich](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.GaussianAuxiliaryField/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.GaussianAuxiliaryField.gaussianHubbardStratonovich)

## Jerrum & Sinclair (1993) · [Polynomial-Time Approximation Algorithms for the Ising Model](https://doi.org/10.1137/0222066)

- **Supporting lemma**. The XOR of the two-site masks of an edge subset is its odd-degree boundary. Products of spin characters give the boundary character; Walsh orthogonality performs the sum over spin configurations. [boundary_empty](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.boundary_empty) · [boundary_insert](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.boundary_insert) · [characterProduct](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.characterProduct) · [orthogonal](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.orthogonal)
- **Special case**. Theorem 1, zero external field and homogeneous unit coupling. The physical Ising partition function is 2ⁿ cosh(β)ᵐ times the total weight tanh(β)^|A| of even subgraphs. Inserting a spin character selects the sector with its specified odd boundary. [boltzmannExpansion](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.boltzmannExpansion) · [edgeFactor](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.edgeFactor) · [partitionExpansion](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.partitionExpansion) · [transform](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.transform)
- **Derived result**. The Gibbs spin correlation is bounded above by one, so prescribed-boundary subgraph mass cannot exceed even mass. The negative ferromagnetic quantum mean energy is the sum of the two-site sector ratios; the even sector has positive mass. [energySectors](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.energySectors) · [evenPositive](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.evenPositive) · [sectorDomination](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.sectorDomination)
- **Supporting lemma**. Insertion or deletion of a specified edge toggles its two-site boundary. A partition by edge occupancy expresses the two-point sector in terms of the even mass and its occupied-edge mass. [edgeReadout](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticReadout/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.edgeReadout) · [occupancyInsert](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticReadout/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.occupancyInsert) · [sectorInsert](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticReadout/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.sectorInsert) · [toggleBoundary](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticReadout/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.toggleBoundary)
- **Special case**. Theorem 12 for the zero-field unit-strength couplings used by Troyer and Wiese. For every β>0 the negative quantum mean energy is the even-subgraph expectation of m x + (x⁻¹−x)|A|, x=tanh β. Its pointwise range lies between m x and m/x. [readoutBounds](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticReadout/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.readoutBounds) · [sumReadout](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticReadout/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.sumReadout) · [tanhPositive](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticReadout/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.tanhPositive) · [thermalReadout](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticReadout/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.thermalReadout) · [totalOccupancy](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticReadout/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.totalOccupancy)
- **Supporting lemma**. Products over odd sites introduce a positive fugacity u. Summing over all possible boundaries gives (1+u)ⁿ; sector expansion and positivity give a normalized positive boosted measure. [boostPositive](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRejection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.boostPositive) · [boostedExpansion](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRejection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.boostedExpansion) · [boostedWeightPositive](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRejection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.boostedWeightPositive) · [factorPositive](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRejection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.factorPositive) · [sumOddFactor](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRejection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.sumOddFactor)
- **Special case**. Theorem 10, μ=0 conditioned from μ₀=1/n. Rejecting boosted configurations with an odd boundary gives the exact even law. The success probability for exact boosted draws is at least 1/3, uniformly over β>0 and finite nonempty site sets. [acceptanceMass](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRejection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.acceptanceMass) · [evenAcceptance](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRejection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.evenAcceptance) · [rejectionLaw](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRejection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.rejectionLaw)
- **Supporting lemma**. Flipping an edge is a fixed-point-free involution. The single-edge lazy Metropolis acceptance is half the minimum weight ratio, lies in (0,1/2], and satisfies detailed balance. [acceptanceBounds](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticMetropolis/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.acceptanceBounds) · [edgeBalance](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticMetropolis/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.edgeBalance) · [edgeProbability](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticMetropolis/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.edgeProbability) · [flipDifferent](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticMetropolis/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.flipDifferent) · [flipTwice](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticMetropolis/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.flipTwice)
- **Special case**. Section 2 subgraphs-world chain. Choosing an edge uniformly and lazily toggling it with a Metropolis test gives a stochastic kernel reversible and stationary for the normalized boosted distribution. The empty-edge kernel is the identity. [detailedBalance](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticMetropolis/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.detailedBalance) · [kernelLazy](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticMetropolis/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.kernelLazy) · [kernelProbability](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticMetropolis/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.kernelProbability) · [stationary](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticMetropolis/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.stationary)
- **Supporting lemma**. The importance proposal increases x to max(x,1/(m+1)). The even mass changes by at most a factor three. Every nonempty even subgraph of a genuine two-site model has at least two occupied edges, which controls the small-x correction. [allSubsets](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticImportance/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.allSubsets) · [boostCorrection](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticImportance/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.boostCorrection) · [emptyLower](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticImportance/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.emptyLower) · [evenAtLeastTwo](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticImportance/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.evenAtLeastTwo) · [proposalRange](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticImportance/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.proposalRange) · [temperatureBoost](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticImportance/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.temperatureBoost)
- **Derived result**. A variant of Theorem 12’s reweighting for unit positive bonds. Under the positive boosted proposal the denominator lies in [0,1] with mean ≥1/9; the dimensionless numerator is bounded by (m+1)² uniformly in β>0. The ratio of their means is exactly the negative energy divided by m tanh β. [observationBounds](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticImportance/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.observationBounds) · [proposalMeans](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticImportance/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.proposalMeans)
- **Supporting lemma**. Independent finite draws have product mass and permit a one-coordinate recursion. Expanding the centered sum gives its exact second moment, linear in the batch size. [batchNormalized](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSamples/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.batchNormalized) · [batchSucc](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSamples/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.batchSucc) · [expectationLinear](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSamples/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.expectationLinear) · [secondMoment](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSamples/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.secondMoment)
- **Derived result**. For a readout in [0,L], k independent approximate draws at unhalved L¹ error η yield mean-square error ≤2 L²(1/k+η²) about the target expectation. Sampling and distributional bias are both included. - **Supporting lemma**. The normalized boosted law realizes the subgraph expectations, and finite Metropolis runs initialized at the empty graph are probability laws. Clamping the empirical denominator below by 1/9 controls the squared error of the ratio. - **Derived result**. At positive inverse temperature on a nonempty ferromagnet, a common mean-square bound B for bounded empirical readouts gives mean-square energy error at most 324 B E². The same estimate applies to independent samples and to trajectories. [ratioEnergyMSE](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticEnergySampling/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.ratioEnergyMSE)
- **Derived result**. For k independent t-step runs at any β>0 on a nonempty unit ferromagnet, relative MSE is bounded by 648(m+1)⁴(1/k+η²), where η bounds the actual finite-time unhalved L¹ distance to stationarity. The small-β importance factors and the finite-chain bias are included. [energySamplingRMS](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticEnergySampling/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.energySamplingRMS)
- **Supporting lemma**. A trail toggles only its endpoints; disjoint edge unions add parity boundaries by XOR. A vertex with no remaining incident edge has even remaining degree. [boundaryQuiet](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticTrails/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.boundaryQuiet) · [boundaryUnion](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticTrails/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.boundaryUnion) · [joinEnds](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticTrails/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.joinEnds) · [otherEnd](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticTrails/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.otherEnd) · [sameEnds](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticTrails/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.sameEnds)
- **Special case**. Section 4 canonical paths. In an arbitrary two-site edge difference, construct edge-disjoint maximal trails, starting at an odd vertex whenever possible. Their concatenation visits every edge once; each prefix has at most two extra odd vertices outside the final odd boundary. - **Supporting lemma**. A symmetric difference adds odd-site parity modulo two. During a canonical prefix the two encoded configurations preserve the sum of occupied edge counts; only the bounded set of extra odd vertices can decrease their combined fugacity. - **Special case**. Section 4 canonical-path weight estimate. For T=I△P and U=F△P, with P a prefix of the symmetric-difference edge ordering, u⁴w(I)w(F)≤w(T)w(U). - **Supporting lemma**. The canonical order is chosen using only the symmetric difference of the endpoints, and its pre-edge prefixes stay within the input bond set. - **Supporting lemma**. Knowing the pre-transition configuration T, the toggled edge, and U determines I△F=T△U. Its fixed order determines the prefix, then I and F are recovered by undoing its flips. - **Special case**. Section 4 canonical path encoding. The total endpoint weight using a given current state T and edge is at most w(T)Z/u⁴ by injectivity and the four-power fugacity comparison. - **Supporting lemma**. A physical edge toggle is symmetric difference with its singleton mask. Telescoping with finite Cauchy–Schwarz bounds the squared endpoint difference by the number of available edges times the sum of the squared gradients along the canonical path. - **Derived result**. All endpoint pairs are summed according to their weights and rerouted by current state and edge. The verified congestion bound compares the full pair quadratic form with the sum of edge gradients. - **Supporting lemma**. A single flip changes the edge count by one and exactly two vertex parities, so a homogeneous boosted edge with 0<x,u≤1 is accepted with probability at least x u²/2. - **Derived result**. Section 4 mixing mechanism in Poincaré form. The stationary endpoint-pair mean squared difference times x u⁶ is at most 2m² times the stationary one-step squared difference for the actual lazy transition kernel. - **Supporting lemma**. The boosted finite mass normalizes to a positive law; the mean squared difference for two independent draws is twice the variance.
