- **Derived result**. Detailed balance, laziness and the proved Poincaré inequality contract the squared stationary L² norm of a centered observable by at least x u⁶/(4m²) per update. The density of the actual empty-start chain minus one evolves by this operator and stays centered. [centered](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticMixing/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.centered) · [contraction](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticMixing/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.contraction) · [densityStep](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticMixing/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.densityStep)
- **Derived result**. Section 4 mixing result for the homogeneous zero-field proposal. The empty initialization has squared centered density norm at most 2ᵐ. The unhalved L¹ distance after t steps is at most the square root of 2ᵐ(1−x u⁶/(4m²))ᵗ. [initialSquare](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticMixing/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.initialSquare) · [mixingDistance](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticMixing/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.mixingDistance)
- **Derived result**. At u=1/n and proposal weight y=max(tanh β,1/(m+1)), an explicit block of 4m²(m+1)n⁶ updates halves the norm contraction factor. The supplied burn-in achieves unhalved L¹ error ≤1/[36(m+1)²s] from the empty subgraph. [burnInAccuracy](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticApproximation/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.burnInAccuracy) · [halving](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticApproximation/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.halving) · [rateBudget](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticApproximation/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.rateBudget)
- **Special case**. Jerrum–Sinclair Theorem 12 for homogeneous unit-strength, zero-field ferromagnetic Hamiltonians. The concrete empty-start lazy edge-toggle chain, run independently for the stated burn-in and batch sizes, approximates the physical quantum thermal energy with relative RMS ≤1/s for every β>0. The stated work counts ideal random edge proposals and real-valued acceptance tests. [allTemperatureEnergy](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticApproximation/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.allTemperatureEnergy)
- **Supporting lemma**. The exact sign-free quantum mean energy is zero both on an empty graph at any β and at β=0 for every two-site graph, so those cases require no sampling. [emptyEnergy](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticApproximation/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.emptyEnergy) · [infiniteTemperature](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticApproximation/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.infiniteTemperature)
- **Supporting lemma**. The actual joint distribution of consecutive Markov states is normalized. Conditioning on the first state proves exact first- and second-moment recurrences for the cumulative readout, with no independence assumption on successive samples. [trajectoryCons](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticTrajectory/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.trajectoryCons) · [trajectoryMoment](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticTrajectory/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.trajectoryMoment) · [trajectoryProbability](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticTrajectory/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.trajectoryProbability)
- **Supporting lemma**. Changing the initial law by unhalved L¹ distance η changes any entire bounded path expectation by at most its range times η. The proved stationary subgraphs-world distribution preserves expectations under the transition operator. [initialDrift](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticTrajectory/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.initialDrift) · [stationaryMean](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticTrajectory/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.stationaryMean)
- **Supporting lemma**. Finite expectation scales and preserves pointwise bounds under nonnegative weights. The expected conditional sum of a centered observable stays zero under the stationary trajectory law. [expectedMono](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticTrajectoryVariance/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.expectedMono) · [expectedScale](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticTrajectoryVariance/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.expectedScale) · [futureCentered](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticTrajectoryVariance/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.futureCentered)
- **Derived result**. For the lazy finite box chain with squared L² contraction 1−r, the conditional expected cumulative readout has stationary squared norm at most 4Var(f)/r². The actual stationary sum of k centered observations has variance at most 6kVar(f)/r, including their temporal correlations. [futureSumBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticTrajectoryVariance/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.futureSumBound) · [futureVariance](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticTrajectoryVariance/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.futureVariance)
- **Derived result**. For one chain whose initial law has unhalved L¹ distance ≤η from stationarity and f in [0,L], the consecutive k-sample mean has squared error about its true stationary mean at most L²[6/(kr)+η]. [trajectoryError](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticTrajectoryVariance/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.trajectoryError)
- **Derived result**. For the importance-weighted numerator and clipped denominator observed along a single boosted chain, the relative squared energy error is at most 324(m+1)⁴[6/(kr)+η], uniformly over homogeneous zero-field unit-coupling systems with positive inverse temperature and at least one bond. [trajectoryEnergyRMS](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.trajectoryEnergyRMS)
- **Special case**. All-temperature sign-free comparison for the homogeneous, zero-field Hamiltonian in Troyer–Wiese Eq. (9), with a single burn-in and consecutive Jerrum–Sinclair proposals. For 0<ε≤1 the actual mean squared error is at most ε² times the squared quantum thermal energy, and the number of ideal edge updates is at most 16m²(m+1)n⁶[m+2+5184(m+1)⁴]/ε². The counted operations use real-valued acceptance probabilities; no finite-bit arithmetic cost is claimed. [polynomialComparison](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.polynomialComparison) · [singleChainEnergy](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.singleChainEnergy) · [singleChainWork](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.singleChainWork)
- **Derived result**. On the actual nearest-neighbour ±1/0 open cubic spin-glass inputs of Eq. (10), there are at most 3N occupied bonds. The checked single-chain sign-free energy estimator has relative RMS error at most ε with at most 764414784 N¹³/ε² ideal edge proposals, for every β>0 and every 0<ε≤1. Zero-bond or β=0 instances have exact zero energy. [cubicPolynomialComparison](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.cubicPolynomialComparison) · [latticeBudget](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.latticeBudget)
- **Supporting lemma**. Integer division counts accepted bit strings for lazy Metropolis. For positive rational proposal and boost, integer weights with a common denominator give the physical acceptance probability to within one unit of the random-word precision. [acceptanceError](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.acceptanceError) · [acceptedStrings](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.acceptedStrings) · [choiceSum](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.choiceSum) · [cutoffLe](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.cutoffLe) · [finiteAcceptance](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.finiteAcceptance) · [integerRatio](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.integerRatio) · [integerWeightSpec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.integerWeightSpec) · [wordAverage](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.wordAverage)
- **Derived result**. Two finite uniform words select the proposed bond and its acceptance. Every readout in [0,1] incurs at most (m+1)/2ᵇ one-step error for m bonds, including non-power-of-two bond counts. [finiteStepError](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.finiteStepError) · [graphReadout](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.graphReadout) · [kernelReadout](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.kernelReadout) · [pairProbability](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.pairProbability) · [pairReadout](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.pairReadout)
- **Derived result**. Row-law errors control arbitrary bounded readouts of an entire correlated trajectory. For rational weights the two-word sampler gives an explicit bound linear in the trajectory length. [finiteTrajectory](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.finiteTrajectory) · [rowL1](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.rowL1) · [trajectoryError](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.trajectoryError)

The dyadic numerator here is specified mathematically by a ceiling. A k-step trajectory driven by two finite words per update differs from the ideal chain by at most kL/2ʳ for any path observable in [0,L]. The direct branch uses 12(s+2) Taylor terms for s-bit accuracy. ## Jiang et al. (2018) · [Quantum Algorithms to Simulate Many-Body Physics of Correlated Fermions](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevApplied.9.044036)

- **Supporting lemma**. An orthonormal frame extends to an orbital unitary. Its exterior power sends the selected occupation determinant to the target wedge. [existsOrbitalUnitary](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants.existsOrbitalUnitary) · [existsSlaterPreparation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants.existsSlaterPreparation)
- **Special case**. The coefficient of a fixed occupation determinant in a wedge of orbitals is the corresponding orbital-matrix minor. [occupationAmplitude](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants.occupationAmplitude)
- **Supporting lemma**. A mode fixed by orbital gates is unchanged by their product, and its coordinate is unchanged under inverse action. [coordinate_of_fixed](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants.coordinate_of_fixed) · [listProduct_fixed](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants.listProduct_fixed)
- **Supporting lemma**. A two-mode orbital unitary can eliminate one coefficient. Iterating eliminations prepares a normalized vector on its allowed support. [eliminateCoefficient](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants.eliminateCoefficient) · [gateForPair](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants.gateForPair) · [prepareVector](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants.prepareVector)
- **Supporting lemma**. Unrestricted pairs of orbital modes suffice to factor an orbital unitary. Exterior-power lifting respects the product of gates. [existsTwoModeFactorization](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants.existsTwoModeFactorization) · [exteriorGate_product](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants.exteriorGate_product)
- **Special case**. Any target orthonormal frame gives a Slater determinant preparable by a finite list of number-preserving one- or two-mode gates. The circuit model permits unrestricted mode pairs and asserts existence of a gate list. [existsTwoModeSlaterPreparation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants.existsTwoModeSlaterPreparation)

## Kato (1950) · [On the Adiabatic Theorem of Quantum Mechanics](https://doi.org/10.1143/JPSJ.5.435)

- **Supporting lemma**. Differentiating an idempotent yields the double-commutator identity for its tangent. [projectionDerivative](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.AdiabaticTheorem/FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.projectionDerivative)
- **Special case**. The commutator-corrected evolution exactly intertwines a differentiable idempotent on [0,1]. A commuting generator and a contractive propagator with specified derivatives and inverse are hypotheses. [katoTransport](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.AdiabaticTheorem/FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.katoTransport)
- **Derived result**. Leakage is at most (4L/g+sD)/T when a reduced inverse of the shifted generator and a differentiable commutator correction obey the stated uniform bounds. [reducedResolventPreparation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.AdiabaticTheorem/FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.reducedResolventPreparation)
- **Special case**. A two-band generator a(s)I+gap·P(s) with fixed nonzero splitting, contractive idempotents, bounded first and second projection derivatives and supplied slow evolutions has O(1/T) leakage. The limit is at s=1. [gappedBandPreparation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.AdiabaticTheorem/FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.gappedBandPreparation) · [gappedBandPreparation_limit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.AdiabaticTheorem/FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.gappedBandPreparation_limit)

## Kitaev, Shen & Vyalyi (2002) · [Classical and Quantum Computation](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/047)

- **Supporting lemma**. Finite matrix expectations on normalized states have a nonempty range bounded below, permitting the use of an infimum for ground energy. [energy_range_bddBelow](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.HistoryHamiltonian/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.energy_range_bddBelow) · [energy_range_nonempty](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.HistoryHamiltonian/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.energy_range_nonempty)
- **Supporting lemma**. For a padded circuit, the positive integer denominator D defines cutoffs 1/D and 2/D. The implementation bounds D by 2¹³·130·s⁴, where s is gates + qubits + 65. [cutoffDen_le](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.HistoryHamiltonian/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.cutoffDen_le) · [cutoffDen_pos](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.HistoryHamiltonian/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.cutoffDen_pos) · [promiseGap](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.HistoryHamiltonian/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.promiseGap) · [rationalCutoffs](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.HistoryHamiltonian/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.rationalCutoffs)
- **Special case**. Explicit resources for the five-local history construction of §14.4. The encoded terms are Hermitian, have norm at most one, and satisfy gate-, clock-, and initialization-dependent count and encoding-length bounds. [resources](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.HistoryHamiltonian/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.resources)
- **Special case**. For a legal verifier with T gates, a normalized witness of acceptance at least 1−1/[2¹⁴(T+65)²] gives low energy. Acceptance at most 1/4 for every legal normalized witness gives high energy for every normalized history state. The three declarations express this via states, an infimum, or a bit-string promise. [groundEnergy_promise](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.HistoryHamiltonian/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.groundEnergy_promise) · [verifier_reduction](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.HistoryHamiltonian/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.verifier_reduction) · [witnessEnergy_promise](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.HistoryHamiltonian/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.witnessEnergy_promise)
- **Supporting lemma**. The Qatlib-derived Hamiltonian and denominator can be recovered uniquely from the chosen bit-string encoding, whose length is polynomial in padded circuit size. [reduction_length](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.HistoryHamiltonian/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.reduction_length) · [serialize_injective](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.HistoryHamiltonian/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.serialize_injective)
- **Supporting lemma**. Implementation lemmas identify template-generated local tables with the history construction, count propagation, initialization, output and clock descriptors, and bound scalar table entries. [scalarEntries_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.HistoryHamiltonian/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.scalarEntries_bound) · [termDescriptors_correct](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.HistoryHamiltonian/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.termDescriptors_correct) · [termDescriptors_count](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.HistoryHamiltonian/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.termDescriptors_count)
- **Derived result**. Separated cutoffs make the two energy promises disjoint. Ground-energy estimation within one third of the stated gap bound, or normalized preparation and measurement each within one fifth, decides the verifier promise. [estimate_decides](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.HistoryHamiltonian/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.estimate_decides) · [localEnergy_disjoint](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.HistoryHamiltonian/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.localEnergy_disjoint) · [preparation_and_measurement_decide](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.HistoryHamiltonian/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.preparation_and_measurement_decide)
- **Supporting lemma**. Linear circuit evolution commutes with complex rescaling, and the zero ket obeys the verifier initialization predicate. [run_scalar](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.HistoryHamiltonian/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.run_scalar) · [zero_input_legal](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.HistoryHamiltonian/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.zero_input_legal)
- **Special case**. If every work wire must start at zero, all legal normalized witnesses give the same acceptance as the zero ket. Its high or low acceptance at the specified thresholds suffices for the history-energy bounds. [witnessFree_acceptance](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.HistoryHamiltonian/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.witnessFree_acceptance) · [witnessFree_groundEnergy](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.HistoryHamiltonian/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.witnessFree_groundEnergy)
- **Supporting lemma**. Checks distinct wire addresses, involutivity of the majority-controlled update and preservation of the three vote bits. [distinct](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Majority/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Majority.distinct) · [involution](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Majority/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Majority.involution) · [read_toggle](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Majority/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Majority.read_toggle)
- **Derived result**. Composes three Toffolis to XOR the majority of three distinct vote bits into a fourth wire. The legal 45-gate word implements that permutation on basis kets and on arbitrary amplitudes. [word_amplitude](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Majority/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Majority.word_amplitude) · [word_ket](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Majority/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Majority.word_ket) · [word_legal](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Majority/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Majority.word_legal) · [word_length](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Majority/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Majority.word_length)
- **Derived result**. For a clean answer wire the readout probability is the total mass of the majority patterns. Independent votes with marginal p, in particular three independently prepared copies, give 3p²−2p³. This is the witness-free gadget used for repetition amplification. [independent_votes](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Majority/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Majority.independent_votes) · [readout_probability](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Majority/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Majority.readout_probability) · [three_copy_readout](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Majority/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Majority.three_copy_readout)
- **Supporting lemma**. The Born weights of independent product amplitudes factor, and three-vote failure obeys the same cubic recursion as success. [failure_probability](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Majority/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Majority.failure_probability) · [productMass](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Majority/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Majority.productMass)
- **Derived result**. For initial decision error at most 1/3, iteration of the three-vote map obeys the explicit geometric, three-round halving, and doubly exponential bounds proved here. [error_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Majority/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Majority.error_bound) · [squared_error_decay](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Majority/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Majority.squared_error_decay) · [three_level_halving](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Majority/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Majority.three_level_halving)
- **Supporting lemma**. Verifies disjoint wire addresses and factorization of zero initialization and evolution for three independently prepared copies. [copy_ne](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Amplify/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Amplify.copy_ne) · [joint_product](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Amplify/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Amplify.joint_product) · [joint_run](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Amplify/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Amplify.joint_run) · [joint_zero](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Amplify/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Amplify.joint_zero)
- **Derived result**. For a legal witness-free gate word on the zero input, moving three copies to disjoint registers and appending majority uses 3T+45 gates and changes acceptance p to 3p²−2p³. [step_accept](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Amplify/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Amplify.step_accept) · [word_accept](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Amplify/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Amplify.word_accept) · [word_legal](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Amplify/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Amplify.word_legal) · [word_length](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Amplify/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Amplify.word_length)
- **Supporting lemma**. Checks all-zero initialization after a round, the gate and width recurrences, and the unit-interval bound on acceptance. [repeat_all_zero](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Amplify/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Amplify.repeat_all_zero) · [repeat_gate_count](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Amplify/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Amplify.repeat_gate_count) · [repeat_width](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Amplify/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Amplify.repeat_width) · [zeroAcceptance_mem](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Amplify/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Amplify.zeroAcceptance_mem)
- **Derived result**. After k recursive rounds on zero-input circuits, the acceptance and rejection probabilities equal k iterations of the three-vote cubic on the original success or error. [repeat_accept](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Amplify/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Amplify.repeat_accept) · [repeat_reject](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Amplify/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Amplify.repeat_reject)
- **Supporting lemma**. Bounds the size and precision budget for the library’s recursively tripled circuit. At 9+floor(log₂ s) rounds the amplified padded size is at most 4⁹s³. - **Derived result**. With initial decision error in [0,1/3], the chosen majority depth reaches error at most 1/(2¹⁴u²), where the final size u satisfies the proved growth bound. [reaches_precision](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.AmplificationBounds/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.AmplificationBounds.reaches_precision)
- **Supporting lemma**. Unary arithmetic, index enumeration and integer logarithm compute the amplifier depth. The correctness and step counts use the library’s list-cell cost model. - **Supporting lemma**. Checks legality, parsing and cost for finite gate-code words. Decoding an encoded legal circuit reconstructs its gates. - **Supporting lemma**. Renumbers a gate or word onto a disjoint register, letting the compiler emit three copies with bounded list-processing cost. - **Derived result**. - **Derived result**. Computes rounded integer entries for the finite gate alphabet and the corresponding propagation terms. Atom extraction and gate entries have explicit quadratic bit-position bounds. [circuit_atom_cost](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.DyadicCoefficients/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.DyadicCoefficients.circuit_atom_cost) · [gateOutput_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.DyadicCoefficients/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.DyadicCoefficients.gateOutput_spec) · [propagation_from_bits](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.DyadicCoefficients/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.DyadicCoefficients.propagation_from_bits)
- **Supporting lemma**. Reconciles potentially repeated Qatlib wire lists with injective local supports and checks basis indexing and agreement on spectator wires. [distinctWire_range](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.ElectronicStructure/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.ElectronicStructure.distinctWire_range) · [distinctWire_readWire](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.ElectronicStructure/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.ElectronicStructure.distinctWire_readWire) · [outside_iff](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.ElectronicStructure/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.ElectronicStructure.outside_iff) · [tableIndex_restrict](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.ElectronicStructure/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.ElectronicStructure.tableIndex_restrict)
- **Supporting lemma**. Reading the history Hamiltonian’s finite tables as local Hermitian spin terms reproduces individual embedded kernels and their matrix sum. [decodeHamiltonian](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.ElectronicStructure/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.ElectronicStructure.decodeHamiltonian) · [decodeTerm_kernel](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.ElectronicStructure/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.ElectronicStructure.decodeTerm_kernel)
- **Supporting lemma**. Bounds local entries by the embedded operator norm; each decoded circuit term acts on at most five qubits with entries of norm at most one. [circuitTables](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.ElectronicStructure/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.ElectronicStructure.circuitTables) · [entry_norm_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.ElectronicStructure/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.ElectronicStructure.entry_norm_bound) · [table_is_global_entry](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.ElectronicStructure/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.ElectronicStructure.table_is_global_entry)
- **Derived result**. For this encoding each history qubit corresponds to one electron among two modes. The number of Pauli words, support size and penalty are explicitly bounded, with up to ten modes per history term. [circuitFermionResources](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.ElectronicStructure/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.ElectronicStructure.circuitFermionResources) · [circuitPenalty_sufficient](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.ElectronicStructure/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.ElectronicStructure.circuitPenalty_sufficient) · [electronCount](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.ElectronicStructure/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.ElectronicStructure.electronCount)
- **Derived result**. For the amplified-verifier promise, both energy cutoffs transfer from the five-local history construction to the fixed-number dual-rail Hamiltonian. This is a construction of occupation-space operators with a penalty. [circuit_to_fixedParticle](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.ElectronicStructure/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.ElectronicStructure.circuit_to_fixedParticle)
- **Derived result**. A normalized preparation and energy readout, each accurate to one fifth of the proved gap bound, suffice to distinguish the amplified verifier promise for this constructed fixed-number Hamiltonian. [electronicPreparation_decides](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.ElectronicStructure/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.ElectronicStructure.electronicPreparation_decides)
- **Supporting lemma**. A sequence of X gates on distinct selected wires loads a specified basis string from the zero register in at most q gates for q wires. [inputWord_legal](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.UniformCircuits/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.UniformCircuits.inputWord_legal) · [inputWord_length](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.UniformCircuits/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.UniformCircuits.inputWord_length) · [inputWord_prepares](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.UniformCircuits/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.UniformCircuits.inputWord_prepares) · [load_subset](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.UniformCircuits/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.UniformCircuits.load_subset)
- **Supporting lemma**. Matches the acceptance probability of the basis-loaded decision word to that of the history verifier, including the degenerate zero-width fallback. [history_acceptance](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.UniformCircuits/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.UniformCircuits.history_acceptance) · [initialized_acceptance](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.UniformCircuits/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.UniformCircuits.initialized_acceptance) · [reject_probability](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.UniformCircuits/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.UniformCircuits.reject_probability)
- **Derived result**. For a valid clocked uniform circuit generator, each basis input’s amplified-completeness or soundness promise gives the corresponding fixed-number energy bound with explicit size control. [generated_electronic_promise](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.UniformCircuits/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.UniformCircuits.generated_electronic_promise)
- **Supporting lemma**. Establishes size growth, full initialization and denominator bounds for the repeated witness-free verifier, including its cubic amplified-size bound. [amplified_size](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.BoundedErrorReduction/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.BoundedErrorReduction.amplified_size) · [denominator_ge_four](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.BoundedErrorReduction/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.BoundedErrorReduction.denominator_ge_four) · [growth](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.BoundedErrorReduction/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.BoundedErrorReduction.growth) · [initialized_after](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.BoundedErrorReduction/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.BoundedErrorReduction.initialized_after) · [size_positive](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.BoundedErrorReduction/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.BoundedErrorReduction.size_positive)
- **Derived result**. An all-zero-input decision circuit with acceptance ≥2/3 or ≤1/3 satisfies the particular high-completeness or soundness promise required by the history encoding after the chosen majority rounds. [amplified_promise](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.BoundedErrorReduction/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.BoundedErrorReduction.amplified_promise)
- **Derived result**. Maps the ordinary bounded-error decision-circuit promise to low or high ground energy of the decoded five-local template output. [circuit_to_local_energy](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.BoundedErrorReduction/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.BoundedErrorReduction.circuit_to_local_energy)
- **Derived result**. Composes bounded-error amplification, the history Hamiltonian, and dual-rail encoding for decision circuits. The uniform version takes a valid clocked generator and arbitrary basis input. [circuit_to_electronic_energy](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.BoundedErrorReduction/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.BoundedErrorReduction.circuit_to_electronic_energy) · [uniform_circuit_to_electronic_energy](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.BoundedErrorReduction/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.BoundedErrorReduction.uniform_circuit_to_electronic_energy)
- **Supporting lemma**. Identifies the bit-list transducer output with applying amplification and the history-template compiler in succession. [transducer_output](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.BoundedErrorReduction/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.BoundedErrorReduction.transducer_output) · [transducer_value](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.BoundedErrorReduction/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.BoundedErrorReduction.transducer_value)
- **Derived result**. The serialized bounded-error decision circuit maps to decodable five-local terms and rational cutoffs. The gap is inverse polynomial in original size and the transducer has a polynomial clock in the specified bit-list model. [transducer_correct](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.BoundedErrorReduction/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.BoundedErrorReduction.transducer_correct) · [transducer_promise_gap](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.BoundedErrorReduction/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.BoundedErrorReduction.transducer_promise_gap) · [transducer_time](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.BoundedErrorReduction/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.BoundedErrorReduction.transducer_time)
- **Supporting lemma**. For Qatlib’s gate vocabulary the interpreter equals matrix-vector multiplication by the gate-word matrix. [run_finiteCodeCircuit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QmaCompleteness.HistoryHam.QmaCompletenessDefs/Qatlib.Concepts.QmaCompleteness.HistoryHam.run_finiteCodeCircuit)
- **Supporting lemma**. Qatlib’s self-delimiting integers let the bit representation uniquely determine its five-local Hamiltonian encoding. [clause3Aux_encNat_selfDelimiting](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QmaCompleteness.HistoryHam.QmaCompletenessRefined/Qatlib.Concepts.QmaCompleteness.HistoryHam.clause3Aux_encNat_selfDelimiting) · [clause3Aux_toBits_injective](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QmaCompleteness.HistoryHam.QmaCompletenessRefined/Qatlib.Concepts.QmaCompleteness.HistoryHam.clause3Aux_toBits_injective)
- **Supporting lemma**. Counts propagation, initialization, output, and ordered clock pairs in Qatlib’s concrete local term constructor. [outputTerms_length](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QueryComplexity.DegreeMethod.QmaCompletenessNativeOutput/Qatlib.Concepts.QueryComplexity.DegreeMethod.outputTerms_length)
- **Supporting lemma**. Identifies the exact integer obtained by rounding a finite gate coefficient atom to dyadic precision for the discretized history construction. [floor_atom](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.UniformBqp.FiniteGateWord.QmaCompletenessNumerics/Qatlib.Concepts.UniformBqp.FiniteGateWord.floor_atom)
- **Special case**. Qatlib checks an explicit five-local dyadic history encoding and energy bounds for its specified verifier promises. This construction supplies part of the circuit-to-Hamiltonian argument in §14.4. [qma_history_locality_description_and_energy_bounds](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Papers.LocalHam06.MainResults.QmaCompleteness/Qatlib.Papers.LocalHam06.MainResults.qma_history_locality_description_and_energy_bounds)
- **Supporting lemma**. Qatlib bounds the scale and the positive inverse-polynomial gap for its normalized dyadic circuit Hamiltonian. [denominator_le_poly](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Papers.LocalHam06.MainResults.QmaCompletenessNormalized/Qatlib.Papers.LocalHam06.MainResults.denominator_le_poly) · [denominator_pos](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Papers.LocalHam06.MainResults.QmaCompletenessNormalized/Qatlib.Papers.LocalHam06.MainResults.denominator_pos) · [gap_inverse_poly](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Papers.LocalHam06.MainResults.QmaCompletenessNormalized/Qatlib.Papers.LocalHam06.MainResults.gap_inverse_poly) · [gap_positive](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Papers.LocalHam06.MainResults.QmaCompletenessNormalized/Qatlib.Papers.LocalHam06.MainResults.gap_positive)
- **Special case**. The padded history Hamiltonian separates legal high-acceptance witnesses from universal low acceptance. Completeness requires deficit ≤1/[2¹⁴(T_padded+1)²] and soundness ≤1/4, with rational thresholds dependent on the encoding. [padded_normalized_contract](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Papers.LocalHam06.MainResults.QmaCompletenessNormalized/Qatlib.Papers.LocalHam06.MainResults.padded_normalized_contract)
- **Supporting lemma**. The circuit used in Qatlib’s history-state soundness estimate has exactly 64 additional gates. [paid64_length](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Papers.LocalHam06.MainResults.QmaCompletenessPadding/Qatlib.Papers.LocalHam06.MainResults.paid64_length)
- **Supporting lemma**. Encoding-specific size facts control the length of self-delimiting integers and the exact number of real and imaginary table entries for a local term. [encNat_length_le_self](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Papers.LocalHam06.MainResults.QmaCompletenessSize/Qatlib.Papers.LocalHam06.MainResults.encNat_length_le_self) · [termEntries_length](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Papers.LocalHam06.MainResults.QmaCompletenessSize/Qatlib.Papers.LocalHam06.MainResults.termEntries_length)
- **Paper result**. Proposition 14.2, pp. 142–143. For a positive contraction H, the positive-square-root outcome operators have effects H and I−H, the binary POVM in the local verification step. [termEffects](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.LocalHamiltonianVerification/FQChem.Complexity.LocalHamiltonianVerification.termEffects)
- **Paper result**. Proposition 14.2, p. 143. Choosing a term uniformly among r positive contractions gives Born acceptance probability 1−⟨ψ,Hψ⟩/r on every normalized witness ψ, where H is the sum of the terms. [acceptance_eq](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.LocalHamiltonianVerification/FQChem.Complexity.LocalHamiltonianVerification.acceptance_eq)
- **Paper result**. Proposition 14.2, pp. 142–143. In finite dimension the best acceptance probability equals 1−λmin(H)/r and a normalized witness attains it. This supplies the measurement and energy-gap argument; efficient synthesis and witness amplification are separate complexity claims. [maximumAcceptance](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.LocalHamiltonianVerification/FQChem.Complexity.LocalHamiltonianVerification.maximumAcceptance)
- **Supporting lemma**. The joint three-copy majority effect of binary witness measurements is monotone in the individual effects. Equal scalar effects give the cubic 3q²−2q³. [monotone](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.WitnessMajority/FQChem.Complexity.WitnessMajority.monotone) · [scalarMajority](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.WitnessMajority/FQChem.Complexity.WitnessMajority.scalarMajority)
- **Special case**. The three-copy case of witness amplification (Lemma 14.1) gives acceptance at most 3q²−2q³ for every normalized, possibly entangled witness when the one-copy effect is bounded by qI. [boundOfWitnesses](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.WitnessMajority/FQChem.Complexity.WitnessMajority.boundOfWitnesses) · [entangledAcceptance](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.WitnessMajority/FQChem.Complexity.WitnessMajority.entangledAcceptance) · [soundness](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.WitnessMajority/FQChem.Complexity.WitnessMajority.soundness) · [threeWitnessSoundness](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.WitnessMajority/FQChem.Complexity.WitnessMajority.threeWitnessSoundness)
- **Proof in the literature · needs Lean proof**. [Five-local Hamiltonian is QMA-complete](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#paper/kitaev-local-hamiltonian). Proposition 14.2 and Theorem 14.3 · five-local clock in §14.4.5. Given a sum of polynomially many qubit terms 0 ≤ Hⱼ ≤ I acting on at most five qubits each and thresholds a < b with inverse-polynomial separation, distinguish λmin(H) ≤ a from λmin(H) > b. This promise problem is QMA-complete under polynomial-time Karp reductions. The book calls the witness class BQNP.
  - **Remaining Lean proof**. The checked history-Hamiltonian reduction accepts amplified verifier circuits. Closing the full theorem requires composing arbitrary-witness amplification with the uniform polynomial-time reduction and implementing the local-term verifier at the promised precision.

## Kitaura et al. (1999) · [Fragment molecular orbital method: an approximate computational method for large molecules](https://doi.org/10.1016/S0009-2614%2899%2900874-X)

- **Special case**. For pair-additive dimer energies the two-body fragment expansion equals the sum of monomer energies and pair corrections. Equality assumes the stated pair model. [pairReconstruction](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Multiscale.ChoosingLayers.Fragments/FQChem.Multiscale.pairReconstruction)

## Kivlichan et al. (2018) · [Quantum Simulation of Electronic Structure with Linear Depth and Connectivity](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevLett.120.110501)

- **Supporting lemma**. Unrestricted pairs of orbital modes suffice to factor an orbital unitary. Exterior-power lifting respects the product of gates. [existsTwoModeFactorization](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants.existsTwoModeFactorization) · [exteriorGate_product](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants.exteriorGate_product)
- **Special case**. Any target orthonormal frame gives a Slater determinant preparable by a finite list of number-preserving one- or two-mode gates. The circuit model permits unrestricted mode pairs and asserts existence of a gate list. [existsTwoModeSlaterPreparation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.SlaterDeterminants.existsTwoModeSlaterPreparation)

## Kraus (1971) · [General state changes in quantum theory](https://doi.org/10.1016/0003-4916%2871%2990108-4)

- **Supporting lemma**. The matrix amplification acting on system blocks equals conjugation and summation with the ancilla-extended Kraus matrices. [lifted_operation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.OpenSystems.Kraus/FQChem.Dynamics.OpenSystems.Kraus.lifted_operation)
- **Derived result**. Kraus completeness preserves trace with an arbitrary finite ancilla. Combined with complete positivity it preserves normalized positive joint states. [jointDensityPreservation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.OpenSystems.Kraus/FQChem.Dynamics.OpenSystems.Kraus.jointDensityPreservation) · [tracePreservation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.OpenSystems.Kraus/FQChem.Dynamics.OpenSystems.Kraus.tracePreservation)
- **Special case**. For a finite family of matrices, the completeness relation ΣK†K=I makes the operator sum trace preserving. [krausTrace](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.OpenSystems.TracePreservation/FQChem.Dynamics.krausTrace)
- **Derived result**. Squared branch norms ‖Kᵢψ‖² are nonnegative and sum to one for a normalized input under Kraus completeness. This extends the probability rule to a finite generalized measurement. [bornCompleteness](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.MeasurementTheory/FQChem.Properties.MeasurementTheory.bornCompleteness)

## Krause, Klamroth & Saalfrank (2005) · [Time-dependent configuration-interaction calculations of laser-pulse-driven many-electron dynamics: Controlled dipole switching in lithium cyanide](https://doi.org/10.1063/1.1999636)

- **Derived result**. In a fixed real basis with symmetric linear H, the real and imaginary TD-CI component equations imply zero derivative of their total squared norm. [tdConfigurationInteraction](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.RealTimePropagation.TimeDependentCI/FQChem.Dynamics.tdConfigurationInteraction)

## Lanczos (1950) · [An iteration method for the solution of the eigenvalue problem of linear differential and integral operators](https://doi.org/10.6028/jres.045.026)

- **Derived result**. Enlarging the Hamiltonian-power Krylov space of a nonzero vector by one step cannot raise its Ritz infimum. [classicalKrylov](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.SubspaceDiagonalization.Krylov/FQChem.VariationalMethods.classicalKrylov)

## Lee et al. (2023) · [Evaluating the evidence for exponential quantum advantage in ground-state quantum chemistry](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-023-37587-6)

- **Supporting lemma**. The exponential of a sum of cluster generators acting on disjoint fragments is the Kronecker product of the fragment exponentials, even for approximate fragment amplitudes. [expRight](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.PerturbationTheory.CoupledClusterReplicas/FQChem.StatePrep.CoupledClusterReplicas.expRight) · [expSeparated](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.PerturbationTheory.CoupledClusterReplicas/FQChem.StatePrep.CoupledClusterReplicas.expSeparated)
- **Derived result**. For independent fragments and a product reference determinant, the similarity-transformed Hamiltonian is the sum of the individual transformed operators lifted to the composite. The projected coupled-cluster energies add exactly. [projectedSeparated](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.PerturbationTheory.CoupledClusterReplicas/FQChem.StatePrep.CoupledClusterReplicas.projectedSeparated) · [transformedSeparated](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.PerturbationTheory.CoupledClusterReplicas/FQChem.StatePrep.CoupledClusterReplicas.transformedSeparated)
- **Supporting lemma**. A positive-semidefinite energy lower bound and a saturating product eigenvector certify that independent fragments have additive ground energies, including minimization over entangled states. [groundSeparated](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.PerturbationTheory.CoupledClusterReplicas/FQChem.StatePrep.CoupledClusterReplicas.groundSeparated) · [tensorAction](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.PerturbationTheory.CoupledClusterReplicas/FQChem.StatePrep.CoupledClusterReplicas.tensorAction)
- **Derived result**. For L identical independent copies, exact ground energy and projected coupled-cluster energy are each L times their fragment value. The cluster amplitudes may be approximate. [groundCopies](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.PerturbationTheory.CoupledClusterReplicas/FQChem.StatePrep.CoupledClusterReplicas.groundCopies) · [projectedCopies](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.PerturbationTheory.CoupledClusterReplicas/FQChem.StatePrep.CoupledClusterReplicas.projectedCopies)
- **Special case**. Lee et al., discussion at Fig. 3A. For L independent copies of a fixed finite fragment, assume the fragment calculation reaches absolute error δ in at most C δ⁻ᵇ work. Solving at δ = ε/L gives total absolute energy error ≤ ε with at most (C+1)Lᵇ⁺¹ ε⁻ᵇ work, counting one energy addition per copy. The fragment convergence rate is an explicit hypothesis. [replicaScaling](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.PerturbationTheory.CoupledClusterReplicas/FQChem.StatePrep.CoupledClusterReplicas.replicaScaling)
- **Derived result**. A counterexample to an inference about uniform complexity. The abstract hierarchy has work L^k and inverse error 2^k. It escapes every C L^a (1/ε)^b bound along L = 1/ε. It does not construct a molecular coupled-cluster counterexample. [fixedRankBoundsDoNotGiveJointPolynomial](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.PerturbationTheory.TruncationScaling/FQChem.StatePrep.TruncationScaling.fixedRankBoundsDoNotGiveJointPolynomial)
- **Literature hypothesis · needs Lean proof**. [Single-reference coupled-cluster scaling](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#paper/lee-coupled-cluster-scaling). Single-reference coupled-cluster discussion at Fig. 3A · Supplementary §§S2.7, S5. Lee et al. propose poly(L)·poly(1/ε) effort for coupled cluster to reach energy error ε on single-reference problems, with L measuring system size. The argument uses empirical excitation-rank convergence for N₂ and extensivity for noninteracting replicas. The Lean statement fixes the promised family and costed solver, uses occupied-to-virtual cluster excitations, and asks for uniform exponents in size and inverse absolute energy error.
  - [Formal statement in Lean](https://github.com/the-matter-lab/fqchem/blob/6770a38071e5b621be99d9d04edbf8a8e41cbd6c/FQChem/Unproven/CoupledCluster.lean#L64)

## Levy (1979) · [Universal variational functionals of electron densities, first-order density matrices, and natural spin-orbitals and solution of the v-representability problem](https://doi.org/10.1073/pnas.76.12.6062)

- **Derived result**. Restricting the Coulomb constrained search to active densities plus a fixed environment gives an upper bound on the library’s molecular trial-class infimum. Attainment by a compatible split gives equality. [frozenMolecularEnergy](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Multiscale.ChoosingLayers.FrozenMolecularDensity/FQChem.Multiscale.frozenMolecularEnergy)
- **Derived result**. In an additive density model each active-plus-frozen split must be feasible. Restricting the infimum gives an upper bound, with equality if a split realizes the unrestricted infimum. [frozenSearch](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Multiscale.ChoosingLayers.QuantumEnvironments/FQChem.Multiscale.frozenSearch)
- **Supporting lemma**. The inverse-distance kernel is locally integrable in three dimensions. Its coordinate lifts give finite nuclear-attraction integrals against marginals of real smooth compactly supported molecular trial states. [coulombLocallyIntegrable](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.DensityFunctionals.CoulombIntegrability/FQChem.MolecularDFT.coulombLocallyIntegrable) · [locallyIntegrable_coordinate](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.DensityFunctionals.CoulombIntegrability/FQChem.MolecularDFT.locallyIntegrable_coordinate) · [nuclearAttractionIntegrable](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.DensityFunctionals.CoulombIntegrability/FQChem.MolecularDFT.nuclearAttractionIntegrable)
- **Special case**. For finitely many fixed point nuclei in three dimensions, the extended-real energy infimum over real, smooth, compactly supported, normalized antisymmetric trial functions equals the density constrained-search infimum. The proof discharges the nuclear-attraction integrability condition within this trial class. [coulombConstrainedSearch](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.DensityFunctionals.CoulombIntegrability/FQChem.MolecularDFT.coulombConstrainedSearch)
- **Supporting lemma**. A normalized molecular trial gives a unit Born measure and a one-body density measure of mass N. Integrating a potential against that density sums its electron-coordinate expectations under the marginal integrability hypothesis. [born_mass](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.DensityFunctionals.MolecularConstrainedSearch/FQChem.MolecularDFT.born_mass) · [density_mass](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.DensityFunctionals.MolecularConstrainedSearch/FQChem.MolecularDFT.density_mass) · [density_pairing](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.DensityFunctionals.MolecularConstrainedSearch/FQChem.MolecularDFT.density_pairing)
- **Special case**. The energy infimum over normalized smooth compactly supported real antisymmetric trial states on (ℝ³)ᴺ equals the infimum over densities of the internal-energy fiber infimum plus the potential pairing. This module assumes marginal integrability, including for fixed nuclei. [constrainedSearch](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.DensityFunctionals.MolecularConstrainedSearch/FQChem.MolecularDFT.constrainedSearch) · [molecularConstrainedSearch](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.DensityFunctionals.MolecularConstrainedSearch/FQChem.MolecularDFT.molecularConstrainedSearch)

## Lewis et al. (2026) · [A provably efficient classical algorithm for simulating OTOC⁽¹⁾ in 1D](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24640)

- **Supporting lemma**. Computational-basis identities connect the repository’s Pauli expansion to normalized traces against Pauli strings and linear combinations. These support the evaluation in Appendix D, equations (D.7)–(D.8). [dualEntry_trace](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.RealTimePropagation.OutOfTimeOrder/FQChem.Dynamics.OTOC.dualEntry_trace) · [expansion](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.RealTimePropagation.OutOfTimeOrder/FQChem.Dynamics.OTOC.expansion) · [traceCoefficient](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.RealTimePropagation.OutOfTimeOrder/FQChem.Dynamics.OTOC.traceCoefficient) · [traceExpansion](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.RealTimePropagation.OutOfTimeOrder/FQChem.Dynamics.OTOC.traceExpansion)
- **Supporting lemma**. Conjugation by a single-site Z multiplies a Pauli word by −1 when the word carries X or Y there and by +1 otherwise. This identifies the sum in equation (D.8). [zConjugation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.RealTimePropagation.OutOfTimeOrder/FQChem.Dynamics.OTOC.zConjugation)
- **Paper result**. Equation (D.7). For a Hermitian involutory n-qubit butterfly the squared real coefficients in the concrete Pauli basis sum to one. [butterflyMass](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.RealTimePropagation.OutOfTimeOrder/FQChem.Dynamics.OTOC.butterflyMass)
- **Paper result**. Equation (D.8). For a Hermitian involutory evolved butterfly, its normalized first-order OTOC with local Z equals one minus twice the squared Pauli weight of words that anticommute with Z. [firstOrder_eq_anticommutingMass](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.RealTimePropagation.OutOfTimeOrder/FQChem.Dynamics.OTOC.firstOrder_eq_anticommutingMass)
- **Derived result**. Dropping words only from the final sum in (D.8) overestimates the exact OTOC by twice their discarded anticommuting weight. The propagation error of the paper’s window algorithm requires Theorem 1. [discardedWeightError](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.RealTimePropagation.OutOfTimeOrder/FQChem.Dynamics.OTOC.discardedWeightError)
- **Supporting lemma**. The unnormalized trace of (A ⊗ B) times the global swap equals Tr(AB). This contraction identity underlies the two-copy readout. [swapTrace](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.RealTimePropagation.OutOfTimeOrder/FQChem.Dynamics.OTOC.swapTrace)
- **Paper result**. Equation (III.2). The normalized four-point trace equals the normalized two-copy trace of the evolved butterfly tensored with itself, the doubled probe and the global swap. This is the readout used for window propagation. [twoCopyReadout](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.RealTimePropagation.OutOfTimeOrder/FQChem.Dynamics.OTOC.twoCopyReadout)
- **Paper result**. Appendix A, Fact 1. Conjugation by U ⊗ U fixes both identity and swap, here for any finite-dimensional unitary U. These invariant tails are used in the window representation. [invariantTails](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.RealTimePropagation.OutOfTimeOrder/FQChem.Dynamics.OTOC.invariantTails)

## Lieb (1983) · [Density functionals for coulomb systems](https://doi.org/10.1002/qua.560240302)

- **Supporting lemma**. The normalized-trial energy infimum and the potential-dual supremum give the inequalities used to establish the density variational identity. - **Special case**. For normalized representable densities in the supplied form setting, the potential-dual supremum defines a universal extended-real functional and recovers the ground-energy infimum. The identity uses infima. - **Special case**. At the density of an attained normalized ground state the supremum-defined functional equals that state’s internal energy. - **Supporting lemma**. The inverse-distance kernel is locally integrable in three dimensions. Its coordinate lifts give finite nuclear-attraction integrals against marginals of real smooth compactly supported molecular trial states. - **Supporting lemma**. Relabeling antisymmetric particle coordinates preserves the density when applied on both indices. - **Paper result**. The attainable energies of a finite N-fermion Hamiltonian that sums two-body terms are exactly the attainable values of the linear energy functional on representable two-particle marginals. This is the variational identity used in the hardness reduction. [energyRange](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FermionicMarginals/FQChem.Complexity.FermionicMarginals.energyRange)
- **Supporting lemma**. Gershgorin bounds the Hermitian trace norm by dimension squared times the largest entry. Explicit Hermitian two-body observables read the real or imaginary entry. [observableReadout](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.MarginalVerification/FQChem.Complexity.MarginalVerification.observableReadout) · [traceNormBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.MarginalVerification/FQChem.Complexity.MarginalVerification.traceNormBound)
- **Derived result**. For a candidate at trace-norm distance at least β from all representable pair densities, an explicit pair observable distinguishes it from every convex mixture of physical block marginals with signal exceeding β/(4d⁴). [measurableDiscrepancy](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.MarginalVerification/FQChem.Complexity.MarginalVerification.measurableDiscrepancy)
- **Supporting lemma**. The expectation discrepancy of a bounded verifier statistic forces a rejection probability lower bound for arbitrary correlated finite measurement outcomes. [rejectionProbability](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.MarginalVerification/FQChem.Complexity.MarginalVerification.rejectionProbability)
- **Proof in the literature · needs Lean proof**. [Fermionic N-representability](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#paper/liu-n-representability). Main N-representability promise problem, pp. 1–2 · oracle reduction and QMA verifier. For N fermions in d ≤ poly(N) modes, the input is a two-fermion density matrix ρ and β ≥ 1/poly(N) given to polynomially many bits. Decide exact representability by an N-fermion mixed state versus trace-norm distance at least β from every representable marginal. The problem is QMA-complete under polynomial-time Turing reductions.
  - **Remaining Lean proof**. The missing steps include implementing the witness measurements as a uniform verifier and reducing weak optimization to weak membership with polynomially controlled precision.

## Lloyd (1996) · [Universal Quantum Simulators](https://doi.org/10.1126/science.273.5278.1073)

- **Special case**. For two commuting finite complex matrices the product of their exponentials, repeated n times, exactly exponentiates n times their sum. Lloyd also treats noncommuting local terms with approximation error. [commutingProductFormula](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.CommutingTerms/FQChem.Algorithms.commutingProductFormula)

- **Derived result**. The row-slot permutation, XOR lookup, public decoder rotation and swap give a projected unitary encoding of the normalized sparse Hermitian matrix. - **Derived result**. For an exact m-bit normalized entry code, a fixed decoder and d row slots, the simulation at physical time t uses 54(64Q+1) ordinary calls to each of the position and value oracles, with Q evaluated at tdb. Its compressed operator error is at most ε²/2 and its Born success probability is at least 1−ε². The theorem counts oracle calls. [sparseQuerySimulation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseOracle/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.Sparse.sparseQuerySimulation)

One sparse QSP circuit jointly realizes all resource and accuracy guarantees at padded scaled time 2·2^k·b·t, with k address bits, a nonempty system, and p-bit Cartesian entries supplied by ordinary value and position oracles. - **Supporting lemma**. Binary phase words multiply to the phase of the represented integer. Phase gates are unitary and reversible lookup conjugates their diagonal action into the clean scalar signal. [conjugate](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseScalar/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseScalar.conjugate) · [counts](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseScalar/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseScalar.counts) · [oneWire_unit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseScalar/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseScalar.oneWire_unit) · [pulses_eval](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseScalar/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseScalar.pulses_eval) · [rotor_add](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseScalar/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseScalar.rotor_add)
- **Paper result**. Theorem 3 upper resource and success guarantees for a nonempty qubit register with ordinary in-place position and XOR value oracles, any 1≤d≤2^n, and exact centered p-bit Cartesian coordinates in public units b≤2c. Setting c to the supplied positive maximum entry norm gives the unpadded |t|dc cutoff. All workspace starts in computational zero. The two gates to prepare and read the signal flags are included. [sparseCoordinateSimulation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseReadout/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseAssembly.sparseCoordinateSimulation)

- **Supporting lemma**. A power of two strictly above a positive supplied maximum lies within twice that maximum. Every signed lattice value x/2^p with −2^p≤x<2^p has exactly the binary representation used by the physical decoder. [binaryRange](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseReadout/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseAssembly.binaryRange) · [representable](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseReadout/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseAssembly.representable)
- **Special case**. A supplied zero maximum entry norm gives a zero Hamiltonian and exact identity evolution for every simulation time. The empty circuit has zero queries and zero additional gates. [zeroHamiltonian](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseReadout/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseAssembly.zeroHamiltonian)
- **Supporting lemma**. The pinned Hermitian evolution stability inequality bounds the spectral perturbation by absolute time times Hamiltonian operator error. It supplies the last step of the source-rounding estimate. [static_lchs_hamiltonian_evolution_stability](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.LchsSimulation.StaticLchs.StaticNoncommutingLCHS/Qatlib.Concepts.LchsSimulation.StaticLchs.static_lchs_hamiltonian_evolution_stability)
- **Supporting lemma**. The pinned pure-state trace-distance inequality relates half trace norm to the real inner product of two normalized vectors. It converts the successful-output vector estimate into trace distance. [pure_trace_distance_le_re_inner](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.MixedFidelity.FidelityMetric.MixedFidelity/Qatlib.Concepts.MixedFidelity.FidelityMetric.pure_trace_distance_le_re_inner)
- **Supporting lemma**. An arbitrary rectangular matrix acting on a Euclidean vector enlarges its norm by at most the operator norm. It specializes spectral simulation error to each normalized input. [l2_norm_mulVec_fromVec_le](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.ReferenceError.RefTraceError.TensorOperatorNorm/Qatlib.Concepts.ReferenceError.RefTraceError.l2_norm_mulVec_fromVec_le)
- **Supporting lemma**. The most-significant-bit-first thermal binary numeral agrees with the finite qubit basis enumeration, giving exact representability on the signed lattice. [thermalBitValue_ofFn](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.UniformBqp.FiniteGateWord.FiniteThermal.FiniteThermalUniformH/Qatlib.Concepts.UniformBqp.FiniteGateWord.FiniteThermalCircuits.thermalBitValue_ofFn)
- **Supporting lemma**. An invertible reindexing of the finite basis preserves Euclidean operator norm. It transports the row/column inequality to the actual qubit register. [l2_norm_relabelGate](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.BornBridge.RelabelOperatorNorm/Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.BornBridge.l2_norm_relabelGate)
- **Supporting lemma**. The Euclidean inner product of two state vectors agrees with their finite coordinate inner product. [inner_euclidean_fromVec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.BornBridge.RepresentationBridge/Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.BornBridge.inner_euclidean_fromVec)
- **Supporting lemma**. The raw normalized-state predicate is equivalent to Euclidean norm one. [normalized_iff_norm_toEuclidean_fromVec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.BornBridge.RepresentationBridge/Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.BornBridge.normalized_iff_norm_toEuclidean_fromVec)
- **Supporting lemma**. A diagonal matrix with unit-modulus entries is unitary. This verifies the explicit scalar binary phase circuit. [diagonal_mem_unitaryGroup](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.Qubit/Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.diagonal_mem_unitaryGroup)
- **Supporting lemma**. The pinned complex-matrix row/column sum bound gives a spectral norm estimate via Cauchy–Schwarz. Applied to Hermitian sparse rounding errors, both sums are bounded by dη. [spectral_norm_le_rowCol_sums](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Papers.ResolventQPE24.MainResults.SpectralTheory.SourceSpectralBounds/Qatlib.Papers.ResolventQPE24.MainResults.SpectralTheory.spectral_norm_le_rowCol_sums)
- **Derived result**. Distinguishing the two parity classes around any threshold requires N/4 coherent whole-row queries, even when the separation is arbitrarily small. [separatedParity](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries/FQChem.Complexity.SparseParityQueries.separatedParity)
- **Derived result**. At arbitrary time t, the evolved endpoint on the selected parity track has Born probability |sin(t/2)|²ᴺ. An estimator accurate to less than half this signal needs at least N/4 row queries. [endpointProbability](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseSimulationPrecision/FQChem.Complexity.SparseSimulationPrecision.endpointProbability) · [shortTimeLowerBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseSimulationPrecision/FQChem.Complexity.SparseSimulationPrecision.shortTimeLowerBound)
- **Supporting lemma**. The endpoint signal before perfect transfer bounds the factorial cutoff using the exponential series and a uniform lower bound on sine. [cutoffFromSignal](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseSimulationPrecision/FQChem.Complexity.SparseSimulationPrecision.cutoffFromSignal) · [factorialBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseSimulationPrecision/FQChem.Complexity.SparseSimulationPrecision.factorialBound) · [factorialObstruction](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseSimulationPrecision/FQChem.Complexity.SparseSimulationPrecision.factorialObstruction)
- **Derived result**. For coherent access to two incident edge bits per row, any q-query approximation to the evolved endpoint at all scaled times through τ, with absolute probability error ε<1/2, admits a factorial cutoff at most 32q+8. [worstCaseCutoff](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseSimulationPrecision/FQChem.Complexity.SparseSimulationPrecision.worstCaseCutoff)
- **Derived result**. From a d-row-sparse Hermitian H with entry magnitudes at most b, one segmented QSP word simulates physical time τ/(db) for rational τ on every input with relative vector-norm error at most 2⁻ᵏ. Both counts belong to that word and charge its walk oracle and exact matrix gates. Theorem 3 (2017) counts elementary gates and position/entry queries with an optimal truncation schedule. [segmentedSparseEvolution](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.SparseHamiltonianSimulation/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.SparseHamiltonianSimulation.segmentedSparseEvolution)
- **Derived result**. For an existing projected unitary encoding, a segmented, amplified QSP word approximates the time-evolution action on the embedded input. Its exact oracle and fixed-matrix gate counts use its own cutoff and segment parameters. [computed_signal_full_segmented_program_and_cost](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.ComputedSignalConsumers/Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.computed_signal_full_segmented_program_and_cost)

## Low & Chuang (2019) · [Hamiltonian Simulation by Qubitization](https://doi.org/10.22331/q-2019-07-12-163)

- **Supporting lemma**. Physical gate words compose and admit wire reindexing. Three ordinary CNOTs give a SWAP and the appropriate diagonal on the two signal flags is unitary for any real angle. [classicalEmits](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSingleton/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.Singleton.classicalEmits) · [diagonalFlags](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSingleton/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.Singleton.diagonalFlags) · [flagsPhase](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSingleton/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.Singleton.flagsPhase) · [merge](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSingleton/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.Singleton.merge) · [onlyGate](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSingleton/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.Singleton.onlyGate) · [phaseUnit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSingleton/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.Singleton.phaseUnit) · [relax](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSingleton/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.Singleton.relax) · [swapWord](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSingleton/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.Singleton.swapWord)
- **Derived result**. A phase addressed to an arbitrary control and the outer flag is routed with at most seven gates in the existing physical vocabulary. Applied to the entire encoding register and both flags, the circuit implements the cubic amplification coordinate reflection for every system size, including zero system wires. [coordinateMark](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSingleton/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.Singleton.coordinateMark) · [flagWires](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSingleton/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.Singleton.flagWires) · [phasePi](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSingleton/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.Singleton.phasePi) · [physicalPhase](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSingleton/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.Singleton.physicalPhase) · [routedPhase](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSingleton/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.Singleton.routedPhase)
- **Derived result**. Source Theorem 1 compilation after replacing the cubic reflection by the ancilla-free synthesis. There is no lower-system-dimension restriction. Binary padding preserves the block and the evolution; each fixed signal-processing instruction contributes at most 11000(a+4) one-/two-qubit gates for a encoding qubits. [allFiniteRegisters](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangAllDimensions/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.Singleton.allFiniteRegisters) · [allFrameGates](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangAllDimensions/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.Singleton.allFrameGates) · [allQubitRegisters](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangAllDimensions/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.Singleton.allQubitRegisters)
- **Paper result**. Theorem 1 standard-form simulation for arbitrary finite dimensions, including a one-dimensional system. With a=⌈log₂ d⌉, r=⌈log₂ N⌉, and the least positive Q of Eq. (1), the same oracle-independent word uses a+r+2 wires, 54(64Q+1) calls to preparation or its adjoint, 27(64Q+1) calls to the controlled block or its adjoint, and ≤11000(a+4)[27(64Q+8)−2]+2 elementary gates. Its compressed coordinate block has error ≤ε²/2 and success ≥1−ε² for 0<ε<1. The linked parity lower bound shows the worst-case query count is optimal up to a constant factor. - **Supporting lemma**. Factorial and geometric inequalities convert any qualifying positive cutoff Q at tolerance at most one into a degree 16Q Taylor truncation with remainder at most ε²/6750. These constants serve the cubic amplification proof. - **Supporting lemma**. The local construction encodes the sine and cosine Taylor polynomials with common attenuation sin(π/54). Repeated cubic amplification preserves the two signal flags, multiplies the ideal angle by three and increases the compression error by at most 15 per level. - **Derived result**. For a Hermitian contraction given by a same-frame projected unitary encoding, the finite two-flag circuit makes 27(64Q+1) controlled encoding queries at the least Eq. (1) cutoff. It attains spectral compression error ε²/2, clean state-vector error ε and postselection probability at least 1−ε². The fixed-gate ledger counts matrix instructions. - **Derived result**. For every finite standard-form input H=⟨0|G†UG|0⟩ at 0<ε<1, a single oracle-independent word attains spectral error ε²/2 and success probability at least 1−ε². Its two signal flags suffice. The exact ledgers are 54(64Q+1) ordinary G queries and 27(64Q+1) controlled U queries including inverses. The fixed-gate count is at the level of matrix instructions. Its controlled implementation makes exactly two ordinary row-position queries and two ordinary binary-value queries. - **Paper result**. Lemma 6 for exact finite binary encodings. For any positive entry scale b, the two sparse oracles of Eq. (11) yield a clean-coordinate encoding of H/(db) using two queries to each oracle. A row permutation extends each position map beyond its d designated slots. Taking b to be the maximum entry norm gives the published normalization for every nonzero input. [sparseStandardForm](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseOracle/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.Sparse.sparseStandardForm)
- **Derived result**. For an exact m-bit normalized entry code, a fixed decoder and d row slots, the simulation at physical time t uses 54(64Q+1) ordinary calls to each of the position and value oracles, with Q evaluated at tdb. Its compressed operator error is at most ε²/2 and its Born success probability is at least 1−ε². The theorem counts oracle calls. [sparseQuerySimulation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseOracle/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.Sparse.sparseQuerySimulation)
