For any d-dimensional ancilla and any finite logical system of dimension N ≥ 2, including non-powers of two, the padded physical oracle simulation uses ⌈log₂ d⌉ + ⌈log₂ N⌉ + 2 qubits and a counted one- and two-wire gate list. Spectral error and success are proved for the original encoded inputs. - **Supporting lemma**. The computational coordinate frame is isometric. Conjugating the word by the two-flag Hadamard changes its preparation and postselection basis while preserving the compressed matrix and every Born success probability. - **Special case**. Theorem 1 for any finite d ≥ 1 and N ≥ 2. The least Eq. (1) cutoff controls the preparation and block query counts. There are at most 250(⌈log₂ d⌉+4)(27(64Q+8)−2)+2 individually verified one- and two-qubit gates, including flag preparation and computational-basis readout, on the exact ceiling-log register widths. Spectral error is at most ε²/2 and success at least 1−ε². - **Supporting lemma**. The matrix product of two compatible isometries is an isometry. This composes preparation with the coordinate embedding in the padded block encoding. - **Supporting lemma**. Unitary operator norms and compression by isometries bound the norm of the projected signal. - **Supporting lemma**. Embedding each input vector at a fixed computational ancilla value preserves its norm. [encodingFiber_isIsometry](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.BlockEncoding.EncodingCore.CoordinateFiber/Qatlib.Concepts.BlockEncoding.EncodingCore.encodingFiber_isIsometry)
- **Supporting lemma**. A finite normalized nonnegative amplitude table extends to a unitary with that table as its designated column. This is an existence and matrix identity used for uniform slot preparation. - **Supporting lemma**. A selector made of unitary diagonal blocks is unitary. [select_unitary](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.BlockEncoding.LCU/Qatlib.Concepts.BlockEncoding.LCU.select_unitary)
- **Supporting lemma**. The block-encoding compression error supplies a squared-amplitude lower bound on successful postselection of a normalized input. - **Supporting lemma**. Cubic coherent amplification is implemented by an expanded query word with an explicit compressed-matrix identity and resource ledger. - **Supporting lemma**. Cubic signal amplification triples the ideal angle, obeys a perturbation estimate, and converts compressed operator approximation to a clean-frame vector guarantee. - **Supporting lemma**. Parallel direct sums of oracle matrices preserve products, identity, adjoints and unitarity. - **Supporting lemma**. An injective endomorphism of a finite basis gives a unitary permutation matrix. - **Supporting lemma**. Adding a Boolean control preserves products, identity, adjoints and unitarity. - **Supporting lemma**. Compiling the two sine/cosine phase lists yields the intended Hamiltonian circuit, with checked well-formedness and instruction counts. [compileHamiltonian_wellFormed](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.CountedHamiltonian/Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.compileHamiltonian_wellFormed) · [counts_compileHamiltonian](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.CountedHamiltonian/Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.counts_compileHamiltonian) · [evaluate_compileHamiltonian](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.CountedHamiltonian/Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.evaluate_compileHamiltonian)
- **Supporting lemma**. Taylor exponential truncations on a bounded interval have an explicit nonnegative remainder bound that eventually falls below any positive tolerance. - **Supporting lemma**. Hermitian projected encodings implement the real polynomial response, accept a scalar phase and combine the sine/cosine Taylor polynomials to form the complex exponential truncation. [exponentialTaylorPolynomial_eq_trig](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.HamiltonianSimulation/Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.exponentialTaylorPolynomial_eq_trig) · [hamiltonian_phase_projectedEncoding](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.HamiltonianSimulation/Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.hamiltonian_phase_projectedEncoding) · [hamiltonian_realPart_of_completion](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.HamiltonianSimulation/Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.hamiltonian_realPart_of_completion) · [hermitian_realPart_projectedEncoding](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.HamiltonianSimulation/Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.hermitian_realPart_projectedEncoding)
- **Supporting lemma**. Averaging two compatible block encodings preserves isometric frames and yields their mean as the encoded operator. [averageFrame_isIsometry](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.RealPartQSVT/Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.averageFrame_isIsometry) · [average_projectedEncoding](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.RealPartQSVT/Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.average_projectedEncoding)
- **Supporting lemma**. Phase sequences exist for real polynomials of the required parity and degree strictly bounded by one on the signal interval. [exists_phases_of_strict](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.RealPolynomial/Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.exists_phases_of_strict)
- **Supporting lemma**. Finite Taylor sine and cosine polynomials obey degree, parity and uniform magnitude estimates on the signal interval. [cosineTaylorPolynomial_abs_lt_two](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.TrigApproximation/Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.cosineTaylorPolynomial_abs_lt_two) · [cosineTaylorPolynomial_comp_neg_X](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.TrigApproximation/Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.cosineTaylorPolynomial_comp_neg_X) · [cosineTaylorPolynomial_natDegree_le](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.TrigApproximation/Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.cosineTaylorPolynomial_natDegree_le) · [sineTaylorPolynomial_abs_lt_two](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.TrigApproximation/Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.sineTaylorPolynomial_abs_lt_two) · [sineTaylorPolynomial_comp_neg_X](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.TrigApproximation/Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.sineTaylorPolynomial_comp_neg_X) · [sineTaylorPolynomial_natDegree_le](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.TrigApproximation/Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.Achievability.sineTaylorPolynomial_natDegree_le)
- **Supporting lemma**. A scalar polynomial approximation over the spectrum gives the corresponding Hermitian operator-norm error bound. [computed_signal_hermitian_polynomial_error](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.JacobiAnger.ComputedSignalSimulation/Qatlib.Concepts.QspSimulation.JacobiAnger.computed_signal_hermitian_polynomial_error)
- **Supporting lemma**. A single-qubit Hadamard sends the coordinate fiber to the averaged frame up to a verified unit scalar. [averageFrame_labelH](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QsvtCore.LinearSystems.FiniteInversePhaseProgram/Qatlib.Concepts.QsvtCore.LinearSystems.averageFrame_labelH) · [coordinatePhaseFrame_apply](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QsvtCore.LinearSystems.FiniteInversePhaseProgram/Qatlib.Concepts.QsvtCore.LinearSystems.coordinatePhaseFrame_apply) · [inverseLabelPhase_unit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.QsvtCore.LinearSystems.FiniteInversePhaseProgram/Qatlib.Concepts.QsvtCore.LinearSystems.inverseLabelPhase_unit)
- **Supporting lemma**. Unitary Hamiltonian evolution preserves normalization. Hadamard and Pauli X are unitary, and involutive basis permutations are unitary and self-adjoint. [hadamard_unitary](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.Qubit/Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.hadamard_unitary) · [hamiltonianEvolution_unitary](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.Qubit/Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.hamiltonianEvolution_unitary) · [normalized_applyGate](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.Qubit/Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.normalized_applyGate) · [pauliX_unitary](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.Qubit/Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.pauliX_unitary) · [permMatrix_conjTranspose](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.Qubit/Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.permMatrix_conjTranspose) · [permMatrix_unitary](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.Qubit/Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.permMatrix_unitary)
- **Supporting lemma**. Tensor gates multiply componentwise, preserve identities and act unitarily for unitary factors. [tensorGate_mul](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.Tensor/Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.tensorGate_mul) · [tensorGate_one](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.Tensor/Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.tensorGate_one) · [tensorGate_unitary](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.Tensor/Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.tensorGate_unitary)
- **Supporting lemma**. A controlled sparse block uses two ordinary in-place position queries and two ordinary binary XOR queries. Reversible lookup and its inverse run with both signal flags untouched. [body_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseAccess/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseBlock.body_spec)
- **Supporting lemma**. A standard-form block with a reversible value table and a uniform public decoder only reads the diagonal amplitude of that decoder on the clean loaded word. The block matrix element is the product of its two slot amplitudes and this decoder amplitude. [compression](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseBlock/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseBlock.compression)
- **Supporting lemma**. Matrix-column identities and unitarity for the prepared sparse block, the position permutation, the register exchange and the reversible entry lookup. These are local ingredients of the sparse-access construction. [frame_iso](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseBlock/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseBlock.frame_iso) · [left_col](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseBlock/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseBlock.left_col) · [loaded_col](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseBlock/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseBlock.loaded_col) · [oracle_unit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseBlock/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseBlock.oracle_unit) · [permutation_action](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseBlock/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseBlock.permutation_action) · [prep_col](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseBlock/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseBlock.prep_col) · [right_col](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseBlock/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseBlock.right_col)
- **Special case**. Every Hermitian input with nonzero positions covered by k address bits and exact centered Cartesian p-bit entries has an explicit block encoding scaled by 2·2^k. The decoder is a single input-independent circuit with at most 190p+4 gates. [cartesianEncoding](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseCartesian/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseBlock.cartesianEncoding)
- **Supporting lemma**. Factorial block inequalities prove that rescaling simulation time by a constant changes the least factorial cutoff by at most a constant in the joint time-and-precision regime. A fourfold dilation costs at most nine times the original cutoff. [cartesianCutoff](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseCutoff/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseBlock.cartesianCutoff) · [cutoff_dilation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseCutoff/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseBlock.cutoff_dilation) · [factorial_block](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseCutoff/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseBlock.factorial_block) · [factorial_dilation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparseCutoff/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseBlock.factorial_dilation)
- **Supporting lemma**. Regrouping the slot and binary-data registers into the QSP ancilla preserves unitarity, ordinary controlled access and the clean compressed block. Rescaling Hamiltonian and time by reciprocal factors preserves evolution. [packed_compression](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparsePacking/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseAssembly.packed_compression) · [push_lifted](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparsePacking/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseAssembly.push_lifted) · [push_unit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparsePacking/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseAssembly.push_unit) · [scaledEvolution](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparsePacking/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseAssembly.scaledEvolution)
- **Special case**. The actual controlled sparse block uses exactly two position and two entry queries with at most 2k+415440(n+q) additional elementary gates for a q-gate decoder. Its fixed circuit is chosen before the row and value tables. [physicalBody](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuangSparsePhysicalBlock/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.LowChuang.SparseControl.physicalBody)
- **Supporting lemma**. An orthogonal projector gives an involutory reflection. A product of two symmetric involutions preserves the complex inner product, as required by the signal walk. [involution_inner](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.Qubitization/FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.Qubitization.Encoding.involution_inner) · [proj_reflection](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.Qubitization/FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.Qubitization.Encoding.proj_reflection) · [reflection_sq](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.Qubitization/FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.Qubitization.Encoding.reflection_sq) · [walk_preserves_inner](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.Qubitization/FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.Qubitization.Encoding.walk_preserves_inner)
- **Supporting lemma**. The compressed powers of the walk obey a second-order operator recurrence for a symmetric involutory oracle and an orthogonal signal projector. [block_recurrence](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.Qubitization/FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.Qubitization.Encoding.block_recurrence)
- **Special case**. For the Hermitian involution encoding, compression of the nth walk power gives the Chebyshev polynomial of the signal. A signal eigenvector of value λ acquires the projected amplitude Tₙ(λ), or cos(n arccos λ) for a unit eigenvector. This realizes the signal dynamics of Theorem 2. [chebyshev_block](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.Qubitization/FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.Qubitization.Encoding.chebyshev_block) · [eigenphase_cosine](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.Qubitization/FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.Qubitization.Encoding.eigenphase_cosine) · [eigenvector_amplitude](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.Qubitization/FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.Qubitization.Encoding.eigenvector_amplitude)
- **Supporting lemma**. A real eigenvalue of the signal on a normalized encoded eigenvector has absolute value at most one. [eigenvalue_abs_le_one](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.Qubitization/FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.Qubitization.Encoding.eigenvalue_abs_le_one)
- **Derived result**. For a unit encoded eigenvector the state-vector error of a finite weighted sum of projected walks is the scalar approximation error at its eigenvalue. [simulation_error](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.Qubitization/FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.Qubitization.Encoding.simulation_error)
- **Derived result**. A degree-two walk polynomial approximates the real-time phase on a unit encoded eigenvector for |t| ≤ 1. Lean gives the explicit error 2|tλ|³/9 for this polynomial. [quadratic_simulation_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.Qubitization/FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.Qubitization.Encoding.quadratic_simulation_bound)
- **Supporting lemma**. The endpoint signal before perfect transfer bounds the factorial cutoff using the exponential series and a uniform lower bound on sine. [cutoffFromSignal](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseSimulationPrecision/FQChem.Complexity.SparseSimulationPrecision.cutoffFromSignal) · [factorialBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseSimulationPrecision/FQChem.Complexity.SparseSimulationPrecision.factorialBound) · [factorialObstruction](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseSimulationPrecision/FQChem.Complexity.SparseSimulationPrecision.factorialObstruction)
- **Derived result**. For coherent access to two incident edge bits per row, any q-query approximation to the evolved endpoint at all scaled times through τ, with absolute probability error ε<1/2, admits a factorial cutoff at most 32q+8. [worstCaseCutoff](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SparseSimulationPrecision/FQChem.Complexity.SparseSimulationPrecision.worstCaseCutoff)
- **Derived result**. A single phase oracle encodes the mean of N signs. The parity Fourier coefficient of its evolution can be evaluated exactly, while it vanishes in any compressed circuit amplitude with fewer than N queries. [blockEncoding](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.BlockSimulationPrecision/FQChem.Complexity.BlockSimulationPrecision.blockEncoding) · [parityCoefficient](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.BlockSimulationPrecision/FQChem.Complexity.BlockSimulationPrecision.parityCoefficient) · [zeroCoefficient](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.BlockSimulationPrecision/FQChem.Complexity.BlockSimulationPrecision.zeroCoefficient)
- **Derived result**. On these explicitly block-encoded inputs, q controlled phase queries at time budget τ and uniform compressed-amplitude error ε<1 force the least admissible factorial cutoff in Eq. (1) to be at most 8(q+1). The simulator may choose its workspace, input and output frame. [precisionBarrier](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.BlockSimulationPrecision/FQChem.Complexity.BlockSimulationPrecision.precisionBarrier) · [worstCaseBlockCutoff](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.BlockSimulationPrecision/FQChem.Complexity.BlockSimulationPrecision.worstCaseBlockCutoff)
- **Special case**. Lemma 6, Eqs. (11)–(13). Starting with row sparsity d and entry bound b>0, Hermiticity gives column sparsity. The constructed isometry and self-adjoint unitary swap compress to H/(db). The construction is in matrix frames. [sparseSignalEncoding](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.SparseHamiltonianSimulation/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.SparseHamiltonianSimulation.sparseSignalEncoding)
- **Derived result**. From a d-row-sparse Hermitian H with entry magnitudes at most b, one segmented QSP word simulates physical time τ/(db) for rational τ on every input with relative vector-norm error at most 2⁻ᵏ. Both counts belong to that word and charge its walk oracle and exact matrix gates. Theorem 3 (2017) counts elementary gates and position/entry queries with an optimal truncation schedule. [segmentedSparseEvolution](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.SparseHamiltonianSimulation/FQChemQatlib.Dynamics.SparseHamiltonianSimulation.segmentedSparseEvolution)
- **Special case**. Lemma 6, Eqs. (11)–(13), in frames. Row and column sparsity d and entry bound b yield isometries with overlap H/(db), related by a self-adjoint unitary swap. The declaration constructs the encoding mathematically. [lemma6_sparse_standard_form](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Papers.Qubitization19.SparseSim.SourceSparse/Qatlib.Papers.Qubitization19.SparseSim.lemma6_sparse_standard_form)

## Lüders (1951) · [Über die Zustandsänderung durch den Meßprozeß](https://doi.org/10.1002/andp.19504430510)

- **Special case**. Given orthogonal idempotent branch operators, conditioning on a positive-probability projection yields a unit state with probability one of the same outcome on repetition. [projectiveRepeatability](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.MeasurementTheory/FQChem.Properties.MeasurementTheory.projectiveRepeatability)

## MacDonald (1933) · [Successive Approximations by the Rayleigh-Ritz Variation Method](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRev.43.830)

- **Supporting lemma**. Dimension counting gives a nonzero vector with prescribed spectral support. The energy quadratic form on such a vector obeys the minimum and maximum eigenvalue bounds on that support. [spectral_lower](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.SubspaceDiagonalization.Interlacing/FQChem.StatePrep.SubspaceDiagonalization.Interlacing.spectral_lower) · [spectral_upper](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.SubspaceDiagonalization.Interlacing/FQChem.StatePrep.SubspaceDiagonalization.Interlacing.spectral_upper) · [spectral_witness](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.SubspaceDiagonalization.Interlacing/FQChem.StatePrep.SubspaceDiagonalization.Interlacing.spectral_witness)
- **Special case**. For an m-dimensional compression of an n-dimensional Hermitian operator, each compressed eigenvalue lies between the two full eigenvalues with the Poincaré index shifts (Lean orders them decreasingly). [ritzInterlacing](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.SubspaceDiagonalization.Interlacing/FQChem.StatePrep.SubspaceDiagonalization.Interlacing.ritzInterlacing)
- **Supporting lemma**. The energy-weighted bound sums termwise inequalities against a normalized family of weights. [weighted_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.ExcitedStates/FQChem.VariationalMethods.weighted_bound)
- **Special case**. In normalized eigenbasis coefficients, vanishing on all eigenstates below the target energy implies that the mean energy is at least the target. [excitedStateBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.ExcitedStates/FQChem.VariationalMethods.excitedStateBound)

- **Special case**. In a real inner-product space any tangent trial velocity whose residual is orthogonal to the tangent subspace minimizes squared residual over that subspace. [mcLachlanProjection](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.RealTimePropagation.VariationalEvolution/FQChem.Dynamics.mcLachlanProjection)

## McMillan (1965) · [Ground State of Liquid He⁴](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRev.138.A442)

- **Supporting lemma**. Squared amplitudes in the finite orthonormal sampling basis sum to one for a unit trial vector. [sum_weight](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VariationalMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VariationalMonteCarlo.sum_weight)
- **Special case**. The finite-basis Born average of local energy equals the trial expectation, including at zero amplitudes via zero weight. For a normalized Hermitian trial problem its real part bounds the lowest eigenvalue. There are separate real-matrix and complex Hilbert-space formulations. [variationalMonteCarlo](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VariationalMonteCarlo/FQChem.Algorithms.variationalMonteCarlo) · [bornMean](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VariationalMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VariationalMonteCarlo.bornMean) · [variationalBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VariationalMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VariationalMonteCarlo.variationalBound)
- **Special case**. With a symmetric stochastic proposal, the finite Metropolis chain preserves the Born distribution of a normalized trial state by detailed balance. This establishes stationarity. Ergodicity needs additional hypotheses. [bornStationary](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VariationalMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VariationalMonteCarlo.bornStationary) · [detailedBalance](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VariationalMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VariationalMonteCarlo.detailedBalance) · [stationary](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VariationalMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VariationalMonteCarlo.stationary)

Adding the rejection probability gives unit row sums and, for a stochastic proposal, nonnegative entries. - **Special case**. With a symmetric stochastic proposal, the finite Metropolis chain preserves the Born distribution of a normalized trial state by detailed balance. This establishes stationarity. Ergodicity needs additional hypotheses. ## Motta et al. (2020) · [Determining eigenstates and thermal states on a quantum computer using quantum imaginary time evolution](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41567-019-0704-4)

- **Derived result**. At fixed geometry the ONIOM solver error equals the high-level imaginary-time energy excess. A finite gapped model with initial ground overlap gives pointwise convergence and, with an excitation cutoff, an exponential upper bound. [layeredProjectionError](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Multiscale.StitchingSolutions.LayeredQM/FQChem.Multiscale.layeredProjectionError) · [layeredProjectionLimit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Multiscale.StitchingSolutions.LayeredQM/FQChem.Multiscale.layeredProjectionLimit) · [projectedLayeredEnergy_eq](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Multiscale.StitchingSolutions.LayeredQM/FQChem.Multiscale.projectedLayeredEnergy_eq)
- **Supporting lemma**. In a finite eigenbasis with nonzero initial ground overlap, normalization gives explicit Born weights. The infidelity is the excited mass divided by total filtered mass. [bornWeight](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.GroundStateProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.bornWeight) · [excited_nonneg](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.GroundStateProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.excited_nonneg) · [infidelity](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.GroundStateProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.infidelity) · [normalization](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.GroundStateProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.normalization) · [total_pos](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.GroundStateProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.total_pos)
- **Supporting lemma**. A positive lower bound on excitation energies exponentially suppresses the unnormalized excited mass, and the resulting exponential envelope tends to zero. [boundTendstoZero](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.GroundStateProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.boundTendstoZero) · [excitedBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.GroundStateProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.excitedBound)
- **Special case**. For nonnegative imaginary time, initial ground mass greater than zero and a finite gapped spectrum, infidelity is bounded by the initial excited-to-ground mass ratio times exp(−2Δτ), so ground-sector fidelity tends to one. Evolution here uses the exact diagonal filter. [projectionBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.GroundStateProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.projectionBound) · [projectionLimit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.GroundStateProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.projectionLimit)
- **Derived result**. The normalized energy excess lies between zero and the spectral width times the infidelity envelope. Finiteness of the spectrum gives an energy excess tending to zero. [energyBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.GroundStateProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.energyBound) · [energyLimit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.GroundStateProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.energyLimit)

## Möttönen et al. (2005) · [Transformation of quantum states using uniformly controlled rotations](https://arxiv.org/abs/quant-ph/0407010)

- **Supporting lemma**. Each complex SU(2) block preserves the pair norm and can split a nonnegative branch radius into its two specified amplitudes, including a zero branch. [preservesNorm](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.ControlledRotations/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.ControlledRotations.Rotation.preservesNorm) · [preparePair_correct](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.ControlledRotations/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.ControlledRotations.preparePair_correct) · [radius_sq](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.ControlledRotations/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.ControlledRotations.radius_sq)
- **Supporting lemma**. Induction on prefixes proves normalization of all outputs of the specified recursive circuit. [normalized](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.ControlledRotations/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.ControlledRotations.Circuit.normalized)
- **Derived result**. The binary prefix tree has 2ⁿ−1 controlled pair rotations and a separate root phase. This counts blocks before elementary-gate decomposition. [blockCount](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.ControlledRotations/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.ControlledRotations.Circuit.blockCount) · [registerCard](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.ControlledRotations/FQChem.StatePrep.CircuitPreparation.ControlledRotations.registerCard)

Costs are measured in prefix-controlled blocks. ## Nam (2022) · [The ionization problem in quantum mechanics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2206.15393)

- **Derived result**. Assuming atomic energy convexity and an admissible trial spinor in each positive electron sector, strict binding of N+1 electrons implies strict binding of N. The implication is proved with extended-real variational infima. [consecutiveBinding_of_convexity](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.AtomicBinding/FQChem.Properties.AtomicBinding.consecutiveBinding_of_convexity)
- **Literature hypothesis · needs Lean proof**. [Consecutive electron binding in atoms](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#paper/atomic-consecutive-binding). Discussion after Conjecture 3. For a single positive integer-charge nucleus, strict binding of N+1 electrons should imply strict binding of N, for N ≥ 1. Each binding test compares successive many-electron ground energies at the same nuclear charge.
  - [Formal statement in Lean](https://github.com/the-matter-lab/fqchem/blob/6770a38071e5b621be99d9d04edbf8a8e41cbd6c/FQChem/Unproven/AtomicEnergy.lean#L59)

## Nielsen & Chuang (2012) · [Quantum Computation and Quantum Information: 10th Anniversary Edition](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511976667)

- **Supporting lemma**. Qatlib’s 15-gate Clifford+T word acts on three distinct wires as a Toffoli on computational-basis inputs. This is the gate used three times in the coherent readout. [toffoli_legal](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Majority/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Majority.toffoli_legal) · [toffoli_run](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Majority/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Majority.toffoli_run)
- **Derived result**. Composes three Toffolis to XOR the majority of three distinct vote bits into a fourth wire. The legal 45-gate word implements that permutation on basis kets and on arbitrary amplitudes. [word_amplitude](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Majority/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Majority.word_amplitude) · [word_ket](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Majority/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Majority.word_ket) · [word_legal](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Majority/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Majority.word_legal) · [word_length](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Majority/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Majority.word_length)
- **Supporting lemma**. Computes gate actions after an injective relabeling of wires, preserving the spectator amplitude factor. Cases cover a single-wire matrix and CNOT on distinct wires. [one_wire](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Wiring/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Wiring.one_wire) · [renamedGate_action](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Wiring/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Wiring.renamedGate_action) · [two_wires](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Wiring/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Wiring.two_wires) · [wireGate_mulVec_update](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Wiring/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Wiring.wireGate_mulVec_update)
- **Supporting lemma**. Relabeling a legal gate word on an injected register preserves legality and length. Its evolution on a factored extension acts only on the selected register. [onWires_legal](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Wiring/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Wiring.onWires_legal) · [onWires_length](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Wiring/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Wiring.onWires_length) · [onWires_run](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Wiring/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Wiring.onWires_run) · [renamedGate_legal](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.Wiring/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.Wiring.renamedGate_legal)
- **Supporting lemma**. Qatlib’s finite gate parser rejects malformed words. Legal words have unitary gates, zero oracle queries and gate count equal to the list length. [finite_gate_description_rejection_unitarity_and_counts](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.GateVocabulary.FiniteGateDescription/Qatlib.Concepts.GateVocabulary.FiniteGateDescription.finite_gate_description_rejection_unitarity_and_counts)
- **Supporting lemma**. Computes the action and entries of a gate on a selected register wire and proves that the classical update corresponding to a CNOT on distinct wires is involutive. [applyGate_wireGate_ket](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.GateVocabulary.LocalReflectionGates/Qatlib.Concepts.GateVocabulary.LocalReflectionGates.applyGate_wireGate_ket) · [cnotUpdate_involutive](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.GateVocabulary.LocalReflectionGates/Qatlib.Concepts.GateVocabulary.LocalReflectionGates.cnotUpdate_involutive) · [wireGate_apply](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.GateVocabulary.LocalReflectionGates/Qatlib.Concepts.GateVocabulary.LocalReflectionGates.wireGate_apply)
- **Special case**. Qatlib verifies a 15-gate Clifford+T realization of the Toffoli transformation on distinct wires, pointwise on basis kets, with exact resource counts. Compare the standard decomposition in Nielsen & Chuang, Fig. 4.9. [thermalToffoliWord_exact](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.UniformBqp.FiniteGateWord.FiniteThermal.FiniteThermalToffoli/Qatlib.Concepts.UniformBqp.FiniteGateWord.FiniteThermalCircuits.thermalToffoliWord_exact)
- **Supporting lemma**. Multiplication by the matrix of an involution permutes pure-state coordinates accordingly. [applyGate_permMatrix](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.Qubit/Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.applyGate_permMatrix)
- **Supporting lemma**. Qatlib’s finite-state primitives expand the complex inner product, normalize each basis ket and give nonnegative event weights from squared amplitudes. [inner_apply](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.Qubit/Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.inner_apply) · [measureProb_nonneg](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.Qubit/Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.measureProb_nonneg) · [normalized_ket](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.Qubit/Qatlib.Foundations.PureStateMath.normalized_ket)

## O’Gorman et al. (2022) · [Intractability of Electronic Structure in a Fixed Basis](https://doi.org/10.1103/PRXQuantum.3.020322)

- **Supporting lemma**. Appendix C.1.a, Eqs. (C3) and (C6). Completing the square for two translated three-dimensional Gaussians and simplifying the normalization factor. [completeSquare](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisGaussians/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisGaussians.completeSquare) · [normalization](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisGaussians/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisGaussians.normalization)
- **Paper result**. Appendix C.1.a, Eqs. (C3), (C6), (C8), (C9). Exact Lebesgue overlap of normalized three-dimensional positive-exponent primitive Gaussians with arbitrary centers and exponents. The unequal-exponent prefactor is expressed as a product of the two normalization factors and the Gaussian volume. [orbital_normalized](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisGaussians/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisGaussians.orbital_normalized) · [overlap](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisGaussians/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisGaussians.overlap) · [overlap_same](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisGaussians/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisGaussians.overlap_same)
- **Derived result**. Appendix C.1.a, Eqs. (C15)–(C16). Multiplication of equal-exponent Gaussian primitives shifts the density to their midpoint and introduces a distance-dependent exponential. Applying the two factors to the six-dimensional 1/‖r−s‖ integral recovers the exchange and three-center reductions as special cases. [pairDensity](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisGaussians/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisGaussians.pairDensity) · [repulsion_reduction](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisGaussians/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisGaussians.repulsion_reduction)
- **Supporting lemma**. Appendix C.1.a, evaluating Eq. (C14) at coincident centers. Three-dimensional radial integration gives 2π/α for exp(−α‖y‖²)/‖y‖. The fourth moment of one normalized orbital is (α/π)^(3/2). [fourthMoment](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisCoulomb/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisCoulomb.fourthMoment) · [radialKernel](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisCoulomb/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisCoulomb.radialKernel)
- **Paper result**. Eqs. (C14) and (F7). Exact six-dimensional Coulomb repulsion of a normalized Gaussian with itself is √(4α/π). A relative-coordinate transformation, absolute-integrability proof, and radial integration justify changing the order of integration at the Coulomb singularity. [onsite](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisCoulomb/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisCoulomb.onsite)
- **Supporting lemma**. Eqs. (C32)–(C33). On nonnegative ω, the rounded hopping profile √(ω² exp(−ω)) equals ω exp(−ω/2), and it tends to zero at infinity. [profile_decay](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisHopping/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisHopping.profile_decay) · [profile_eq_sqrt](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisHopping/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisHopping.profile_eq_sqrt)
- **Paper result**. Appendix C.4, Eqs. (C59), (C62), (C63), and the coefficient-range argument following Eq. (C69). Every strictly negative target in the stated range is reached by a positive real γ with α γ²≥ω₀. A single positive β and one separation per edge match the scaled onsite and hopping coefficients. Finite-precision encoding and control of other physical coefficients are additional steps. [chooseDistance](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisHopping/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisHopping.chooseDistance) · [matchCoefficients](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisHopping/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisHopping.matchCoefficients)
- **Paper result**. Appendix F, Eqs. (F1)–(F4). In the k-electron sector, an independent k-set gives a zero-energy spin-polarized occupation. Without such a set every occupation has a doubly occupied site or a neighboring occupied pair and costs at least V, assuming U≥V≥0. [independent_sites](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterEnergy/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterEnergy.independent_sites) · [injective_sites](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterEnergy/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterEnergy.injective_sites) · [noInstance](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterEnergy/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterEnergy.noInstance) · [polarized_zero](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterEnergy/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterEnergy.polarized_zero)
- **Paper result**. Section IV, Eq. (14), and Appendix F after Eq. (F2). The coordinate state of every fixed-size spin-orbital occupation is exactly the exterior product of an orthonormal frame, with equality proved in the exterior-power occupation basis. [occupation_slater](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterEnergy/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterEnergy.occupation_slater)
- **Supporting lemma**. Appendix F, Eqs. (F1)–(F3). The expectation of the diagonal spin-orbital Hamiltonian is the probability-weighted sum of onsite and edge costs, including all spin multiplicities on each edge. [diagonal_energy](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterEnergy/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterEnergy.diagonal_energy)
- **Derived result**. Appendix F, Eqs. (F1)–(F5). Sector operator-norm error ε<V/2, after a scalar shift, gives strictly separated thresholds shift+ε and shift+V−ε. A YES instance has a normalized single determinant; a NO instance has the lower bound for all normalized many-electron states. This reduction assumes the stated norm control for its input matrix. [expectationPerturbation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterEnergy/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterEnergy.expectationPerturbation) · [robustSlaterReduction](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterEnergy/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterEnergy.robustSlaterReduction) · [slaterDecisionGap](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterEnergy/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterEnergy.slaterDecisionGap)
- **Supporting lemma**. Appendix C.1 describes separate groups of primitive centers for the graph edges. This construction uses an integer line embedded in three-space, a distinct block for each unordered partner pair, and one point for each ordered pair. [lineDistance](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisGeometry/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisGeometry.lineDistance) · [offset_le](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisGeometry/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisGeometry.offset_le) · [partnerDistance](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisGeometry/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisGeometry.partnerDistance) · [sameBlock](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisGeometry/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisGeometry.sameBlock) · [separateBlocks](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisGeometry/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisGeometry.separateBlocks)
- **Derived result**. Explicit graph embedding for an alternative compact-support realization of the Appendix F independent-set reduction. The n² centers lie on an integer line with coordinates below 3Rn²+R. For R≥4, all distinct centers are at least 4 apart, designated edge centers are exactly 4 apart, and every cross-site component pair of different nonadjacent sites is at least R apart. - **Supporting lemma**. Finite-dimensional estimates relate the entrywise orbital and Gram checks to the Hilbert-space norm and operator norm. The orbital-frame columns satisfy the orthonormality condition of Eq. (14) exactly when their Gram matrix is the identity. [entryNorm](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate.entryNorm) · [entry_of_unit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate.entry_of_unit) · [gram_iff](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate.gram_iff) · [matrixNorm](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate.matrixNorm) · [sumBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate.sumBound) · [vectorBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate.vectorBound)
- **Derived result**. For two frames with entry moduli bounded by two and one- and two-body integral moduli bounded by M, an entrywise perturbation ε changes the complex score by at most (4d²Mk + 64d⁴Mk²)ε. Here d counts modes and k counts occupied orbitals. [constant_cast](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate.constant_cast) · [productError](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate.productError) · [score_error](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate.score_error)
- **Derived result**. Löwdin symmetric orthonormalization corrects an approximately orthonormal complex d-by-k orbital matrix to an exact occupied frame. If every input entry has modulus at most two and each Gram residual is at most z with k²z ≤ 1/2, the change per entry is at most 4k³z. [corrected](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate.corrected)
- **Derived result**. For the certificate in §IV A, rounding each real and imaginary component on the 1/D grid takes two signed integers per orbital coefficient. It introduces at most 2/D entrywise error and at most 8d/D error per Gram entry. [digits_norm](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate.digits_norm) · [finiteWitness](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate.finiteWitness) · [floor_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate.floor_bound) · [floor_error](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate.floor_error) · [gram_error](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate.gram_error)
- **Derived result**. For bounded integrals and a positive inverse promise gap, rounding a low-energy determinant supplies a passing finite grid certificate. Every accepted certificate yields an exactly normalized Slater determinant below the NO threshold after Gram correction. This proves the numerical witness implications used in the membership part of Theorem 3. [completeness](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate.completeness) · [soundness](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate.soundness)
- **Supporting lemma**. Exact integer scaling recovers each decoded orbital, its one-particle density, and its Gram residual. Comparing a Gaussian integer’s squared norm with (8dD)² implements the Gram test in §IV A’s finite witness model. [decode_raw](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterIntegerVerifier/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterIntegerVerifier.decode_raw) · [gram_test](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterIntegerVerifier/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterIntegerVerifier.gram_test) · [proj_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterIntegerVerifier/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterIntegerVerifier.proj_spec) · [residual_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterIntegerVerifier/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterIntegerVerifier.residual_spec)
- **Supporting lemma**. With integral numerators over a common positive denominator C and orbital coordinates on a 1/D grid, the exact integer score has denominator CD⁴. The Boolean acceptance test agrees with the real Gram, component, and energy conditions. [accept_iff](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterIntegerVerifier/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterIntegerVerifier.accept_iff) · [energy_test](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterIntegerVerifier/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterIntegerVerifier.energy_test) · [score_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterIntegerVerifier/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterIntegerVerifier.score_spec)
- **Derived result**. Two offset-encoded bit words represent each occupied orbital coefficient. If d, k, the magnitude bound M, and the inverse gap t are each at most 2ᵇ, every word has at most 13 + 12b bits. This quantifies §IV A’s polynomial-length Slater witness. [pack](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterIntegerVerifier/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterIntegerVerifier.pack) · [witnessWidth](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterIntegerVerifier/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterIntegerVerifier.witnessWidth)
- **Derived result**. The finite-bit Boolean verifier accepts some witness in every YES instance and rejects every witness in every NO instance for general complex one- and two-body integrals with Gaussian integer numerators over one positive denominator. The promise concerns the exact expectation on orthonormal exterior determinants. [verifies](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterIntegerVerifier/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterIntegerVerifier.verifies)
- **Derived result**. An entrywise coefficient error of ε in the one- and two-body terms changes any normalized determinant’s expectation by at most (d²k + 2d⁴k²)ε. This transfers the precision of the coefficients in Eqs. (6)–(8) to the energy decision. [integralError](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterIntegralPrecision/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterIntegralPrecision.integralError) · [sumBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterIntegralPrecision/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterIntegralPrecision.sumBound)
- **Derived result**. For physical coefficients approximated by the verifier’s rational input within a quarter of the promised energy gap after error propagation, the verifier preserves both decisions. It doubles the inverse-gap parameter and shifts the rational acceptance threshold. The input supplies certified coefficient approximations, as motivated by §II A’s efficient-integral assumption. [verifiesApproximations](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterIntegralPrecision/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterIntegralPrecision.verifiesApproximations)
- **Supporting lemma**. A Gaussian integer’s component magnitudes obey sum and product bounds. Each operand throughout an arithmetic expression with s input and operation nodes has at most bs magnitude bits when its inputs satisfy the b-bit bound. [binaryCap](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.binaryCap) · [mag_add](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.mag_add) · [mag_mul](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.mag_mul) · [mag_sub](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.mag_sub) · [operandWidth](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.operandWidth) · [smallMono](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.smallMono) · [smallValue](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.smallValue)
- **Supporting lemma**. Recursive finite sums compile the density, Gram residual, and full direct-minus-exchange score into division-free Gaussian integer arithmetic. Expanding all sums gives polynomial operation bounds in d and k, including recomputation of density entries. [density_eval](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.density_eval) · [density_work](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.density_work) · [gram_eval](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.gram_eval) · [gram_work](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.gram_work) · [score_eval](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.score_eval) · [score_work](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.score_work) · [total_eval](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.total_eval) · [total_work](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.total_work)
- **Supporting lemma**. The compiled integer comparisons implement the component, Gram, and threshold tests of the executable verifier. Cross-multiplication uses the positive grid and common denominators. Boolean conjunction gives exactly the same answer. [digit_check](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.digit_check) · [energy_check](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.energy_check) · [gram_check](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.gram_check) · [run_eq](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.run_eq) · [scalar_square](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.scalar_square)
- **Derived result**. The entire family of 2dk + k² + 1 comparisons uses a polynomial number of input reads, Gaussian integer arithmetic operations, and scalar comparisons. Both the bound and the expression evaluator are explicit. This count begins with the integer input words and precomputed grid and Lipschitz parameters. [verifierWork](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.verifierWork)
- **Derived result**. For a b-bit bound on the encoded inputs and two precomputed integer parameters, every signed Gaussian-integer component in every comparison has polynomially many magnitude bits. This includes all intermediate products. The verified work model counts bounded-width arithmetic operations. [allInputs](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.allInputs) · [cmpSmall](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.cmpSmall) · [density_inputs](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.density_inputs) · [sum_inputs](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.sum_inputs) · [verifierWidth](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.verifierWidth) · [within](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterVerifierWork.within)
- **Proof in the literature · needs Lean proof**. [Fixed-basis electronic energy is QMA-complete](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#paper/ogorman-fixed-basis-energy). Theorem 1 · fixed-basis electronic structure problem in §II and hardness proof in Appendix C. Given electron number η, a concisely specified single-particle basis and external potential with efficiently computable integrals, decide E₀ ≤ a versus E₀ ≥ b on the η-electron sector for b−a ≥ 1/poly(η). This promise problem is QMA-complete. The hardness construction uses V = 0 and physical kinetic and Coulomb integrals.
  - **Remaining Lean proof**. The global Löwdin transformation and perturbation estimates for one- and two-electron integrals are checked. The full theorem still requires the geometry-specific Gaussian tail bounds, the spin-to-Hubbard hardness reductions with their promise gaps, and a finite-bit QMA verification procedure.
- **Proof in the literature · needs Lean proof**. [Lowest Slater energy is NP-complete](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#paper/ogorman-slater-energy). Theorem 3 · §IV A and Appendix F, Eqs. (F1)–(F5). For the same fixed-basis electronic Hamiltonians and electron-number sector, decide whether some single Slater determinant has expected energy ≤ a or every such determinant has energy ≥ b, at inverse-polynomial promise gap. This problem is NP-complete.
  - **Remaining Lean proof**. The physical 3-SAT energy gap and the finite-precision determinant certificate are checked. The verifier has polynomial witness size, integer operation count and intermediate bit width. Full NP-completeness still requires uniformly encoding and costing the reduction on bit strings, decoding the promised integral description, and compiling the verifier arithmetic and comparisons into the same machine model.
  - [Cook–Levin in Lean · NP-completeness of encoded 3-SAT](https://github.com/starpos/lean-np-complete/blob/10ebb6683345d8d4596e864f8592942dd65f882e/NPComplete/CookLevin/NPComplete.lean#L79)

## Park & Light (1986) · [Unitary quantum time evolution by iterative Lanczos reduction](https://doi.org/10.1063/1.451548)

- **Supporting lemma**. An exact matrix intertwiner HQ=QK propagates to all powers HᵏQ=QKᵏ. [krylovExact](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.RealTimePropagation.KrylovPropagation/FQChem.Dynamics.krylovExact)
- **Special case**. If QQ† fixes every Krylov vector through degree d, lifting the dth power of Q†HQ reproduces Hᵈv. The paper uses the projected operator for time propagation. [krylovMoment](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.RealTimePropagation.KrylovPropagation/FQChem.Dynamics.krylovMoment)

## Per-Olov Löwdin (1950) · [On the Non-Orthogonality Problem Connected with the Use of Atomic Wave Functions in the Theory of Molecules and Crystals](https://doi.org/10.1063/1.1747632)

- **Supporting lemma**. The spectral theorem, a reciprocal-square-root estimate and a matrix-product bound control the change of coefficients. [sandwichError](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisOrthonormalization/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisOrthonormalization.sandwichError) · [spectralDistance](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisOrthonormalization/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisOrthonormalization.spectralDistance) · [squareRootBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisOrthonormalization/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisOrthonormalization.squareRootBound)
- **Paper result**. Symmetric orthogonalization with the inverse square root of a positive overlap near the identity gives an exactly orthonormal orbital frame. [orthonormalColumns](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisOrthonormalization/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisOrthonormalization.orthonormalColumns)
- **Derived result**. The full overlap matrix determines the transformation. For spectral overlap error δ at most one half, the one- and two-electron integral perturbations cost at most (1+2δ)²−1 and (1+2δ)⁴−1 times their respective spectral norms. [globalTransform](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisOrthonormalization/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisOrthonormalization.globalTransform) · [oneBodyError](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisOrthonormalization/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisOrthonormalization.oneBodyError) · [pairTransform](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisOrthonormalization/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisOrthonormalization.pairTransform) · [twoBodyError](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisOrthonormalization/FQChem.Complexity.FixedBasisOrthonormalization.twoBodyError)
- **Derived result**. Löwdin symmetric orthonormalization corrects an approximately orthonormal complex d-by-k orbital matrix to an exact occupied frame. If every input entry has modulus at most two and each Gram residual is at most z with k²z ≤ 1/2, the change per entry is at most 4k³z. [corrected](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterCertificate.corrected)

## Peruzzo et al. (2014) · [A variational eigenvalue solver on a photonic quantum processor](https://doi.org/10.1038/ncomms5213)

- **Special case**. Rayleigh–Ritz applied to the encoded fermionic operator gives an upper bound on ground energy for each isometrically prepared normalized trial state. [variationalUpperBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.ElectronicStructureOperators/FQChem.Complexity.ElectronicStructure.variationalUpperBound)
- **Derived result**. A normalized trial within ε of the variational optimum, readout error ≤ δ, sufficient pair penalty and ε+2δ < b−a yield a threshold test for the spin energy promise. [variationalThreshold](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.ElectronicStructureOperators/FQChem.Complexity.ElectronicStructure.variationalThreshold)
- **Special case**. A real-coefficient finite Pauli decomposition reconstructs the Hamiltonian expectation as the coefficient-weighted sum of measured term expectations. [energyReconstruction](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.VQEMeasurement/FQChem.VQEMeasurement.energyReconstruction)
- **Derived result**. For equally many calibrated ±1 shots per term with pairwise independence, Lean proves unbiasedness and bounds the energy-estimator variance by Σcᵢ²/n. The statistics lemma also gives the exact variance. [finiteShotVQE](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.VQEMeasurement/FQChem.VQEMeasurement.finiteShotVQE) · [shotStatistics](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.VQEMeasurement/FQChem.VQEMeasurement.shotStatistics)
- **Special case**. A unitary ansatz acting on a normalized reference gives a variational upper bound. The parameterized energies attain the variational ground value if the ansatz reaches a ground eigenvector. [reachableVQEMinimum](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VQE/FQChem.VariationalMethods.reachableVQEMinimum) · [vqe](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VQE/FQChem.VariationalMethods.vqe)

## Pfau et al. (2020) · [Ab initio solution of the many-electron Schrödinger equation with deep neural networks](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevResearch.2.033429)

- **Special case**. A weighted sum of determinants built from row-equivariant orbital matrices is antisymmetric under particle permutations. This proves the symmetry property of the ansatz under the stated equivariance. [neuralAntisymmetry](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.FermionicAnsatz/FQChem.VariationalMethods.neuralAntisymmetry)

## Pichler et al. (2018) · [Quantum Optimization for Maximum Independent Set Using Rydberg Atom Arrays](https://arxiv.org/abs/1808.10816)

- **Special case**. For finite classical configurations with unit reward per occupied vertex and uniform edge penalty greater than the vertex count, the energy minimizers are exactly the maximum independent sets. Physical geometry and preparation time are outside this energy model. [rydbergBlockade](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.AdiabaticPreparation.RydbergBlockade/FQChem.Algorithms.rydbergBlockade)

## Ramakrishnan et al. (2015) · [Big Data Meets Quantum Chemistry Approximations: The Δ-Machine Learning Approach](https://doi.org/10.1021/acs.jctc.5b00099)

- **Derived result**. Given error bounds on learned high-minus-low energy corrections at two configurations, the gap error is at most the sum of the endpoint bounds. [deltaLearningGap](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Multiscale.ChoosingLayers.LearnedEnvironments/FQChem.Multiscale.deltaLearningGap)

## Richard & Herbert (2012) · [A generalized many-body expansion and a unified view of fragment-based methods in electronic structure theory](https://doi.org/10.1063/1.4742816)

- **Derived result**. For finite supported interaction terms, regional inclusion–exclusion plus terms crossing the two regions reconstructs the union energy. This is an accounting identity for a specified decomposition. [overlapAccounting](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Multiscale.ChoosingLayers.Partitions/FQChem.Multiscale.Partitions.overlapAccounting)

## Rigol, Bryant & Singh (2006) · [Numerical Linked-Cluster Approach to Quantum Lattice Models](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevLett.97.187202)

- **Special case**. When clusters are labeled finite subsets and a weight is the observable minus all proper subcluster weights, Boolean-lattice Möbius inversion recovers that weight. This models the linked-cluster subtraction step. [linkedClusterInversion](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.RealTimePropagation.ClusterExpansions/FQChem.Algorithms.linkedClusterInversion)

## Ritz (1909) · [Über eine neue Methode zur Lösung gewisser Variationsprobleme der mathematischen Physik](https://doi.org/10.1515/crll.1909.135.1)

- **Special case**. Rayleigh–Ritz applied to the encoded fermionic operator gives an upper bound on ground energy for each isometrically prepared normalized trial state. [variationalUpperBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.ElectronicStructureOperators/FQChem.Complexity.ElectronicStructure.variationalUpperBound)
- **Supporting lemma**. Applies the variational lower bound for the encoded fermionic operator to a normalized occupation-state trial. [ground_le_trial](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemKitaev.ElectronicStructure/FQChem.Complexity.Kitaev.ElectronicStructure.ground_le_trial)
- **Derived result**. A bounded Hamiltonian and nested spans of determinants give nonincreasing Ritz energy whenever the smaller span contains a nonzero trial determinant. [selectedCI](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.SubspaceDiagonalization.ConfigurationInteraction/FQChem.VariationalMethods.selectedCI)
- **Derived result**. The Ritz infimum in a sampled span is exact if the span contains a nonzero vector attaining the full-space infimum. Sampling that span with adequate probability is an additional algorithmic question. [sampledDiagonalization](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.SubspaceDiagonalization.ConfigurationInteraction/FQChem.VariationalMethods.sampledDiagonalization)
- **Supporting lemma**. The nested spans of finite prefixes of an arbitrary nonzero starting vector’s orbit give nonincreasing Ritz infima. [orbit_step](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.SubspaceDiagonalization.Krylov/FQChem.VariationalMethods.orbit_step)
- **Derived result**. Enlarging the Hamiltonian-power Krylov space of a nonzero vector by one step cannot raise its Ritz infimum. [classicalKrylov](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.SubspaceDiagonalization.Krylov/FQChem.VariationalMethods.classicalKrylov)
- **Derived result**. Enlarging the orbit space of a nonzero vector under any unitary step cannot raise the Hamiltonian Ritz infimum. The statement assumes exact subspaces and overlaps. [quantumKrylov](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.SubspaceDiagonalization.Krylov/FQChem.VariationalMethods.quantumKrylov)
- **Special case**. For a bounded operator and an orthogonally projectable subspace, a nonzero trial vector solves the compressed eigenproblem exactly when its full residual is orthogonal to the trial subspace. [residualCriterion](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.SubspaceDiagonalization.RitzGalerkin/FQChem.StatePrep.SubspaceDiagonalization.residualCriterion)
- **Derived result**. A nonzero finite-basis Slater determinant is antisymmetric, so its Rayleigh quotient bounds the infimum over the fermion sector. The proof concerns the trial-state upper bound used in Hartree–Fock. [slaterTrialBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.FermionicAnsatz/FQChem.VariationalMethods.slaterTrialBound)
- **Supporting lemma**. Boundedness of a continuous operator bounds its real Rayleigh quotients below. The full-space and restricted infima therefore bound each admissible nonzero trial quotient. [groundEnergy_le](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.RayleighRitz/FQChem.RayleighRitz.groundEnergy_le) · [rayleigh_bddBelow](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.RayleighRitz/FQChem.RayleighRitz.rayleigh_bddBelow) · [ritzEnergy_le](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.RayleighRitz/FQChem.RayleighRitz.ritzEnergy_le)
- **Special case**. For a self-adjoint operator on a nontrivial finite-dimensional complex Hilbert space, the Rayleigh-quotient infimum is an eigenvalue and bounds every nonzero trial quotient. [groundEnergyPrinciple](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.RayleighRitz/FQChem.RayleighRitz.groundEnergyPrinciple)
- **Special case**. Enlarging a nonzero trial subspace decreases its Ritz infimum, which still bounds the full-space infimum from above. [basisEnlargement](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.RayleighRitz/FQChem.RayleighRitz.basisEnlargement)
- **Special case**. A unitary ansatz acting on a normalized reference gives a variational upper bound. The parameterized energies attain the variational ground value if the ansatz reaches a ground eigenvector. [reachableVQEMinimum](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VQE/FQChem.VariationalMethods.reachableVQEMinimum) · [vqe](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VQE/FQChem.VariationalMethods.vqe)
- **Special case**. The finite-basis Born average of local energy equals the trial expectation, including at zero amplitudes via zero weight. For a normalized Hermitian trial problem its real part bounds the lowest eigenvalue. There are separate real-matrix and complex Hilbert-space formulations. [variationalMonteCarlo](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VariationalMonteCarlo/FQChem.Algorithms.variationalMonteCarlo) · [bornMean](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VariationalMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VariationalMonteCarlo.bornMean) · [variationalBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VariationalMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VariationalMonteCarlo.variationalBound)

## Robledo-Moreno et al. (2025) · [Chemistry beyond the scale of exact diagonalization on a quantum-centric supercomputer](https://doi.org/10.1126/sciadv.adu9991)

- **Derived result**. The Ritz infimum in a sampled span is exact if the span contains a nonzero vector attaining the full-space infimum. Sampling that span with adequate probability is an additional algorithmic question. [sampledDiagonalization](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.SubspaceDiagonalization.ConfigurationInteraction/FQChem.VariationalMethods.sampledDiagonalization)

## Schrödinger (1926) · [Quantisierung als Eigenwertproblem](https://doi.org/10.1002/andp.19263851302)

- **Supporting lemma**. A simple target level gives nonzero energy denominators. Hermiticity turns the contraction of the first-order response into the real quadratic shift. [gap_ne_zero](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.PerturbationTheory.RayleighSchrodinger/FQChem.StatePrep.PerturbationTheory.gap_ne_zero) · [shift_sum](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.PerturbationTheory.RayleighSchrodinger/FQChem.StatePrep.PerturbationTheory.shift_sum)
- **Special case**. In a finite unperturbed eigenbasis with simple target and Hermitian perturbation, the coefficient equations through degree two admit a solution, and fix the first-order state and first two energy corrections in intermediate normalization. [nondegenerateExpansion](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.PerturbationTheory.RayleighSchrodinger/FQChem.StatePrep.PerturbationTheory.nondegenerateExpansion)
- **Derived result**. The second-order truncated state and energy from coefficient matching obey an exact polynomial residual identity with factor t³ for the finite matrix family. This controls the quasimode residual. [residualIdentity_of_coefficients](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.PerturbationTheory.RayleighSchrodinger/FQChem.StatePrep.PerturbationTheory.residualIdentity_of_coefficients) · [secondOrderQuasimode](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.PerturbationTheory.RayleighSchrodinger/FQChem.StatePrep.PerturbationTheory.secondOrderQuasimode)

## Shor (1997) · [Polynomial-Time Algorithms for Prime Factorization and Discrete Logarithms on a Quantum Computer](https://doi.org/10.1137/S0097539795293172)

- **Special case**. Shor, §5, Eqs. (5.12)–(5.13). With clock 2ᵗ ≥ N², reduced d/r with 0 ≤ d < r < N, and |rc−d2ᵗ| ≤ r/2, the bounded scan returns (d,r) using at most N(N−1)/2 comparisons. This implements the unique-fraction step with a scan. The paper uses a continued fraction to obtain polynomial bit cost. [shorWindowRecovery](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Complexity.QueryAlgorithms/FQChem.Complexity.QueryAlgorithms.shorWindowRecovery)
- **Special case**. Shor, §5, Eqs. (5.12)–(5.13). With clock 2ᵗ ≥ N², reduced d/r with 0 ≤ d < r < N, and |rc−d2ᵗ| ≤ r/2, the bounded scan returns (d,r) using at most N(N−1)/2 comparisons. This implements the unique-fraction step with a scan. The paper uses a continued fraction to obtain polynomial bit cost. [source_clock_residue_window_actual_decoder_recovery](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Papers.ShorFactoring97.OrderRecovery.SourceWindowDecode/Qatlib.Papers.ShorFactoring97.OrderRecovery.source_clock_residue_window_actual_decoder_recovery)
- **Derived result**. A two-readout variant of Shor’s order recovery. For a unit a mod N of order r > 1 and clock M > 2N², the least-common-multiple decoder recovers r from two independent modular-order phase readouts with probability at least (8/π²)²/4. The paper’s single-readout coprimality estimate has a different success bound. [shorTwoSampleOrder](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Complexity.QueryAlgorithms/FQChem.Complexity.QueryAlgorithms.shorTwoSampleOrder)
- **Derived result**. A two-readout variant of Shor’s order recovery. For a unit a mod N of order r > 1 and clock M > 2N², the least-common-multiple decoder recovers r from two independent modular-order phase readouts with probability at least (8/π²)²/4. The paper’s single-readout coprimality estimate has a different success bound. [pair_lcm_order_actual_fresh_success_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.PeriodSampling.OrderRecovery.PairLCMOrderRecovery/Qatlib.Concepts.PeriodSampling.OrderRecovery.pair_lcm_order_actual_fresh_success_bound)
- **Derived result**. For composite N ≥ 3, N ≤ 2ᵏ and clock M > 2N², the modeled residue-sampling program returns only nontrivial divisors. Its success after R rounds is at least 1−(1−p)ᴿ for Qatlib’s explicit single-attempt lower bound p. This program uses two independent phase readouts; its statement establishes success and soundness. [shorFactoringSuccess](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Complexity.QueryAlgorithms/FQChem.Complexity.QueryAlgorithms.shorFactoringSuccess)
- **Derived result**. For composite N ≥ 3, N ≤ 2ᵏ and clock M > 2N², the modeled residue-sampling program returns only nontrivial divisors. Its success after R rounds is at least 1−(1−p)ᴿ for Qatlib’s explicit single-attempt lower bound p. This program uses two independent phase readouts; its statement establishes success and soundness. [residueFactoringAlgorithm](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.PeriodSampling.FactorAlgorithm.ResidueFactoringAlgorithmShor/Qatlib.Concepts.PeriodSampling.FactorAlgorithm.residueFactoringAlgorithm)
- **Supporting lemma**. Classical certificate check used after a trial: an optional candidate is accepted exactly when it is an integer d with 1 < d < m and d divides m. [certify_isSome_iff](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion.certify_isSome_iff) · [certify_some](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion.certify_some)
- **Supporting lemma**. A checked proper divisor and its exact cofactor multiply to the current input, and both recursive arguments are strictly smaller and at least two. [pair](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion.Divisor.pair)
- **Derived result**. Independent repetitions of Shor’s factor attempt. A row misses exactly when every checked proposal fails; its probability under the explicit product of one-shot laws is the one-shot miss probability raised to the number of draws. [first_none](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion.first_none) · [row_failure](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion.row_failure)
- **Derived result**. The recursively produced factors multiply to N on every tape, even after unsuccessful trials. For N ≥ 2 all leaves are at least two, and the binary splitting tree visits exactly twice its leaf count minus one nodes. [factor_tree](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion.factor_tree) · [product_always](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion.product_always)
- **Derived result**. Counting multiplicities, the splitting tree visits at most 2 floor(log₂ N) nodes, including the leaves. Allowing a full batch at each visited node bounds total charged shots by 256 floor(log₂ N)(floor(log₂ N)+1)(floor(log₂ N)+3). [trial_budget](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion.trial_budget) · [two_pow_length](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion.two_pow_length) · [visit_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion.visit_bound)
- **Supporting lemma**. Prime leaves and repeated factors are handled by the same recursion.
