If every candidate composite that could be reached has a successful row, all terminal labels are prime; no primality test is run. [prime_leaves](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion.prime_leaves) · [prime_stops](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion.prime_stops)
- **Supporting lemma**. Amplification constants for the recursive factoring reduction. D shots with individual success ≥ 1/D cut miss probability to at most 1/2. Repeating this block floor(log₂ N)+3 times bounds the union of failures over at most N+1 possible moduli by 1/4. [aggregate_failure](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion.aggregate_failure) · [block_failure](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion.block_failure)
- **Derived result**. Bounded-error completion of Shor’s Section 5 splitting reduction. For N ≥ 1, each composite m ≤ N has a certified single-shot factor probability ≥ 1/[128(floor(log₂ N)+1)]. The adaptive routine returns a product equal to N always, and a list of primes with multiplicities with probability ≥ 2/3. Its cubic-in-bitlength trial bound holds on every run; exhausted batches leave m unsplit. [recursiveFactorization](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringRecursion.recursiveFactorization)
- **Supporting lemma**. Sections 3 and 5 use modular exponentiation. The executable binary run agrees with natural powering modulo N and performs at most two modular multiplications per exponent bit. [modPower_multiplications](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction.modPower_multiplications) · [modPower_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction.modPower_spec)
- **Supporting lemma**. For the Section 5 classical reduction, every reported divisor is proper, even when the order candidate is incorrect. A positive nonunit below N yields its gcd with N before order finding. [extractFactor_nonunit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction.extractFactor_nonunit) · [extractFactor_sound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction.extractFactor_sound)
- **Supporting lemma**. Section 5 compares the 2-adic valuations of component orders. An unsuccessful unit projects to an element with the full global order valuation under any residue-ring homomorphism to a modulus larger than two. [bad_projection](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction.bad_projection) · [odd_quotient](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction.odd_quotient)
- **Supporting lemma**. In a finite cyclic group of even size, multiplication by a generator changes the 2-adic valuation of the order. This supplies the local half bound for the Section 5 CRT argument. [generator_shift](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction.generator_shift)
- **Supporting lemma**. Counting injection for the Section 5 CRT proof. Once one coordinate of a k-fold product is fixed, a generator shift that changes labels independently in every other coordinate bounds agreement by a fraction 2^(1−k). [constant_label_count](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction.constant_label_count)
- **Paper result**. Section 5, before Eq. (5.1). Among uniformly random units modulo every odd N > 1 with k distinct prime factors, an even order whose half-power differs from −1 occurs with probability at least 1−2^(1−k). Lean constructs the prime-power CRT decomposition from N. [badUnitCount](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction.badUnitCount) · [goodUnitFraction](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction.goodUnitFraction)
- **Paper result**. Section 5, classical reduction. A good unit and its true order yield a proper divisor using the single minus-sign gcd of the modular half-power. [extractFactor_of_good](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction.extractFactor_of_good)
- **Derived result**. With t independent uniformly random units and exact orders, at most a fraction 2^(−(k−1)t) of the sample tuples produce no factor. This augments the single-trial bound before Eq. (5.1). [failedRetryCount](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction.failedRetryCount)
- **Paper result**. Section 5, random-base reduction. Uniform sampling from all 1,…,N−1 has failure fraction at most 2^(1−k), since every nonunit gives a factor immediately and the candidate is the exact order on units. [randomBaseFailure](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction.randomBaseFailure)
- **Supporting lemma**. For the optional classical perfect-power step in Section 5, binary search makes exactly one power comparison per root bit and recovers any exact integer root in its initial interval. [rootBits_exact](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringPreprocessing/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction.rootBits_exact) · [rootBits_tests](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringPreprocessing/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction.rootBits_tests)
- **Supporting lemma**. The implementation checks candidate exponents in descending order. Its power scan returns only proper divisors, finds every exact power with exponent in its search range, and counts the worst-case number of comparisons. [scanPowers_complete](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringPreprocessing/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction.scanPowers_complete) · [scanPowers_sound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringPreprocessing/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction.scanPowers_sound) · [scanPowers_tests](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringPreprocessing/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction.scanPowers_tests)
- **Derived result**. Implements the Section 5 classical treatment of easy inputs. The routine returns a proper factor of every even N > 2 and every perfect power a^e with a,e ≥ 2. [preprocess_even](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringPreprocessing/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction.preprocess_even) · [preprocess_power](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringPreprocessing/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction.preprocess_power) · [preprocess_sound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringPreprocessing/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction.preprocess_sound)
- **Derived result**. The Section 5 preprocessing implementation uses at most (L−2)(L+1) power comparisons at positive input width L, with natural subtraction. This is an arithmetic operation count. [preprocess_tests](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringPreprocessing/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction.preprocess_tests)
- **Supporting lemma**. If a composite number above one survives classical preprocessing, it is odd and has at least two distinct prime factors. These are the hypotheses of Shor’s order-to-factoring reduction. [preprocess_miss](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringPreprocessing/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction.preprocess_miss)
- **Supporting lemma**. For each exponent e ≤ floor(log₂ N), all candidate powers along the binary root search fit in L·e ≤ L² bits. This certifies polynomial operand widths for the classical preprocessing comparisons. [preprocessingPowerWidth](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringPreprocessing/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction.preprocessingPowerWidth) · [rootTestsFit_width](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringPreprocessing/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction.rootTestsFit_width)
- **Supporting lemma**. An explicit fair-bit implementation of the random positive base draw in Section 5. Every target base has a unique accepted word of length 1+floor(log₂(N−1)) and at least half the words are accepted. [fairBitBaseSampler](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringReduction.fairBitBaseSampler)
- **Supporting lemma**. Binary datapath primitives used in the Section 5 factoring reduction. On unsigned words that fit in w bits, one 6w-gate ripple subtraction computes a comparison or a saturating predecessor. Fixed-width wiring and branch selection use the repository’s branching-program model. [leWord_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost.leWord_spec) · [predWord_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost.predWord_spec)
- **Supporting lemma**. Computes the gcd of two unsigned w-bit integers by binary restoring division and the Euclidean remainder recurrence. A public limit of 2w divisions suffices and at most 40(w+1)³ Boolean arithmetic gates are charged. [gcdRun_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost.gcdRun_spec) · [gcdWord_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost.gcdWord_spec)
- **Supporting lemma**. Implements the factoring reduction’s even-number and perfect-power checks. For a b-bit input it tests small exponents by binary root search, carries exact powers in (b+1)² bits, and returns the same optional divisor as the executable preprocessor within 100(b+1)⁶ Boolean arithmetic gates. [exactPower_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost.exactPower_spec) · [powersRun_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost.powersRun_spec) · [preprocessWord_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost.preprocessWord_spec) · [rootRun_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost.rootRun_spec) · [root_range](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost.root_range)
- **Supporting lemma**. Implements the Section 5 gcd reduction and checks both children of a proposed split. Word-based modular powering, Euclidean gcd and one restoring division agree with extractFactor and certify. Every successful pair includes the actual quotient. [candidate_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost.candidate_spec) · [checkedPair_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost.checkedPair_spec) · [extractPair_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost.extractPair_spec)
- **Supporting lemma**. Converts a fair binary word into the same positive base as the probability proof, and detects words outside the allowed range. A comparison, decrement and increment cost at most 18w Boolean arithmetic gates. [readBase_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost.readBase_spec)
- **Derived result**. The decoder returns an order candidate at most the clock size. With a proper positive base and a fitting positive clock, the postprocessing branch returns the certified divisor and cofactor from the same observed clock value within 2000(w+1)⁴ Boolean arithmetic gates. Nonunit bases take the gcd branch directly. [baseRun_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost.baseRun_spec) · [decoded_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost.decoded_bound)
- **Derived result**. For m ≥ 2, a fair word fitting floor(log₂ m)+1 bits, and a measured record below a clock size fitting twice that width plus two bits, all classical branches of a factor attempt return exactly the verified split and cost at most 10⁶(floor(log₂ m)+2)⁶ Boolean arithmetic gates. [bitTrial_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringBitCost.bitTrial_spec)
- **Derived result**. ForShor’s weighted logical-gate count for the allocated order-finding circuit is bounded uniformly over every modulus and legal coprime base by C(floor(log₂ m)+1)³. A finite maximum absorbs the small inputs left open by the asymptotic circuit theorem. [allWidths](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringResources/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringResources.allWidths) · [gateCap_covers](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringResources/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringResources.gateCap_covers)
- **Supporting lemma**. Lifts a bound on one modulus to the actual recursive divisor tree by summing over the nodes the splitter visits. Binary input widths decrease along subproblems. [log_mono](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringResources/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringResources.log_mono) · [treeCharge_le](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringResources/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringResources.treeCharge_le)
- **Derived result**. At every tape for input N, the adaptively visited subproblems, including all bases in a padded batch, cost at most C(floor(log₂ N)+1)⁶ logical gates in the pinned ForShor resource model. This is an execution count for the specified circuit family. [totalQuantumGates](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringResources/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringResources.totalQuantumGates)
- **Supporting lemma**. The fair base word and actual Fourier clock fit the argument widths required by the bit-level factoring program. Taking the finite maximum of the program’s costs over the physical records preserves the polynomial one-shot bound. [classicalCap_le](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringResources/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringResources.classicalCap_le) · [clockFits](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringResources/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringResources.clockFits) · [recordCost](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringResources/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringResources.recordCost) · [wordFits](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringResources/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringResources.wordFits)
- **Supporting lemma**. Connects each branch in the physical law to the bit-level program. On a coprime base it processes the same clock result, a nonunit yields its gcd, an oversized draw is rejected, and successful preprocessing returns the divisor together with its cofactor. [measuredRecord](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringResources/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringResources.measuredRecord) · [nonunitRecord](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringResources/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringResources.nonunitRecord) · [preprocessedRecord](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringResources/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringResources.preprocessedRecord) · [rejectedRecord](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringResources/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringResources.rejectedRecord)
- **Derived result**. For every recursive tape, with L=floor(log₂ N), the total classical trial datapath cost is at most 768000000(L+2)⁹ Boolean arithmetic gates and at most 768(L+1)⁴ independent fair bits need be drawn for base selection. Both counts pad every visited modulus to a full batch. [totalClassicalBits](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringResources/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringResources.totalClassicalBits) · [totalFairBits](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringResources/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringResources.totalFairBits)
- **Supporting lemma**. Every factor fits in the original binary word and there are at most floor(log₂ m) leaves below a recursive call at m ≥ 2. Even copying the complete output list at each return takes at most 2(floor(log₂ N)+2)³ bit writes under the fixed-width tagged representation. [leavesFit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringResources/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringResources.leavesFit) · [totalOutputBits](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringResources/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringResources.totalOutputBits)
- **Derived result**. Shor Sections 3–5 at the level of adaptive logical-circuit execution. For every positive N the physical procedure returns a product equal to N on every tape and a prime factorization with probability at least 2/3. Worst-case bounds jointly cover weighted logical gates, binary trial arithmetic, random bits and output copies. Shor’s Section 2 uniform circuit-construction and numerical gate-entry generation costs are separate. [factoringPolynomialResources](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringResources/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringResources.factoringPolynomialResources)
- **Supporting lemma**. Section 5 Eqs. (5.2)–(5.4). The explicit joint state has amplitude 1/√Q on each of the Q numerically distinct clock labels paired with its modular power. Its squared norm is one. [powerRegister_normalized](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FourierOrbit/FQChem.Complexity.FourierOrbit.powerRegister_normalized) · [scale_sq](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FourierOrbit/FQChem.Complexity.FourierOrbit.scale_sq)
- **Supporting lemma**. Section 5 uses the modular-power orbit. The natural modular data label depends only on t mod r; distinct residues below the exact order give distinct data basis labels. [label_injective](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FourierOrbit/FQChem.Complexity.FourierOrbit.label_injective) · [power_residue](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FourierOrbit/FQChem.Complexity.FourierOrbit.power_residue)
- **Paper result**. Section 5 Eqs. (5.4)–(5.7). Multiplying the normalized inverse Fourier matrix by the modular-power state and summing over every data label gives the sum of squared period-class amplitudes. No register-outcome probability formula is assumed. [amplitude_expand](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FourierOrbit/FQChem.Complexity.FourierOrbit.amplitude_expand) · [amplitude_label](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FourierOrbit/FQChem.Complexity.FourierOrbit.amplitude_label) · [born_eq_period_formula](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FourierOrbit/FQChem.Complexity.FourierOrbit.born_eq_period_formula)
- **Supporting lemma**. Sections 3 and 5. The primitive Q-th root gives orthogonal Fourier columns, and the explicitly normalized inverse Fourier matrix satisfies FᴴF=I for every positive Q. [inverseFourier_unitary](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FourierOrbit/FQChem.Complexity.FourierOrbit.inverseFourier_unitary) · [omega_norm](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FourierOrbit/FQChem.Complexity.FourierOrbit.omega_norm) · [phase_cross](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FourierOrbit/FQChem.Complexity.FourierOrbit.phase_cross)
- **Supporting lemma**. Matrix trace and inverse Fourier unitarity imply that the actual clock marginal of the modular-power superposition has total probability one. [born_normalized](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FourierOrbit/FQChem.Complexity.FourierOrbit.born_normalized) · [total_trace](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FourierOrbit/FQChem.Complexity.FourierOrbit.total_trace)
- **Supporting lemma**. Section 5 after Eq. (5.13). ForShor’s good-outcome recovery statement transfers to the integer implementation of the bounded continued-fraction scan. [good_decodes](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FourierOrbit/FQChem.Complexity.FourierOrbit.good_decodes)
- **Paper result**. Section 5 Fourier order recovery. The ideal matrix experiment and executable decoder yield the true order r with probability at least (4/π²)φ(r)/r. The proof covers every integer Q>r² and specializes to the power-of-two clock N²≤Q<2N² for every valid modulus, including powers of two. [idealRecovery](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FourierOrbit/FQChem.Complexity.FourierOrbit.idealRecovery) · [publishedClockRecovery](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FourierOrbit/FQChem.Complexity.FourierOrbit.publishedClockRecovery)
- **Supporting lemma**. Section 5 selects a binary clock in the half-open interval N²≤Q<2N². The construction and its inequalities apply even when N² is already a power of two. [paperClock_bounds](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FourierOrbit/FQChem.Complexity.FourierOrbit.paperClock_bounds)
- **Supporting lemma**. Uniqueness of the reduced inverse identifies the residue (1 + N − v) mod N with the reference circuit’s Step-5 cleanup constant selected by bounded search. [cleanup_constant](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.ModularConstants/FQChem.Complexity.ModularConstants.cleanup_constant)
- **Supporting lemma**. Squaring a reduced multiplier and its inverse together emits exactly the reference modular-exponentiation stages. Their integer constants require at most 162(w+1)² Boolean gates per exponent control. [steps_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.ModularConstants/FQChem.Complexity.ModularConstants.steps_spec)
- **Derived result**. Shor §3. From the input modulus and a coprime base, Euclidean inversion and repeated modular squaring produce every controlled multiplier and cleanup residue for the reference clock. The emitted modular-exponentiation gate tree is equal to ForShor’s specification, with at most 624(w+1)³ Boolean gates for generating the integer constants at modulus width w. Gate lowering and finite-precision evaluation of phase entries require separate bounds. [compile_constants](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.ModularConstants/FQChem.Complexity.ModularConstants.compile_constants)
- **Supporting lemma**. Classical arithmetic for the modular multiplication constants in Shor §3. For residues u,p below a positive w-bit modulus N, a padded sum, subtraction, and restoring division compute (u+N−p) mod N using at most 22(w+1)² Boolean gates. [subtractMod_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.ModularInverse/FQChem.Complexity.ModularInverse.subtractMod_spec)
- **Supporting lemma**. Shor §3 uses the classical modular inverse to implement reversible multiplication. The Euclidean loop maintains a coefficient residue for each remainder and returns a coefficient of the gcd. With valid input residues and t steps covering the Euclidean divisions, the loop uses at most 150t(w+1)² Boolean gates. [remainderCoefficient](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.ModularInverse/FQChem.Complexity.ModularInverse.remainderCoefficient) · [run_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.ModularInverse/FQChem.Complexity.ModularInverse.run_spec)
- **Derived result**. Shor §3, classical calculation of c⁻¹ mod n in the reversible cleanup pseudocode (preprint p. 12). For 1<N<2ʷ and 0≤a<N coprime to N, the executable bounded Euclidean algorithm produces b<N satisfying ab mod N=1 in at most 300(w+1)³ Boolean data-path gates. The explicit bound applies to this circuit-generation subroutine. [inverse_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.ModularInverse/FQChem.Complexity.ModularInverse.inverse_spec)
- **Supporting lemma**. Elementary Boolean arithmetic for Section 5 postprocessing. The six-gate conditional-complement ripple cell handles one bit of addition or subtraction. A width-w chain has 6w gates, an exact carry/borrow specification and a bounded result. [add_word](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost.add_word) · [cell](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost.cell) · [low_split](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost.low_split) · [ripple_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost.ripple_spec)
- **Supporting lemma**. Executable candidate selection and comparison. Word equality uses 3w gates, a selector uses 4w, and checking the returned residue and updating the least positive candidate costs 24w gates. [equal_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost.equal_spec) · [select_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost.select_spec) · [update_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost.update_spec)
- **Supporting lemma**. The explicit shift-add multiplier with per-bit selection computes x·y when it fits in the w-bit result word and uses exactly 10wn Boolean gates for n multiplier bits. [multiply_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost.multiply_spec)
- **Supporting lemma**. The restoring divider computes quotient and remainder together in exactly 10wn Boolean gates for an n-bit dividend, positive divisor y and padded remainder width satisfying 2y ≤ 2^w. [divide_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost.divide_spec)
- **Derived result**. The binary Euclidean recurrence returns precisely the Section 5 decoder’s numerator and denominator at index t, with at most 30(t+1)(w+1)² Boolean data-path operations on w-bit inputs. [fraction_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost.fraction_spec)
- **Supporting lemma**. Double-width schoolbook multiplication followed by restoring division gives modular reduction. Evaluating both possible square-and-multiply products at every exponent bit implements modular powering in at most 150n(w+1)² Boolean data-path operations. [power_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost.power_spec) · [productMod_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost.productMod_spec)
- **Derived result**. Executable Boolean refinement of the continued-fraction and modular-verification decoder after Eq. (5.13). Its result exactly equals the natural-arithmetic decoder, with at most 1000(w+1)⁴ Boolean operations at w=1+floor(log₂ max(N,Q)). The branching computation treats shifts, indexing, wiring and fan-out as free. Gate counts come from explicit ripple, selection, multiplication and division routines. [decode_input_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost.decode_input_bound) · [decoder_bit_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost.decoder_bit_bound) · [scan_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingBitCost.scan_spec)
- **Supporting lemma**. For the Section 5 continued-fraction decoder, the integer recurrence maintains coprimality, a positive denominator and bounded numerator/denominator. Computing index t takes at most t+1 quotient levels. [convergent_bounds](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingDecoder/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingDecoder.convergent_bounds) · [convergent_invariants](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingDecoder/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingDecoder.convergent_invariants)
- **Supporting lemma**. At every index, including after an exact rational terminates, the Euclidean recurrence agrees with Mathlib’s Real.convergent. It already produces the reduced denominator, as needed for the order test after Eq. (5.13). [convergent_eq_real](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingDecoder/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingDecoder.convergent_eq_real) · [denominator_eq_real](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingDecoder/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingDecoder.denominator_eq_real)
- **Supporting lemma**. Invariant for the Section 5 implementation that keeps the least positive passing denominator. It updates the minimum when another candidate is inserted, using zero for the empty set. [least_insert](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingDecoder/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingDecoder.least_insert)
- **Derived result**. Exact algorithmic refinement of Section 5 continued-fraction postprocessing. Scanning t integer convergents and testing denominators by binary modular exponentiation yields the reference minimum over verified real convergent denominators, or zero if there are none. [scan_eq_reference](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingDecoder/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingDecoder.scan_eq_reference)
- **Derived result**. The actual scan counts its Euclidean frames and modular multiplications. For clock width L and budget 2L+1, their bounds are (2L+1)(2L+2) and 2L(2L+1), respectively. This supplies an explicit arithmetic-work bound for Section 5 postprocessing. [decode_work](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingDecoder/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingDecoder.decode_work) · [scan_work](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingDecoder/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingDecoder.scan_work)
- **Supporting lemma**. At each visited Euclidean frame the operands and intermediate numerator products and sums are at most the clock size Q, and both unreduced products in each modular multiplication frame are below N². These width bounds complement the polynomial arithmetic counts. [euclidean_width](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingDecoder/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingDecoder.euclidean_width) · [modular_product_width](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingDecoder/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingDecoder.modular_product_width)
- **Supporting lemma**. Two Euclidean remainder steps halve the denominator. Starting below 2^k, the algorithm performs at most 2k divisions. [divisions_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingPostprocessing/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingPostprocessing.divisions_bound) · [two_steps_halve](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingPostprocessing/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingPostprocessing.two_steps_halve)
- **Derived result**. Identifies termination of the real continued fraction for a measured rational o/Q with an explicit Euclidean division count. The scan over the first 2(log₂ Q + 1) + 1 indices suffices for every o < Q, with the logarithm rounded down. [logarithmic_scan](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingPostprocessing/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingPostprocessing.logarithmic_scan) · [stream_terminates](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingPostprocessing/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingPostprocessing.stream_terminates)
- **Supporting lemma**. The explicit logarithmic scan satisfies ForShor’s continued-fraction completeness specification for every clock width. [complete](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.OrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFinding.complete)
- **Paper result**. The ideal initialized Fourier circuit and the bounded order decoder together return the exact order with Born probability at least 4φ(r)/(π²r), hence at least φ(r)/(3r). ForShor chooses N² < Q ≤ 2N². This includes the paper’s clock for moduli which are not powers of two. [fourierRecovery](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.OrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFinding.fourierRecovery) · [oneRun](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.OrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFinding.oneRun)
- **Derived result**. After ceil(12 ln(1+floor(log₂(r+1)))) independent trials, the failure bound obtained from the φ(r)/(3r) certificate is at most exp(−1). [independentTrialBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.OrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFinding.independentTrialBound)
- **Derived result**. Finite matrix semantics of the reference two-limb Toom–Cook implementation. On the all-zero input, at precision chosen for the modulus and with fqchem’s bounded decoder, its one-run success probability is at least a strictly positive certified bound. [compiledRecovery](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.OrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFinding.compiledRecovery)
- **Supporting lemma**. An explicit lower bound for the unit fraction φ(r)/r, logarithmic in the bit width, supplies the independent-retry estimate. [shor_totient_ratio](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Shor.Proofs.Math.TotientBoundsLarge/Shor.shor_totient_ratio)
- **Supporting lemma**. Preparing the zero registers and applying Hadamards and modular exponentiation yields the pre-inverse-QFT state used in Section 5. [eval_orderFindingIdeal_prefix](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Shor.Proofs.NaiveShor.GoodOutcomeMassLowerBound/Shor.eval_orderFindingIdeal_prefix)
- **Supporting lemma**. There are at least φ(r) distinct good clock outcomes and each has probability at least 4/(π²r). Summation gives the order-recovery mass. [goodOutcome_card_ge_totient](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Shor.Proofs.NaiveShor.OrderFindingAnalysis/Shor.goodOutcome_card_ge_totient) · [goodOutcome_mass_lower_bound_from_card_and_pointwise](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Shor.Proofs.NaiveShor.OrderFindingAnalysis/Shor.goodOutcome_mass_lower_bound_from_card_and_pointwise) · [shor_paper_goodOutcome_lower_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Shor.Proofs.NaiveShor.OrderFindingAnalysis/Shor.shor_paper_goodOutcome_lower_bound)
- **Supporting lemma**. For the initialized ideal circuit the Born distribution agrees with the finite exponential sums of Section 5. [measProbAfter_orderFindingIdeal_eq_paper_formula](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Shor.Proofs.NaiveShor.PhaseEstimation/Shor.measProbAfter_orderFindingIdeal_eq_paper_formula)
- **Supporting lemma**. The register bounds imply r² < Q and every good outcome passes the order check within a complete scan. [goodOutcome_mass_le_probability_of_success](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Shor.Proofs.NaiveShor.Preliminaries/Shor.goodOutcome_mass_le_probability_of_success) · [shor_order_parameter_bounds](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Shor.Proofs.NaiveShor.Preliminaries/Shor.shor_order_parameter_bounds)
- **Derived result**. An executable positive-denominator scan using binary modular multiplication returns exactly the same natural number as ForShor’s specification. [executableDecoder](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.OrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFinding.executableDecoder)
- **Derived result**. At the explicit integer precision schedule the number of extra modular work bits is bounded by 70 times the data width. [extraBits_linear](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.OrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFinding.extraBits_linear)
- **Derived result**. For the two-limb reference multiplication table and explicit precision level, the same circuit evaluated by programRecovery uses O(n³) logical gates at data width n, in the ForShor elementary-gate model. [programGateCount](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.OrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFinding.programGateCount)
- **Derived result**. The explicit two-limb circuit on the zero vector, measured in its finite matrix semantics, has success at least 1/(2π² ln(1+floor(log₂ N))). Its executable decoder and precision schedule are fixed. [programRecovery](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.OrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFinding.programRecovery)
- **Derived result**. The executable precision level 2²⁶n⁴ gives enough accuracy for the lowered circuit to keep half the ideal success certificate. [referencePrecision_le](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.OrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFinding.referencePrecision_le)
- **Derived result**. Summation of Born weights over clock outcomes returning a verified factor gives a nonnegative result. [factorProbability_nonneg](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.QuantumFactoring/FQChem.Complexity.QuantumFactoring.factorProbability_nonneg)
- **Derived result**. For each good unit the circuit’s exact-order outcomes yield genuine factors after the executable gcd check. Thus the one-shot factor probability is at least the quantum recovery certificate. [goodBaseRecovery](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.QuantumFactoring/FQChem.Complexity.QuantumFactoring.goodBaseRecovery)
- **Derived result**. Every clock outcome of the physical low-level matrix circuit has nonnegative Born weight. [mass_nonneg](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.QuantumFactoring/FQChem.Complexity.QuantumFactoring.mass_nonneg)
- **Derived result**. Averaging the physical matrix Born probability over a uniformly chosen unit proves an unconditional one-run lower bound (1−2^(1−k))/(2π² ln(1+floor(log₂ N))) for odd N>1 with k distinct primes. [uniformFactorProbability](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.QuantumFactoring/FQChem.Complexity.QuantumFactoring.uniformFactorProbability)
- **Derived result**. Converting the chosen unit to the input structure preserves the multiplicative order used by the quantum circuit. [unitOrder](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.QuantumFactoring/FQChem.Complexity.QuantumFactoring.unitOrder)
- **Supporting lemma**. Taking the least nonzero executable candidate returns the true order whenever at least one trial recovers it. Every positive candidate passes modular verification and hence is at least the order. [selected_if_hit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.OrderFindingTrials/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingTrials.selected_if_hit)
- **Paper result**. At Shor’s binary clock, the wrong-answer probability of the independent Fourier experiments and bounded decoder is ≤1/e in ceil(12 ln(1+floor(log₂(r+1)))) trials. The joint law is the product of the normalized matrix Born distributions. [boundedErrorOrderFinding](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.OrderFindingTrials/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingTrials.boundedErrorOrderFinding)
- **Derived result**. Each clock outcome has nonnegative weight in the explicit projected matrix column. [mass_pos](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.CompiledSampling/FQChem.Complexity.CompiledSampling.mass_pos)
- **Derived result**. Summing over the complete clock register gives probability one. The circuit matrix is unitary, the input is the zero basis vector, and the register projectors partition the final amplitudes. [normalized](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.CompiledSampling/FQChem.Complexity.CompiledSampling.normalized)
- **Derived result**. For an arbitrary positive base, the stochastic output measure counts exactly the verified factor probability, including the immediate gcd branch. [baseLaw_certified](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringLaw/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringLaw.baseLaw_certified)
- **Derived result**. For any composite m ≤ N, preprocessing and one fair-word trial return a checked proper divisor with probability at least 1/[128(1+floor(log₂ N))]. [compositeShot](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringLaw/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringLaw.compositeShot)
- **Derived result**. Summation over uniformly sampled fair words accounts for rejected draws; one physical trial keeps at least half the mean accepted-base factor probability. [drawLaw_certified](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringLaw/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringLaw.drawLaw_certified)
- **Derived result**. Two order-finding inputs with the same base and modulus describe the same quantum experiment. [input_ext](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringLaw/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringLaw.input_ext)
- **Derived result**. The finite-matrix circuit, bit decoder, classical gcd check and recursive splitter return primes multiplying to N with probability at least 2/3. The product is exact on every tape and the bound on trial count is cubic in the input bit length. [primeFactorization](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringLaw/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringLaw.primeFactorization)
- **Derived result**. Every accepted factor in the pushed-forward clock distribution satisfies both strict bounds and divides the modulus. [unitLaw_certified](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringLaw/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringLaw.unitLaw_certified)
- **Derived result**. Pushing the normalized clock measure through the executable decoder and factor extractor recovers its explicit finite Born sum. [unitLaw_success](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringLaw/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringLaw.unitLaw_success)
- **Derived result**. The fair-word sample space has between N−1 and 2(N−1) elements, so at least half the words encode usable positive bases. [wordBounds](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringLaw/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringLaw.wordBounds)
- **Derived result**. Whenever the verified extractor accepts the true order, every circuit outcome decoding that order returns the certified divisor. [acceptedOrder](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.QuantumFactoring/FQChem.Complexity.QuantumFactoring.acceptedOrder)
- **Derived result**. A uniform draw from all integers 1,…,N−1 and one compiled experiment yield a factor with probability at least (1−2^(1−k))/(2π² ln(1+floor(log₂ N))). This includes nonunits, for which the first gcd already splits N. [positiveBaseFactorProbability](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.QuantumFactoring/FQChem.Complexity.QuantumFactoring.positiveBaseFactorProbability)
- **Derived result**. Converting a positive coprime base less than N to a modular unit preserves its natural representative. [representative](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.QuantumFactoring/FQChem.Complexity.QuantumFactoring.representative)
- **Derived result**. A schedule using only the modulus gives at least 2/t success per run of the chosen ForShor circuit, using t = 64(1+floor(log₂(1+floor(log₂N)))). The bound comes from its matrix recovery theorem with κ=1/π². [oneShot](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.VerifiedOrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.VerifiedOrderFinding.oneShot) · [trial_inverse](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.VerifiedOrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.VerifiedOrderFinding.trial_inverse)
- **Supporting lemma**. The allocated compiled circuit measures a clock of size 2^floor(log₂(2N²)). This specifies the physical input to the decoder; the ideal paper-clock theorem separately uses N²≤Q<2N². [physicalClock](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.VerifiedOrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.VerifiedOrderFinding.physicalClock)
- **Derived result**. The known-N schedule has failure at most exp(−2) < 1/3 under the actual product of independent compiled-circuit Born laws. Those tuple weights sum to one. [compiledError](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.VerifiedOrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.VerifiedOrderFinding.compiledError) · [expNegTwo_lt_third](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.VerifiedOrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.VerifiedOrderFinding.expNegTwo_lt_third) · [joint_born_mass](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.VerifiedOrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.VerifiedOrderFinding.joint_born_mass)
- **Supporting lemma**. Every decoded denominator fits the clock. Streaming the records through bit-level decoding, a zero check, and a minimum selector agrees exactly with the reference minimum of the nonzero verified estimates, with all those data-path operations counted. [decoded_le_clock](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.VerifiedOrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.VerifiedOrderFinding.decoded_le_clock) · [records_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.VerifiedOrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.VerifiedOrderFinding.records_spec) · [select_cons](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.VerifiedOrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.VerifiedOrderFinding.select_cons)
- **Derived result**. The physical bounded-error statement uses the executable Boolean decoder and streaming minimum on fresh zero-state preparations of the same compiled order-finding circuit. The number of preparations is determined from the known modulus. [physical_bounded_error](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.VerifiedOrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.VerifiedOrderFinding.physical_bounded_error)
- **Derived result**. Across the complete known-modulus repetition schedule for a valid unit input, the allocated circuit uses O(L⁴) logical gate operations and the decoder plus aggregation use ≤40,000,000 L⁵ Boolean data-path operations at sufficiently large input width L. These are loose upper bounds in the stated cost models. [polynomialWork](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.VerifiedOrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.VerifiedOrderFinding.polynomialWork)
- **Paper result**. Shor §§3–5. The normalized ideal paper-clock experiment has one-shot success ≥(4/π²)φ(r)/r and constant error after O(log log r) independent shots. A concrete compiled circuit, run for a count computed solely from N, yields the exact order with error <1/3 after Boolean decoding and aggregation. Total logical gates and classical data-path operations are polynomial in the bit length of N, with the distinct ideal and compiled clocks explicit. The separate uniformity requirement in §2 concerns the classical time to generate gate descriptions. Its cost is outside this execution bound. [shorOrderFinding](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.VerifiedOrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.VerifiedOrderFinding.shorOrderFinding)

## Silver, Sivia & Gubernatis (1990) · [Maximum-entropy method for analytic continuation of quantum Monte Carlo data](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevB.41.2380)

- **Derived result**. For known distinct real energies, exact samples at n uniformly spaced imaginary times with positive spacing identify n discrete amplitudes by a Vandermonde argument. Silver et al. study ill-conditioned noisy analytic continuation. [spectralIdentification](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Properties.ResponseAndEstimation.SpectralReconstruction/FQChem.Algorithms.spectralIdentification)

## Simon (1997) · [On the Power of Quantum Computation](https://doi.org/10.1137/S0097539796298637)

- **Paper result**. Simon, Theorem 3.2. Under the injective-or-hidden-XOR-mask promise, a verified retry process halts almost surely with the correct mask (zero denotes injective). Its expected number of trials is at most two, with at most 2(n+3) expected oracle calls and an explicit classical decoder cost in Qatlib’s model. [simonMaskRecovery](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Complexity.QueryAlgorithms/FQChem.Complexity.QueryAlgorithms.simonMaskRecovery)
- **Paper result**. Simon, Theorem 3.2. Under the injective-or-hidden-XOR-mask promise, a verified retry process halts almost surely with the correct mask (zero denotes injective). Its expected number of trials is at most two, with at most 2(n+3) expected oracle calls and an explicit classical decoder cost in Qatlib’s model. [simonTheorem32](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Papers.QPowerSimon97.Recovery.Theorem32Paper/Qatlib.Papers.QPowerSimon97.Recovery.simonTheorem32)
- **Paper result**. Simon, Theorem 3.3. For n ≥ 1, a randomized adaptive tree with at most 2^(n/4) queries guesses the fair branch of the random oracle with probability at most 1/2 + 2^(−n/2). The branches are a uniform permutation and a uniform two-to-one function invariant under a uniformly sampled XOR mask, including zero as in the printed distribution. [simonClassicalQueryBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Complexity.QueryAlgorithms/FQChem.Complexity.QueryAlgorithms.simonClassicalQueryBound)
- **Paper result**. Simon, Theorem 3.3. For n ≥ 1, a randomized adaptive tree with at most 2^(n/4) queries guesses the fair branch of the random oracle with probability at most 1/2 + 2^(−n/2). The branches are a uniform permutation and a uniform two-to-one function invariant under a uniformly sampled XOR mask, including zero as in the printed distribution. [simonTheorem33RealBudget](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Papers.QPowerSimon97.StandardModel.Theorem34Separation/Qatlib.Papers.QPowerSimon97.StandardDefs.simonTheorem33RealBudget)
- **Special case**. Simon, Theorem 3.4 in the length-indexed query-tree model. For any countable family of randomized trees with q(n) ≤ 2^(n/4), a single oracle family gives the quantum decision procedure success ≥ 2/3 eventually, while each classical family fails to exceed 2/3 at infinitely many widths. At width n each tree queries only the n-bit function. [simonOracleFamilySeparation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Complexity.QueryAlgorithms/FQChem.Complexity.QueryAlgorithms.simonOracleFamilySeparation)
- **Special case**. Simon, Theorem 3.4 in the length-indexed query-tree model. For any countable family of randomized trees with q(n) ≤ 2^(n/4), a single oracle family gives the quantum decision procedure success ≥ 2/3 eventually, while each classical family fails to exceed 2/3 at infinitely many widths. At width n each tree queries only the n-bit function. [simon_infiniteOracle_separation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Papers.QPowerSimon97.StandardModel.InfiniteOracle/Qatlib.Papers.QPowerSimon97.StandardDefs.simon_infiniteOracle_separation)
- **Supporting lemma**. Fix all widths except n in a function oracle. Hardwiring the off-width answers gives a width-n adaptive decision tree with the same Boolean output and at most the original number of queries. State labels are inaccessible to the algorithm. [restrictBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Complexity.SimonOracleMachines/FQChem.Complexity.SimonOracleMachines.Tree.restrictBound) · [restrictOutput](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Complexity.SimonOracleMachines/FQChem.Complexity.SimonOracleMachines.Tree.restrictOutput)
- **Supporting lemma**. Execution depends only on the oracle at widths the tree can reach. Success probability additionally uses the target width's correct label. These statements preserve earlier diagonal requirements while later widths are set. [outputCongr](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Complexity.SimonOracleMachines/FQChem.Complexity.SimonOracleMachines.Tree.outputCongr) · [successCongr](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Complexity.SimonOracleMachines/FQChem.Complexity.SimonOracleMachines.Tree.successCongr)
- **Derived result**. Simon, Theorem 3.3, applied to the restriction of a randomized adaptive tree: for n ≥ 64 and at most 2^(n/4) queries, some promised replacement at width n reduces success below 2/3, even with arbitrary fixed values at other widths. [existsHardAt](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Complexity.SimonOracleMachines/FQChem.Complexity.SimonOracleMachines.Tree.existsHardAt)
- **Derived result**. Simon, §3.2. One function oracle defeats every member of a countable collection of randomized adaptive query algorithms infinitely often. Each program may query across lengths, with any given finite width budget and at most 2^(n/4) calls. The amplified quantum procedure succeeds with probability at least 7/8 at all n ≥ 64. [oracleSeparation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Complexity.SimonOracleMachines/FQChem.Complexity.SimonOracleMachines.oracleSeparation)
- **Supporting lemma**. The finite segment of the physical query tape grows by at most one cell per local machine step. Its binary query ends at the first blank, and a length-preserving oracle reply occupies exactly that many cells. [cellsStep](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Complexity.SimonTuringSeparation/FQChem.Complexity.SimonOracleMachines.Tape.cellsStep) · [replyCells](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Complexity.SimonTuringSeparation/FQChem.Complexity.SimonOracleMachines.Tape.replyCells) · [wordLength](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Complexity.SimonTuringSeparation/FQChem.Complexity.SimonOracleMachines.Tape.wordLength)
- **Supporting lemma**. Fix the random tape of the two-tape probabilistic oracle machine. Its finite adaptive query tree returns the machine's direct operational result for every fixed oracle, including cross-width queries. Local steps pass the clock without consuming a query. [padOutput](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Complexity.SimonTuringSeparation/FQChem.Complexity.SimonOracleMachines.Tree.padOutput) · [unrollOutput](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Complexity.SimonTuringSeparation/FQChem.Complexity.SimonOracleMachines.unrollOutput)
- **Derived result**. Simon, §3.2. A single fixed promised oracle gives amplified quantum success ≥ 7/8 from width 64 and defeats each finite two-tape probabilistic oracle machine with probability > 1/3 on arbitrarily large unary inputs under the floor(2^(n/4)) step clock. The paper schedule uses 3n² sampling queries and 6n³ local gates. [turingSeparation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Complexity.SimonTuringSeparation/FQChem.Complexity.SimonOracleMachines.turingSeparation)
- **Supporting lemma**. On unary strings the target bit is the oracle's permutation-versus-hidden-period label. The amplified Born law evaluated at that bit equals Qatlib's two-sided success quantity by normalization of the Boolean output. [amplifiedCorrect](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Complexity.SimonTuringSeparation/FQChem.Complexity.SimonOracleMachines.amplifiedCorrect) · [unaryLabel](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Complexity.SimonTuringSeparation/FQChem.Complexity.SimonOracleMachines.unaryLabel)
- **Derived result**. A uniform schedule of n independent blocks of 3n samples uses polynomially many operations: the actual Hadamard lists, oracle calls, measurements, bit conversion, counted binary Gaussian elimination on arbitrary sample rows, and final candidate scan fit in 100(n+1)⁴ operations. [polynomialWork](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Complexity.SimonTuringSeparation/FQChem.Complexity.SimonOracleMachines.polynomialWork)
- **Derived result**. For each real constant C, any integer time bound at most C 2^(n/8) eventually fits in the diagonalization clock floor(2^(n/4)). [clockDominates](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Complexity.SimonTuringSeparation/FQChem.Complexity.SimonOracleMachines.clockDominates)
- **Paper result**. Simon, Theorem 3.4, for a length-preserving function oracle. One oracle and unary language admit ≥ 7/8 quantum success on every binary input via the uniform Simon circuit and a fixed 64-entry small-input table. Quantum work is polynomial. Every finite-table two-tape fair-coin oracle Turing machine under the floor(2^(n/4)) clock has success < 2/3 on arbitrarily large unary inputs, even with adaptive queries at other widths. [unaryLanguageSeparation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemQatlib.Complexity.SimonTuringSeparation/FQChem.Complexity.SimonOracleMachines.unaryLanguageSeparation)
- **Supporting lemma**. Probability helper: pointwise equal score functions have equal expectation under a discrete law. It transfers machine execution equivalences through random choices. [expectation_congr](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.AEMeanReadout.LocalAEBackend.FiniteStoppingTail/Qatlib.Concepts.AEMeanReadout.LocalAEBackend.CostedLaw.expectation_congr)
- **Supporting lemma**. Probability helper: the expectation of a score under a pushed-forward discrete law equals its expectation after composition. It transfers Simon success probabilities through transcript compilation. [expectation_map](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.LinearLearning.ConcisePipeline.RandomizedCost/Qatlib.Concepts.LinearLearning.ConcisePipeline.CostedLaw.expectation_map)
- **Supporting lemma**. The counted binary Gaussian decoder returns the specified Simon-secret decoder’s answer and uses at most 10(mn²+n³+mn+n²+n+1) bit operations on m input rows of width n. [boolean_gaussian_actual_operation_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Concepts.HspRecovery.Reduction.BooleanGaussianDecoder/Qatlib.Concepts.HspRecovery.ReductionCore.boolean_gaussian_actual_operation_bound)
- **Supporting lemma**. Simon, §3.2. At width n ≥ 64 every randomized classical width-n tree of at most 2^(n/4) queries has success below 2/3 on some promised labeled oracle. [exists_good_low](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Papers.QPowerSimon97.StandardModel.InfiniteOracle/Qatlib.Papers.QPowerSimon97.StandardDefs.exists_good_low)
- **Supporting lemma**. Enumeration helper: the first component of the natural-number pairing bijection takes each machine index at arbitrarily large integers. The diagonal proof tests every machine infinitely often. [exists_length_of_index](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Papers.QPowerSimon97.StandardModel.InfiniteOracle/Qatlib.Papers.QPowerSimon97.StandardDefs.exists_length_of_index)
- **Supporting lemma**. On every injective or strict nonzero-period oracle of width n ≥ 64 the amplified Simon schedule has success at least 7/8. This is the quantum upper bound used after diagonalization. [simonQuantumSuccess_good](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Papers.QPowerSimon97.StandardModel.InfiniteOracle/Qatlib.Papers.QPowerSimon97.StandardDefs.simonQuantumSuccess_good)
- **Supporting lemma**. Normalization of the two Boolean probabilities of the amplified Simon decision law lets success be read directly at the correct label. [simonReject_total](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Papers.QPowerSimon97.StandardModel.JointSeparation/Qatlib.Papers.QPowerSimon97.StandardDefs.simonReject_total)
- **Supporting lemma**. The explicit concatenated local gate program has the gate count in the ledger. The other entries count sampled queries, measurements and conversion steps for r blocks of n+k samples. [simonExactLedger_eq](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Papers.QPowerSimon97.StandardModel.SimonRetry/Qatlib.Papers.QPowerSimon97.StandardDefs.simonExactLedger_eq)
- **Supporting lemma**. At the fixed schedule k = 2n and r = n, the local Simon experiment ledger has 3n² queries, 3n² measurements, 6n³ local gates and 3n³ bit conversion steps. [simonPaperScheduleLedger](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Papers.QPowerSimon97.StandardModel.Theorem34Separation/Qatlib.Papers.QPowerSimon97.StandardDefs.simonPaperScheduleLedger)

## Slater (1929) · [The Theory of Complex Spectra](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRev.34.1293)

- **Supporting lemma**. For a finite mode space, conjugating the occupation-basis amplitudes of an exterior determinant gives its physical bra on every state. Orthonormal occupied orbitals give norm one, as in O’Gorman et al., Eq. (14). [braCoefficient](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction.braCoefficient) · [normalized](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction.normalized) · [physicalBra](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction.physicalBra)
- **Derived result**. The overlap with the exterior power of an identity plus m coordinate moves equals an m-by-m determinant of shifted one-particle density entries. The finite-rank identity supplies the one- and two-body contractions. [overlap](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction.overlap)
- **Special case**. For an orthonormal complex occupied frame, the one-body expectation is the density entry and the two-body expectation is its antisymmetrized two-by-two minor. These are number-conserving two- and four-operator cases of Wick contraction on a single determinant. [oneBody](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction.oneBody) · [twoBody](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction.twoBody)
- **Derived result**. Slater, Part 2, and O’Gorman et al., Eqs. (3)–(5) and (14). For arbitrary finite complex one- and two-body coefficients, a normalized determinant has energy given by contracting its one-particle density and its direct-minus-exchange minor. The two-body coefficient includes the Hamiltonian’s double-counting factor. [energy](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction.energy)

## Sorella (2005) · [Wave function optimization in the variational Monte Carlo method](https://arxiv.org/abs/cond-mat/0502553)

- **Special case**. For a finite real symmetric Hamiltonian and differentiable real trial amplitudes with full support and nonzero mass, the derivative of the normalized energy is twice the covariance of local energy and the logarithmic derivative. [energyGradient](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VariationalMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.VariationalMonteCarlo.energyGradient)

## Stair, Huang & Evangelista (2020) · [A Multireference Quantum Krylov Algorithm for Strongly Correlated Electrons](https://doi.org/10.1021/acs.jctc.9b01125)

- **Derived result**. Enlarging the orbit space of a nonzero vector under any unitary step cannot raise the Hamiltonian Ritz infimum. The statement assumes exact subspaces and overlaps. [quantumKrylov](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.SubspaceDiagonalization.Krylov/FQChem.VariationalMethods.quantumKrylov)

## Stone (1932) · [On One-Parameter Unitary Groups in Hilbert Space](https://doi.org/10.2307/1968538)

- **Special case**. For an everywhere-defined bounded self-adjoint Hamiltonian, exp(−itH) is a unitary one-parameter group and is differentiable with the Schrödinger generator. Stone also characterizes general strongly continuous groups via possibly unbounded generators. [unitaryEvolution](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.RealTimePropagation.SchrodingerGroup/FQChem.Dynamics.RealTimePropagation.unitaryEvolution)

## Subaşı, Somma & Orsucci (2019) · [Quantum algorithms for systems of linear equations inspired by adiabatic quantum computing](https://arxiv.org/abs/1805.10549)

- **Supporting lemma**. Eq. (1) of Subaşı, Somma and Orsucci and the HHL solution-state readout. For a unitary eigenbasis and nonzero real eigenvalues, the spectral Hermitian matrix’s pseudoinverse equals its ordinary inverse. [pseudoinverse_eq_inv_of_ne_zero](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#Qatlib.Papers.QLinearSolver18.MainResults.Readout.PseudoinverseReadout/Qatlib.Papers.QLinearSolver18.MainResults.Readout.PseudoinverseReadout.pseudoinverse_eq_inv_of_ne_zero)

## Sugiyama & Koonin (1986) · [Auxiliary field Monte-Carlo for quantum many-body ground states](https://doi.org/10.1016/0003-4916%2886%2990107-7)

- **Supporting lemma**. Distributivity expands a power of a finite noncommutative sum into all ordered field histories. [fieldHistoryExpansion](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC.fieldHistoryExpansion)
- **Derived result**. A fixed number of split kinetic/interaction steps equals the exact sum of all discrete Hubbard field histories. [hubbardFreeProjection](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC.hubbardFreeProjection)
- **Special case**. For a finite field decomposition by weighted one-body exterior maps, a Slater state propagates as the exact signed or complex weighted sum of propagated orbital wedges. [freeProjection](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC.freeProjection) · [slaterHistory](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC.slaterHistory)
- **Special case**. A trial exterior-product bra evaluated on the finite field-history sum gives a weighted sum of orbital overlap determinants. [projectedOverlap](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC.projectedOverlap)

## Suresh, Courtney, Tao & Cao (2026) · [Formally verified harness for Shor’s order finding algorithm with O(n²⁺ᵋ) gate cost](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20342)

- **Supporting lemma**. Uniqueness of the reduced inverse identifies the residue (1 + N − v) mod N with the reference circuit’s Step-5 cleanup constant selected by bounded search. [cleanup_constant](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.ModularConstants/FQChem.Complexity.ModularConstants.cleanup_constant)
- **Supporting lemma**. Squaring a reduced multiplier and its inverse together emits exactly the reference modular-exponentiation stages. Their integer constants require at most 162(w+1)² Boolean gates per exponent control. [steps_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.ModularConstants/FQChem.Complexity.ModularConstants.steps_spec)
- **Derived result**. Shor §3. From the input modulus and a coprime base, Euclidean inversion and repeated modular squaring produce every controlled multiplier and cleanup residue for the reference clock. The emitted modular-exponentiation gate tree is equal to ForShor’s specification, with at most 624(w+1)³ Boolean gates for generating the integer constants at modulus width w. Gate lowering and finite-precision evaluation of phase entries require separate bounds. [compile_constants](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.ModularConstants/FQChem.Complexity.ModularConstants.compile_constants)
- **Derived result**. Identifies termination of the real continued fraction for a measured rational o/Q with an explicit Euclidean division count. The scan over the first 2(log₂ Q + 1) + 1 indices suffices for every o < Q, with the logarithm rounded down. [logarithmic_scan](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingPostprocessing/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingPostprocessing.logarithmic_scan) · [stream_terminates](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingPostprocessing/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingPostprocessing.stream_terminates)
- **Supporting lemma**. The explicit logarithmic scan satisfies ForShor’s continued-fraction completeness specification for every clock width. [complete](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.OrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFinding.complete)
- **Paper result**. The ideal initialized Fourier circuit and the bounded order decoder together return the exact order with Born probability at least 4φ(r)/(π²r), hence at least φ(r)/(3r). ForShor chooses N² < Q ≤ 2N². This includes the paper’s clock for moduli which are not powers of two. [fourierRecovery](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.OrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFinding.fourierRecovery) · [oneRun](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.OrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFinding.oneRun)
- **Derived result**. After ceil(12 ln(1+floor(log₂(r+1)))) independent trials, the failure bound obtained from the φ(r)/(3r) certificate is at most exp(−1). [independentTrialBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.OrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFinding.independentTrialBound)
- **Derived result**. Finite matrix semantics of the reference two-limb Toom–Cook implementation. On the all-zero input, at precision chosen for the modulus and with fqchem’s bounded decoder, its one-run success probability is at least a strictly positive certified bound. [compiledRecovery](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.OrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFinding.compiledRecovery)
- **Supporting lemma**. The checked interpolation table gives an admissible constant lowering policy for the modular arithmetic circuit. [constPolicy_admissible](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Framework.Policy/Shor.ShorLoweringPolicy.constPolicy_admissible) · [table_admissible](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Framework.Policy/Shor.ShorLoweringSetup.table_admissible)
- **Paper result**. The submitted low-level circuit achieves the reference bound using its allocated layout, selected precision, all-zero input, and any complete continued-fraction scan. [referenceSubmittedProgram_correct](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Reference.Standard.Main/Shor.Reference.referenceSubmittedProgram_correct)
- **Supporting lemma**. There is enough precision for the reference circuit to receive a positive probability certificate at every modulus. [referenceSuccessProbability_pos](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Reference.Standard.Proofs.Success/Shor.Reference.referenceSuccessProbability_pos)
- **Supporting lemma**. An explicit lower bound for the unit fraction φ(r)/r, logarithmic in the bit width, supplies the independent-retry estimate. [shor_totient_ratio](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Shor.Proofs.Math.TotientBoundsLarge/Shor.shor_totient_ratio)
- **Supporting lemma**. Preparing the zero registers and applying Hadamards and modular exponentiation yields the pre-inverse-QFT state used in Section 5. [eval_orderFindingIdeal_prefix](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Shor.Proofs.NaiveShor.GoodOutcomeMassLowerBound/Shor.eval_orderFindingIdeal_prefix)
- **Supporting lemma**. There are at least φ(r) distinct good clock outcomes and each has probability at least 4/(π²r). Summation gives the order-recovery mass. [goodOutcome_card_ge_totient](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Shor.Proofs.NaiveShor.OrderFindingAnalysis/Shor.goodOutcome_card_ge_totient) · [goodOutcome_mass_lower_bound_from_card_and_pointwise](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Shor.Proofs.NaiveShor.OrderFindingAnalysis/Shor.goodOutcome_mass_lower_bound_from_card_and_pointwise) · [shor_paper_goodOutcome_lower_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Shor.Proofs.NaiveShor.OrderFindingAnalysis/Shor.shor_paper_goodOutcome_lower_bound)
- **Supporting lemma**. For the initialized ideal circuit the Born distribution agrees with the finite exponential sums of Section 5. [measProbAfter_orderFindingIdeal_eq_paper_formula](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Shor.Proofs.NaiveShor.PhaseEstimation/Shor.measProbAfter_orderFindingIdeal_eq_paper_formula)
- **Supporting lemma**. The register bounds imply r² < Q and every good outcome passes the order check within a complete scan. [goodOutcome_mass_le_probability_of_success](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Shor.Proofs.NaiveShor.Preliminaries/Shor.goodOutcome_mass_le_probability_of_success) · [shor_order_parameter_bounds](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Shor.Proofs.NaiveShor.Preliminaries/Shor.shor_order_parameter_bounds)
- **Supporting lemma**. For a circuit within its qubit ceiling, the abstract Born probability on zero input equals the finite-dimensional circuit matrix probability. [probability_of_success_eq](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Model.QuantumLib.Measure/Shor.Model.QuantumLib.probability_of_success_eq)
- **Derived result**. An executable positive-denominator scan using binary modular multiplication returns exactly the same natural number as ForShor’s specification. [executableDecoder](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.OrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFinding.executableDecoder)
- **Derived result**. At the explicit integer precision schedule the number of extra modular work bits is bounded by 70 times the data width. [extraBits_linear](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.OrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFinding.extraBits_linear)
- **Derived result**. For the two-limb reference multiplication table and explicit precision level, the same circuit evaluated by programRecovery uses O(n³) logical gates at data width n, in the ForShor elementary-gate model. [programGateCount](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.OrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFinding.programGateCount)
- **Derived result**. The explicit two-limb circuit on the zero vector, measured in its finite matrix semantics, has success at least 1/(2π² ln(1+floor(log₂ N))). Its executable decoder and precision schedule are fixed. [programRecovery](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.OrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFinding.programRecovery)
- **Derived result**. The executable precision level 2²⁶n⁴ gives enough accuracy for the lowered circuit to keep half the ideal success certificate. [referencePrecision_le](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.OrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFinding.referencePrecision_le)
- **Derived result**. Summation of Born weights over clock outcomes returning a verified factor gives a nonnegative result. [factorProbability_nonneg](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.QuantumFactoring/FQChem.Complexity.QuantumFactoring.factorProbability_nonneg)
- **Derived result**. For each good unit the circuit’s exact-order outcomes yield genuine factors after the executable gcd check. Thus the one-shot factor probability is at least the quantum recovery certificate. [goodBaseRecovery](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.QuantumFactoring/FQChem.Complexity.QuantumFactoring.goodBaseRecovery)
- **Derived result**. Every clock outcome of the physical low-level matrix circuit has nonnegative Born weight. [mass_nonneg](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.QuantumFactoring/FQChem.Complexity.QuantumFactoring.mass_nonneg)
- **Derived result**. Averaging the physical matrix Born probability over a uniformly chosen unit proves an unconditional one-run lower bound (1−2^(1−k))/(2π² ln(1+floor(log₂ N))) for odd N>1 with k distinct primes. [uniformFactorProbability](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.QuantumFactoring/FQChem.Complexity.QuantumFactoring.uniformFactorProbability)
- **Derived result**. Converting the chosen unit to the input structure preserves the multiplicative order used by the quantum circuit. [unitOrder](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.QuantumFactoring/FQChem.Complexity.QuantumFactoring.unitOrder)
- **Supporting lemma**. Computes the extra register bits at an integer reference precision in terms of the natural ceiling logarithm. [algorithm1ExtraBits_referencePrecision](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Reference.Standard.Proofs.Layout/Shor.Reference.algorithm1ExtraBits_referencePrecision)
- **Supporting lemma**. The reference allocator sets the data register width to floor(log₂(2N)). [allocateReferenceLayout_data_width](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Reference.Standard.Proofs.Layout/Shor.Reference.allocateReferenceLayout_data_width)
- **Supporting lemma**. The reference allocator sets the clock width to floor(log₂(2N²)). [allocateReferenceLayout_x_width](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Reference.Standard.Proofs.Layout/Shor.Reference.allocateReferenceLayout_x_width)
- **Supporting lemma**. At sufficiently fine approximation precision the complete lowered circuit keeps half the reference success bound. [Shor_correct_approx_lowered](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Shor.Main/Shor.Shor_correct_approx_lowered)
- **Supporting lemma**. An admissible Toom–Cook interpolation table with bounded exponent gives the logical gate-count bound for all ready layouts and linearly many extra work bits. [shorGateCountBound_of_setup](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Shor.Main/Shor.shorGateCountBound_of_setup)
- **Supporting lemma**. The constant 1/π² in the compiled success bound is positive. [κ_pos](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Shor.Proofs.Correctness/Shor.%CE%BA_pos)
- **Supporting lemma**. The reciprocal precision needed for a valid order-finding layout grows at most quartically with data width. [shorPrecision_inv_le](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Shor.Proofs.GateCount/Shor.shorPrecision_inv_le)
- **Supporting lemma**. An initialized ready layout supplies the workspace required by the compiler. [workspace](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Shor.Proofs.Workspace/Shor.LoweredShorReady.workspace)
- **Supporting lemma**. Taking the least nonzero executable candidate returns the true order whenever at least one trial recovers it. Every positive candidate passes modular verification and hence is at least the order. [selected_if_hit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.OrderFindingTrials/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingTrials.selected_if_hit)
- **Paper result**. At Shor’s binary clock, the wrong-answer probability of the independent Fourier experiments and bounded decoder is ≤1/e in ceil(12 ln(1+floor(log₂(r+1)))) trials. The joint law is the product of the normalized matrix Born distributions. [boundedErrorOrderFinding](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.OrderFindingTrials/FQChem.Complexity.OrderFindingTrials.boundedErrorOrderFinding)
- **Derived result**. Each clock outcome has nonnegative weight in the explicit projected matrix column. [mass_pos](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.CompiledSampling/FQChem.Complexity.CompiledSampling.mass_pos)
- **Derived result**. Summing over the complete clock register gives probability one. The circuit matrix is unitary, the input is the zero basis vector, and the register projectors partition the final amplitudes. [normalized](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.CompiledSampling/FQChem.Complexity.CompiledSampling.normalized)
- **Derived result**. For an arbitrary positive base, the stochastic output measure counts exactly the verified factor probability, including the immediate gcd branch. [baseLaw_certified](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringLaw/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringLaw.baseLaw_certified)
- **Derived result**. For any composite m ≤ N, preprocessing and one fair-word trial return a checked proper divisor with probability at least 1/[128(1+floor(log₂ N))]. [compositeShot](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringLaw/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringLaw.compositeShot)
- **Derived result**. Summation over uniformly sampled fair words accounts for rejected draws; one physical trial keeps at least half the mean accepted-base factor probability. [drawLaw_certified](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringLaw/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringLaw.drawLaw_certified)
- **Derived result**. Two order-finding inputs with the same base and modulus describe the same quantum experiment. [input_ext](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringLaw/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringLaw.input_ext)
