- **Derived result**. The finite-matrix circuit, bit decoder, classical gcd check and recursive splitter return primes multiplying to N with probability at least 2/3. The product is exact on every tape and the bound on trial count is cubic in the input bit length. [primeFactorization](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringLaw/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringLaw.primeFactorization)
- **Derived result**. Every accepted factor in the pushed-forward clock distribution satisfies both strict bounds and divides the modulus. [unitLaw_certified](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringLaw/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringLaw.unitLaw_certified)
- **Derived result**. Pushing the normalized clock measure through the executable decoder and factor extractor recovers its explicit finite Born sum. [unitLaw_success](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringLaw/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringLaw.unitLaw_success)
- **Derived result**. The fair-word sample space has between N−1 and 2(N−1) elements, so at least half the words encode usable positive bases. [wordBounds](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.FactoringLaw/FQChem.Complexity.FactoringLaw.wordBounds)
- **Derived result**. Whenever the verified extractor accepts the true order, every circuit outcome decoding that order returns the certified divisor. [acceptedOrder](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.QuantumFactoring/FQChem.Complexity.QuantumFactoring.acceptedOrder)
- **Derived result**. A uniform draw from all integers 1,…,N−1 and one compiled experiment yield a factor with probability at least (1−2^(1−k))/(2π² ln(1+floor(log₂ N))). This includes nonunits, for which the first gcd already splits N. [positiveBaseFactorProbability](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.QuantumFactoring/FQChem.Complexity.QuantumFactoring.positiveBaseFactorProbability)
- **Derived result**. Converting a positive coprime base less than N to a modular unit preserves its natural representative. [representative](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.QuantumFactoring/FQChem.Complexity.QuantumFactoring.representative)
- **Supporting lemma**. Every Fourier phase has unit modulus, as needed for normalization of the clock transform. [norm_qftPhase_one_shor](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.QFT.Proofs.QftPhase/Shor.norm_qftPhase_one_shor)
- **Supporting lemma**. An interval-allocated register has exactly its declared binary width. [regSize_interval](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Reference.Standard.Proofs.Layout/Shor.Reference.regSize_interval)
- **Supporting lemma**. Every positive exponent passing modular verification is at least the multiplicative order. [ord_le_of_pow_mod_eq_one](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Shor.Proofs.NaiveShor.OrderFindingAnalysis/Shor.ord_le_of_pow_mod_eq_one)
- **Supporting lemma**. A near-peak outcome for an index coprime to the order passes the complete continued-fraction decoder. [goodOutcome_r_found_one](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Implementation.Shor.Proofs.NaiveShor.Preliminaries/Shor.goodOutcome_r_found_one)
- **Supporting lemma**. The finite matrix of the lowered circuit is unitary at every qubit dimension large enough for its gates. [denote_unitary](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Model.QuantumLib.Denote/Shor.Model.QuantumLib.denote_unitary)
- **Supporting lemma**. The register projector keeps exactly the amplitudes whose measured bits encode the requested outcome. [proj_mul_apply](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Model.QuantumLib.Measure/Shor.Model.QuantumLib.proj_mul_apply)
- **Supporting lemma**. The integer obtained by reading the register bits is smaller than the register basis size. [mToNat_lt](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#ForShor.ShorVerification.Model.Standard.Basis/Shor.Model.mToNat_lt)
- **Derived result**. A schedule using only the modulus gives at least 2/t success per run of the chosen ForShor circuit, using t = 64(1+floor(log₂(1+floor(log₂N)))). The bound comes from its matrix recovery theorem with κ=1/π². [oneShot](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.VerifiedOrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.VerifiedOrderFinding.oneShot) · [trial_inverse](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.VerifiedOrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.VerifiedOrderFinding.trial_inverse)
- **Supporting lemma**. The allocated compiled circuit measures a clock of size 2^floor(log₂(2N²)). This specifies the physical input to the decoder; the ideal paper-clock theorem separately uses N²≤Q<2N². [physicalClock](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.VerifiedOrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.VerifiedOrderFinding.physicalClock)
- **Derived result**. The known-N schedule has failure at most exp(−2) < 1/3 under the actual product of independent compiled-circuit Born laws. Those tuple weights sum to one. [compiledError](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.VerifiedOrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.VerifiedOrderFinding.compiledError) · [expNegTwo_lt_third](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.VerifiedOrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.VerifiedOrderFinding.expNegTwo_lt_third) · [joint_born_mass](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.VerifiedOrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.VerifiedOrderFinding.joint_born_mass)
- **Supporting lemma**. Every decoded denominator fits the clock. Streaming the records through bit-level decoding, a zero check, and a minimum selector agrees exactly with the reference minimum of the nonzero verified estimates, with all those data-path operations counted. [decoded_le_clock](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.VerifiedOrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.VerifiedOrderFinding.decoded_le_clock) · [records_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.VerifiedOrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.VerifiedOrderFinding.records_spec) · [select_cons](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.VerifiedOrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.VerifiedOrderFinding.select_cons)
- **Derived result**. The physical bounded-error statement uses the executable Boolean decoder and streaming minimum on fresh zero-state preparations of the same compiled order-finding circuit. The number of preparations is determined from the known modulus. [physical_bounded_error](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.VerifiedOrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.VerifiedOrderFinding.physical_bounded_error)
- **Derived result**. Across the complete known-modulus repetition schedule for a valid unit input, the allocated circuit uses O(L⁴) logical gate operations and the decoder plus aggregation use ≤40,000,000 L⁵ Boolean data-path operations at sufficiently large input width L. These are loose upper bounds in the stated cost models. [polynomialWork](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.VerifiedOrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.VerifiedOrderFinding.polynomialWork)
- **Paper result**. Shor §§3–5. The normalized ideal paper-clock experiment has one-shot success ≥(4/π²)φ(r)/r and constant error after O(log log r) independent shots. A concrete compiled circuit, run for a count computed solely from N, yields the exact order with error <1/3 after Boolean decoding and aggregation. Total logical gates and classical data-path operations are polynomial in the bit length of N, with the distinct ideal and compiled clocks explicit. The separate uniformity requirement in §2 concerns the classical time to generate gate descriptions. Its cost is outside this execution bound. [shorOrderFinding](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChemForShor.VerifiedOrderFinding/FQChem.Complexity.VerifiedOrderFinding.shorOrderFinding)

## Svensson et al. (1996) · [ONIOM: A Multilayered Integrated MO + MM Method for Geometry Optimizations and Single Point Energy Predictions. A Test for Diels−Alder Reactions and Pt(P(t-Bu)3)2 + H2 Oxidative Addition](https://doi.org/10.1021/jp962071j)

- **Derived result**. Differentiating the subtractive two-layer ONIOM energy gives full-low force plus model-high force minus model-low force along one coordinate. [layeredForce](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Multiscale.ChoosingLayers.ClassicalEnvironments/FQChem.Multiscale.layeredForce)
- **Derived result**. At fixed geometry the ONIOM solver error equals the high-level imaginary-time energy excess. A finite gapped model with initial ground overlap gives pointwise convergence and, with an excitation cutoff, an exponential upper bound. [layeredProjectionError](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Multiscale.StitchingSolutions.LayeredQM/FQChem.Multiscale.layeredProjectionError) · [layeredProjectionLimit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Multiscale.StitchingSolutions.LayeredQM/FQChem.Multiscale.layeredProjectionLimit) · [projectedLayeredEnergy_eq](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Multiscale.StitchingSolutions.LayeredQM/FQChem.Multiscale.projectedLayeredEnergy_eq)
- **Derived result**. The derivative of a normalized differentiable eigenbranch of a locally symmetric bounded Hamiltonian supplies the high-level force in the one-coordinate ONIOM surface. [layeredEigenForce](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Multiscale.StitchingSolutions.LayeredQM/FQChem.Multiscale.layeredEigenForce)
- **Derived result**. On a one-coordinate Newton trajectory driven by the eigenbranch force and both MM forces, kinetic plus layered electronic potential energy has zero derivative. [layeredBornOppenheimerEnergy](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Multiscale.StitchingSolutions.LayeredQM/FQChem.Multiscale.layeredBornOppenheimerEnergy)

## Trotter (1959) · [On the product of semi-groups of operators](https://doi.org/10.1090/S0002-9939-1959-0108732-6)

- **Special case**. Two bounded generators in a complete unital real normed algebra obey the Lie–Trotter limit in norm. Trotter’s semigroup theorem also allows generators with domain conditions. [lieTrotter](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.LieTrotter/FQChem.Dynamics.lieTrotter)
- **Supporting lemma**. Telescoping bounds the norm difference of nth powers in terms of the one-step difference. For two contractions the factor is at most n. [globalStepError](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.LieTrotter/FQChem.Dynamics.globalStepError) · [power_defect](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.LieTrotter/FQChem.Dynamics.power_defect)
- **Supporting lemma**. Bounds the norm of the algebra exponential by exp of the argument norm in the bounded-generator proof. [exp_norm_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.DigitalAlgorithms.LieTrotter/FQChem.Dynamics.exp_norm_bound)
- **Derived result**. The shifted lattice interaction step is its diagonal exponential. For β,U≥0 the exact sum over binary field histories realizes the m-step Lie product at time β. [hirschHistoryMean_eq_split](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.HubbardProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC.hirschHistoryMean_eq_split) · [interactionStep_eq_exp](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.HubbardProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC.interactionStep_eq_exp)
- **Special case**. At fixed β≥0 and repulsive U, the finite-dimensional, exactly summed field-history propagator tends to exp(−β(K+V)), including after application to a fixed state. The limit refines the time grid. [hirschHistoryMean_mulVec_tendsto](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.HubbardProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC.hirschHistoryMean_mulVec_tendsto) · [hirschHistoryMean_tendsto](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.HubbardProjection/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC.hirschHistoryMean_tendsto)

## Troyer and Wiese (2005) · [Computational Complexity and Fundamental Limitations to Fermionic Quantum Monte Carlo Simulations](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevLett.94.170201)

- **Special case**. All-temperature sign-free comparison for the homogeneous, zero-field Hamiltonian in Troyer–Wiese Eq. (9), with a single burn-in and consecutive Jerrum–Sinclair proposals. For 0<ε≤1 the actual mean squared error is at most ε² times the squared quantum thermal energy, and the number of ideal edge updates is at most 16m²(m+1)n⁶[m+2+5184(m+1)⁴]/ε². The counted operations use real-valued acceptance probabilities; no finite-bit arithmetic cost is claimed. [polynomialComparison](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.polynomialComparison) · [singleChainEnergy](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.singleChainEnergy) · [singleChainWork](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.singleChainWork)
- **Derived result**. On the actual nearest-neighbour ±1/0 open cubic spin-glass inputs of Eq. (10), there are at most 3N occupied bonds. The checked single-chain sign-free energy estimator has relative RMS error at most ε with at most 764414784 N¹³/ε² ideal edge proposals, for every β>0 and every 0<ε≤1. Zero-bond or β=0 instances have exact zero energy. [cubicPolynomialComparison](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.cubicPolynomialComparison) · [latticeBudget](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.latticeBudget)
- **Supporting lemma**. Integer division counts accepted bit strings for lazy Metropolis. For positive rational proposal and boost, integer weights with a common denominator give the physical acceptance probability to within one unit of the random-word precision. [acceptanceError](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.acceptanceError) · [acceptedStrings](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.acceptedStrings) · [choiceSum](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.choiceSum) · [cutoffLe](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.cutoffLe) · [finiteAcceptance](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.finiteAcceptance) · [integerRatio](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.integerRatio) · [integerWeightSpec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.integerWeightSpec) · [wordAverage](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.wordAverage)
- **Derived result**. Two finite uniform words select the proposed bond and its acceptance. Every readout in [0,1] incurs at most (m+1)/2ᵇ one-step error for m bonds, including non-power-of-two bond counts. [finiteStepError](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.finiteStepError) · [graphReadout](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.graphReadout) · [kernelReadout](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.kernelReadout) · [pairProbability](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.pairProbability) · [pairReadout](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.pairReadout)
- **Derived result**. Row-law errors control arbitrary bounded readouts of an entire correlated trajectory. For rational weights the two-word sampler gives an explicit bound linear in the trajectory length. [finiteTrajectory](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.finiteTrajectory) · [rowL1](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.rowL1) · [trajectoryError](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticRandomBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.trajectoryError)
- **Supporting lemma**. Perturbing the thermal proposal changes the Metropolis acceptance ratio by a controlled amount. A polynomial number of precision bits pays for the change over the full graph. [boundedRatio](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticThermalBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.boundedRatio) · [powerError](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticThermalBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.powerError) · [proposalAcceptance](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticThermalBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.proposalAcceptance) · [scaleBudget](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticThermalBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.scaleBudget) · [scaleSpec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticThermalBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.scaleSpec) · [thermalRow](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticThermalBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.thermalRow) · [weightRatio](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticThermalBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.weightRatio)
- **Derived result**. For any positive temperature parameter β, use a rounded dyadic proposal and two words of (m+2)²+2+r bits per update. For a k-step trajectory and readout in [0,L] the expected readout changes by at most kL/2ʳ compared with the real-temperature chain. The dyadic numerator here is specified mathematically by a ceiling. [thermalTrajectory](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticThermalBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.thermalTrajectory) · [trajectoryWithProposal](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticThermalBits/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.trajectoryWithProposal)
- **Derived result**. From an encoded positive rational inverse temperature, rational arithmetic computes an upper enclosure of tanh β and the boosted proposal. A k-step trajectory driven by two finite words per update differs from the ideal chain by at most kL/2ʳ for any path observable in [0,L]. The direct branch uses 12(s+2) Taylor terms for s-bit accuracy. [computedThermalTrajectory](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticThermalParameter/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.computedThermalTrajectory) · [tailBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticThermalParameter/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.tailBound) · [tanhForm](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticThermalParameter/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.tanhForm) · [thermalUpperSpec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticThermalParameter/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.FerromagneticSubgraphs.RandomBits.thermalUpperSpec)
- **Supporting lemma**. Each sparse Ising bond is determined by an unordered pair of different sites. Three signed literal-copy bonds and three triangle bonds have distinct masks even when source variables or signed literals repeat. [distinctEnds](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SpinGlassSatisfiability/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SpinGlassSAT.distinctEnds) · [maskInjective](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SpinGlassSatisfiability/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SpinGlassSAT.maskInjective) · [maskUnordered](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SpinGlassSatisfiability/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SpinGlassSAT.maskUnordered)
- **Supporting lemma**. In the unrestricted two-body gadget each NAE clause gives exactly six occupied spin pairs; the Hamiltonian coefficients reproduce the signs in the executable edge table. [coupling](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SpinGlassSatisfiability/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SpinGlassSAT.coupling) · [edgeCount](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SpinGlassSatisfiability/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SpinGlassSAT.edgeCount)
- **Supporting lemma**. For the classical energy in Eq. (10), a signed two-site Walsh character contributes −1 exactly when the endpoint spins obey the sign on that bond. A negated literal may be handled by a spin flip. [bondCharacter](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SpinGlassSatisfiability/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SpinGlassSAT.bondCharacter) · [literalBond](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SpinGlassSatisfiability/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SpinGlassSAT.literalBond)
- **Derived result**. Direct truth-table verification for a new signed Ising gadget. Three copy edges and an antiferromagnetic triangle have joint energy at least −4. Equality requires exact literal copies and a not-all-equal clause. [clauseMinimum](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SpinGlassSatisfiability/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SpinGlassSAT.clauseMinimum)
- **Derived result**. For m not-all-equal clauses, the unrestricted signed two-body glass energy is the sum of the explicit clause scores; the threshold −4m is attainable exactly for satisfiable formulas. This supplies a direct satisfiability source for the energy decision in Eqs. (10)–(11). - **Supporting lemma**. Each ordinary 3-CNF clause becomes two not-all-equal clauses sharing an auxiliary bit. A common anchor permits simultaneous spin inversion. Truth-table soundness and completeness give equivalence for empty formulas, repeated variables and negated literals. - **Derived result**. For an ordinary 3-CNF on n variables with m clauses the unrestricted signed spin model has n+7m+1 sites and exactly 12m unit-strength bonds. - **Derived result**. A single signed quantum thermal-energy estimate at β=13N and relative root-mean-square precision 1/(24N) decides ordinary 3-SAT with failure probability at most 1/4 on the linear-size unrestricted two-body gadget. Its sign-free counterpart at this query is estimated by −12m. - **Supporting lemma**. A nearest-neighbour six-cycle with three negative bonds has minimum energy −4. - **Derived result**. Dense occurrence-based renaming preserves satisfiability for arbitrary natural variable labels, including duplicates and unused numeric ranges. Constructing the table uses at most (3m)² equality comparisons on variable labels. This counts label comparisons; binary labels may span multiple bits. - **Derived result**. End-to-end nearest-neighbour reduction from ordinary 3-SAT with arbitrary natural variable names. One integrable estimate having the stated relative mean-square bound decides satisfiability with error ≤1/4 using the emitted bond count as its threshold offset. At precisely that query the sign-free estimator is deterministic. Every nonempty instance also has a closed negative-weight path. An all-temperature sign-free sampling theorem and machine-level NP-to-BPP composition are outside this result. - **Paper result**. Eqs. (4), (8), (11). In the computational basis each two-site flip has a unique bond mask. Making all couplings nonnegative replaces every entry of −H and every spin-flip path weight by its exact absolute value. - **Supporting lemma**. Eq. (10). Walsh characters are signs. On a two-site mask the character is the product of the two classical Ising spins, so the absolute integer energy is bounded by the number of occupied bonds. - **Paper result**. Eq. (11). The signed quantum Hamiltonian is exactly the sum of the two-site Pauli X flip matrices multiplied by −Jₑ, with couplings 0, ±1 in units of J. [pauliExpansion](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.IsingSign/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.IsingSign.pauliExpansion)
- **Special case**. Eq. (4) and Fig. 2. A four-site square with one negative bond has a concrete length-four closed spin-flip path of unscaled weight −1. The same bosonic path has weight +1. - **Supporting lemma**. Eqs. (10)–(11). The Walsh basis gives an invertible change of basis from the matrix of signed two-site flips to the diagonal matrix of classical energies. This retains all eigenvalue multiplicities. - **Paper result**. Eqs. (3), (10)–(11). The quantum mean Tr[H exp(−βH)]/Tr[exp(−βH)] computed with the matrix exponential equals the Gibbs-weighted mean of the classical spin-glass energies for every real β. [thermal_identity](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.IsingSign/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.IsingSign.thermal_identity)
- **Supporting lemma**. Paragraph after Eq. (10). The finite energy minimum exists. Shifting to one ground configuration keeps the Gibbs mean, yields a partition sum at least one, and bounds all integer excitation energies by twice the bond count. - **Derived result**. Paragraph after Eq. (10). For N spins with integer excitation energies in [0,D], D>0 and β≥0, the finite-temperature energy exceeds the minimum by less than 2ᴺ D exp(−β). - **Paper result**. Paragraph after Eq. (10), energy unit J=1. For N>0, at most 3N occupied bonds and β≥N log 2+log(12N), a ground energy ≤t implies thermal mean <t+1/2. Otherwise the thermal mean is ≥t+1. The non-strict NO endpoint includes flat spectra and gives the required decision gap. For physical coupling J>0, the proof scales the temperature as βJ and the energy window as J/2. - **Derived result**. Paragraph after Eq. (10). An inverse temperature β=13N in units J=1 satisfies the published logarithmic lower bound for every positive spin count. - **Supporting lemma**. Eq. (9) and the reduction after Eq. (10). The absolute thermal energy is bounded by the number of occupied bonds, converting an inverse-linear relative error target into constant absolute error. - **Paper result**. Eq. (9) and note [9]. A single signed-energy estimate at β=13N with relative root mean squared error ≤1/(24N) decides the integer threshold with failure probability ≤1/4 over any probability space. This implication concerns the spin-glass decision problem; an NP-class reduction is additional. [statisticalDecision](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SignDecisionEstimator/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.IsingSign.statisticalDecision)
- **Supporting lemma**. After Eq. (11). All-aligned spins attain energy −m for m nonzero ferromagnetic bonds, and this is a ground configuration. - **Derived result**. Sign-free comparison after Eq. (11), restricted to the thermal decision query. - **Special case**. Eqs. (8)–(11), note [9]. For an explicit three-dimensional lattice with couplings 0, ±1, one signed thermal estimate gives a bounded-error ground-threshold decision. At exactly its temperature and tolerance the bosonic comparison is computed by the occupied-bond count. Barahona’s NP-hardness result, uniform machine complexity and all-temperature cluster mixing are separate claims. [cubicDecision](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.CubicSpinGlass/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.IsingSign.cubicDecision)
- **Paper result**. Eq. (4). For each integer k≥0 the closed paths with k+1 transitions sum to the signed trace Taylor coefficient β^(k+1) Tr[(−H)^(k+1)]/(k+1)!. Including the trace of the identity at order zero, the complete series converges to Tr[exp(−βH)]. [kernelPaths](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SpinGlassPaths/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.IsingSign.kernelPaths) · [taylorPartition](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SpinGlassPaths/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.IsingSign.taylorPartition) · [taylorTrace](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SpinGlassPaths/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.IsingSign.taylorTrace)
- **Paper result**. Eqs. (6)–(8). At nonnegative β the ferromagnetic Taylor weights are exactly the absolute signed weights. At any order of nonzero signed mass the signed observable mean is recovered by the ratio of two bosonic expectations. [taylorAbsolute](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SpinGlassPaths/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.IsingSign.taylorAbsolute) · [taylorReweighting](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SpinGlassPaths/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.IsingSign.taylorReweighting)
- **Special case**. For finite real signed weights the absolute-weight average of the sign gives the mean sign. Dividing the signed numerator estimate by it recovers the reweighted average when the signed partition sum is nonzero. Lean division is total. [absMean_sign](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SignProblem/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SignProblem.absMean_sign) · [abs_mul_sign](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SignProblem/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SignProblem.abs_mul_sign) · [reweighting](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SignProblem/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SignProblem.reweighting)
- **Derived result**. For independent product weights the mean sign factorizes. The explicit two-outcome weights 3 and −1 give mean sign 2⁻ᴸ. [factorizedSign](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SignProblem/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SignProblem.factorizedSign) · [productMeanSign](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SignProblem/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SignProblem.productMeanSign)
- **Derived result**. For n positive, pairwise-independent sign draws from the absolute-weight law give variance (1−s²)/n. For the explicit product example a relative RMS target δ requires nδ²≥4ᴸ−1. The paper’s separate NP-hardness reduction concerns a generic solution of the sign problem. [productSampleCost](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SignProblem/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SignProblem.productSampleCost) · [signStatistics](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SignProblem/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.SignProblem.signStatistics)
- **Proof in the literature · needs Lean proof**. [Complexity of a generic sign-problem solution](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#paper/troyer-wiese-sign-problem). Sign-free comparison and complexity definition, Eqs. (8)–(9) · spin-glass reduction and note [9]. For the constructed quantum spin model, the related sign-free bosonic system has a polynomial-time Monte Carlo method. A generic polynomial-complexity cure for estimating the signed thermal average would decide an NP-complete spin-glass instance in polynomial time with bounded error. Monte Carlo sampling gives the randomized implication NP ⊆ BPP.
  - **Remaining Lean proof**. For positive rational inverse temperature the library now computes the thermal proposal and bounds the finite-bit trajectory error for all bounded observables. The machine-level claim still needs the finite-precision energy readout and total bit-operation count, and a uniform composition of the signed decision procedure with encoded 3-SAT.
  - [Cook–Levin in Lean · NP-completeness of encoded 3-SAT](https://github.com/starpos/lean-np-complete/blob/10ebb6683345d8d4596e864f8592942dd65f882e/NPComplete/CookLevin/NPComplete.lean#L79)

## Turturean (2026) · [A proof of the Aaronson-Ambainis conjecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09773)

- **Paper result**. Turturean, Corollary 1.2, proves the Aaronson–Ambainis influence conjecture on the uniform Boolean cube. For a [0,1]-valued polynomial of degree at most d and variance at least ε > 0, some squared-flip influence is at least (ε/d)⁷. [aaronson_ambainis](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#AaronsonAmbainisPort.Main/AaronsonAmbainis.aaronson_ambainis)
- **Derived result**. The pinned proof converts Turturean’s Theorem 1.1 to the {0,1} cube and the unnormalized squared flip difference. The bound has coefficient 8√15/(125π⁴) multiplying ε^(5/2)/d⁶. [aaronson_ambainis_explicit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#AaronsonAmbainisPort.Main/AaronsonAmbainis.aaronson_ambainis_explicit)
- **Special case**. Applies the proved Boolean influence bound to the acceptance probability of a T-query phase circuit on independent uniform input bits, assuming positive variance at least ε. [acceptance_influential](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainis/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.acceptance_influential)
- **Special case**. Applies the explicit squared-flip influence bound with degree 2T to a chosen orbital population after T binary onsite pulses, assuming uniform independent inputs and variance at least ε. [orbitalPopulation_influential](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainis/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.orbitalPopulation_influential)
- **Supporting lemma**. Computes the uniform mean of a function on one Boolean bit for the two-orbital witness. [mean_oneBit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainis/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.mean_oneBit)
- **Derived result**. For the specific one-bit orbital probe, the Bernoulli antibonding readout has variance 1/4 and squared-flip influence 1. [antibondingInfluence](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainis/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.antibondingInfluence) · [antibondingVariance](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainis/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.antibondingVariance)
- **Derived result**. Instantiates the explicit influence inequality for a single binary pulse, the antibonding readout, and variance 1/4. [antibonding_influential](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.AaronsonAmbainis/FQChem.Complexity.PhaseQuery.antibonding_influential)

## Umrigar et al. (1993) · [A diffusion Monte Carlo algorithm with very small time-step errors](https://doi.org/10.1063/1.465195)

- **Special case**. For a positive guide and positive propagated guide on a finite state space, the guided transition and branching factors expand Gᵏ exactly. Nonnegative entries of G make the transition a Markov kernel. [guidedFeynmanKac](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.DiffusionMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.DiffusionMonteCarlo.guidedFeynmanKac) · [transitionMarkov](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.DiffusionMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.DiffusionMonteCarlo.transitionMarkov)
- **Derived result**. A nowhere-zero guide has constant branching exactly when it is an eigenvector. For a shifted Euler propagator, the branching factor is one minus the step times the local-energy offset. [branchingLocalEnergy](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.DiffusionMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.DiffusionMonteCarlo.branchingLocalEnergy) · [constantBranchingIff](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.DiffusionMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.DiffusionMonteCarlo.constantBranchingIff)
- **Derived result**. With a symmetric nonnegative finite propagator and a positive eigenvector guide, the guided kernel obeys detailed balance and preserves the squared guide weights. [exactGuideStationarity](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.DiffusionMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.DiffusionMonteCarlo.exactGuideStationarity)

## Vidal (2003) · [Efficient Classical Simulation of Slightly Entangled Quantum Computations](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevLett.91.147902)

- **Supporting lemma**. Elimination backend used for Lemma 2. Padding with a zero preserves the old factor entries. Materialized tables preserve values, and the deterministic first nonzero pivot scan detects exactly the nonzero rows. [expand_succ](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.RankRevealing/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSElimination.expand_succ) · [expand_zero](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.RankRevealing/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSElimination.expand_zero) · [pivot_none](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.RankRevealing/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSElimination.pivot_none) · [pivot_nonzero](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.RankRevealing/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSElimination.pivot_nonzero) · [table_apply](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.RankRevealing/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSElimination.table_apply)
- **Derived result**. Lemma 2 uses diagonalization to split the two-site tensor. This implementation instead constructs an exact rectangular rank factorization by pivot elimination over a field with exact zero tests. [factor_exact](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.RankRevealing/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSElimination.factor_exact) · [factor_matrix](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.RankRevealing/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSElimination.factor_matrix)
- **Derived result**. Eliminating a nonzero pivot strictly reduces the row-space dimension. The number of returned rank-one components equals the input rank, including for singular and zero matrices. [factor_minimal](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.RankRevealing/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSElimination.factor_minimal) · [factor_rank](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.RankRevealing/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSElimination.factor_rank) · [residual_rank](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.RankRevealing/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSElimination.residual_rank)
- **Derived result**. For an r by c dense matrix, pivot scans and the cached residual updates charge at most rc+r²+2r²c field operations and equality tests. This supplies the cubic refactorization cost used in the two-site update. [factor_work](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.RankRevealing/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSElimination.factor_work)
- **Paper result**. Lemma 1, Eq. (16). Replacing the addressed site by the one-qubit matrix applied to its physical index implements the gate for arbitrary left and right environments. [oneSite_amplitude](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.CircuitUpdate/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.oneSite_amplitude)
- **Paper result**. Lemma 2, Eq. (22). Contracting the two current sites and then applying the two-qubit gate gives every coefficient of the updated two-site tensor. Intervening bond weights are absorbed into the input sites. [theta_entry](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.CircuitUpdate/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.theta_entry)
- **Derived result**. Lemma 2, Eqs. (21)–(27). A pivot-based split reconstructs every gate-transformed coefficient. Its minimal bond is exactly the updated coefficient-matrix rank; in independent outer frames this is the full physical cut rank. [newBond](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.CircuitUpdate/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.newBond) · [pair_entry](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.CircuitUpdate/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.pair_entry) · [physicalBond](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.CircuitUpdate/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.physicalBond)
- **Derived result**. Lemma 2. With all three input bonds at most χ, contraction, gate application and exact pivot refactorization charge at most 24χ³+40χ² scalar operations and exact zero probes. [pair_work](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.CircuitUpdate/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.pair_work)
- **Supporting lemma**. Eq. (4). An open n-site qubit chain of peak bond χ stores at most 2nχ² scalar tensor entries. [left_le_width](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.CircuitSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.left_le_width) · [stored_le](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.CircuitSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.stored_le)
- **Derived result**. Theorem 1, adjacent-gate execution. Updating any addressed pair and repeating for a finite sequence gives on every bit string exactly the amplitude obtained by dense evolution. [advance_exact](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.CircuitSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.advance_exact) · [simulate_exact](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.CircuitSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.simulate_exact)
- **Derived result**. Lemmas 1–2 and Theorem 1. A G-gate adjacent sequence with actual peak virtual bond χ charges at most G(24χ³+40χ²) scalar operations. The physical Schmidt-rank justification is proved separately. [advance_work](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.CircuitSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.advance_work) · [simulate_work](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.CircuitSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.simulate_work)
- **Supporting lemma**. Paragraph preceding Theorem 1. Interleaved physical labels enumerate the bra/ket pairs. Cached matrix products and the one-site transfer implement the dense contraction for each local observable. - **Paper result**. Paragraph preceding Theorem 1. Right-to-left contraction of any product of local qubit operators is equal to the full sum over pairs of physical basis strings. At maximum bond χ it uses at most n(20χ³+8χ²) scalar operations including conjugations. [readout_exact](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.CircuitReadout/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.readout_exact) · [readout_work](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.CircuitReadout/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.readout_work)
- **Supporting lemma**. Lemma 2. Full rank gives injectivity of the linear maps from the virtual bond labels into a physical-site unfolding. [injective_of_rank](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.MinimalBonds/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.injective_of_rank) · [left_of_rank](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.MinimalBonds/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.left_of_rank) · [right_of_rank](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.MinimalBonds/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.right_of_rank)
- **Paper result**. Lemma 2, Eq. (22). Every two-site physical slice after the update is the prescribed linear combination of the pre-gate slices. - **Derived result**. Lemma 2. The rank-revealing split has full rank on both sides of the updated bond. For an invertible physical gate, the old slices can be recovered, which preserves the other two full-rank conditions. These facts maintain minimal reachable bond spaces under arbitrary gate order. - **Supporting lemma**. Eq. (2) and Lemma 2. The full-rank suffix and accumulated prefix frame recover the bond dimension from the physical flattening rank of the state at the current split. - **Special case**. Theorem 1 for nearest-neighbour circuits. - **Paper result**. Paragraph preceding Theorem 1. Transfer contraction on the computed chain equals the product-observable expectation of the original dense circuit. Taking a local projector and identity on all other qubits gives its Born probability on a normalized input. [invertible](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.GateRouting/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.Bond.invertible) · [invertible](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.GateRouting/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.Route.invertible)
- **Supporting lemma**. Routing bookkeeping identifies both physical target bits and the basis input after replacing only those bits. Adding a spectator preserves its value throughout the route. [exact_window](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.GateRouting/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.Address.exact_window) · [input_eq](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.GateRouting/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.Bond.input_eq) · [exact_window](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.GateRouting/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.Distance.exact_window) · [lift_window](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.GateRouting/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.Route.lift_window)
- **Derived result**. Routing inserts one outward and one return SWAP per skipped site and one central gate. A single arbitrary-pair operation compiles to at most 2n adjacent operations on n qubits. [gate_count](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.GateRouting/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.Address.gate_count) · [hops_le](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.GateRouting/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.Distance.hops_le) · [gate_count](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.GateRouting/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.Route.gate_count) · [lift_hops](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.GateRouting/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.Route.lift_hops)
- **Derived result**. Vidal’s nonconsecutive-gate routing step before Theorem 1. The dense action of the compiled SWAP sequence is exactly the intended matrix action on the selected original pair. [exact](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.GateRouting/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.Address.exact) · [exact](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.GateRouting/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.Route.exact) · [dense_append](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.GateRouting/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.dense_append)
- **Supporting lemma**. Every pair of numerical positions i < j < n occurs among the simulator’s addresses, including the two ends of the chain. [coverage](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.GateRouting/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.Address.coverage) · [at_gap](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.GateRouting/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.Distance.at_gap)
- **Supporting lemma**. Coordinates of computational-basis strings agree with the explicit wire permutation of a nearest-neighbor SWAP. The indexing permutation is its own inverse. [exchange_digit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.RoutedSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.Bond.exchange_digit) · [index_involutive](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.RoutedSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.Bond.index_involutive) · [digit_tabulate](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.RoutedSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.digit_tabulate) · [tabulate_digit](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.RoutedSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.tabulate_digit)
- **Derived result**. Vidal Eq. (2) bounds Schmidt rank over every bipartition. Prefix/suffix coefficient matrices over every permutation express this bound, so each SWAP inherits it on every cut. [physical](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.RoutedSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.AllCuts.physical) · [swap](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.RoutedSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.AllCuts.swap)
- **Derived result**. All intermediate states introduced by routing are wire permutations of the pre- or post-gate logical state. A physical rank bound on the original circuit suffices throughout the adjacent simulation. [trajectory](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.RoutedSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.Route.trajectory) · [compile_trajectory](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.RoutedSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.compile_trajectory) · [promised_first](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.RoutedSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.promised_first) · [trajectory_append](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.RoutedSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.trajectory_append)
- **Derived result**. An entire program on arbitrary qubit pairs compiles to an exactly equivalent adjacent circuit of invertible gates of length at most 2nG for G original operations. [compile_exact](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.RoutedSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.compile_exact) · [compile_invertible](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.RoutedSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.compile_invertible) · [compile_length](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.RoutedSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.compile_length)
- **Supporting lemma**. A one-wire operation acts with an identity on its unused neighbor. Encoding mixed circuits this way preserves dense evolution, invertibility and the rank promises at the original gate boundaries. [encode_exact](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.VidalSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.Local.encode_exact) · [encode_invertible](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.VidalSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.Local.encode_invertible) · [encode_promise](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.VidalSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.encode_promise) · [encode_reference](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.VidalSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.encode_reference)
- **Derived result**. From a nonzero product input and physical all-bipartition rank bound χ, the actual routed simulation on n ≥ 2 wires gives the dense final product expectation. Work including readout is ≤ 2nG(24χ³ + 40χ²) + n(20χ³ + 8χ²); peak scalar storage is ≤ 2nχ² + 36χ² + 4χ. [chainMeasurement_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.VidalSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.chainMeasurement_spec)
- **Supporting lemma**. Singleton-register base case: arbitrary sequences reduce to ordinary matrix–vector products in two amplitudes, with eight scalar operations per gate. [oneMatrix_act](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.VidalSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.oneMatrix_act) · [oneState_spec](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.VidalSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.oneState_spec)
- **Paper result**. Theorem 1, all widths in the scalar-arithmetic model. Given nonzero product input and G arbitrary one- or two-qubit invertible gates, χ bounds the physical Schmidt ranks across all bipartitions at the original steps. An explicit classical algorithm returns the exact product-observable expectation in ≤ 200(G+1)(n+1)(χ+1)³ operations and ≤ 100(n+1)(χ+1)² scalar slots. This includes local Born projectors on normalized states and all unitary circuits. The paper’s separate finite-precision discussion is in note [10]. [vidal_simulation](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.VidalSimulation/FQChem.Algorithms.MPSCircuit.Routing.vidal_simulation)

## Werner, Oka & Millis (2009) · [Diagrammatic Monte Carlo simulation of nonequilibrium systems](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevB.79.035320)

- **Special case**. For finite complex trajectory weights of nonzero total weight, sampling with magnitudes and restoring the phase gives the exact reweighted ratio. The average phase has modulus at most one. [realTimeReweighting](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Dynamics.RealTimePropagation.PathSampling/FQChem.Algorithms.realTimeReweighting)

## Wesołowski & Warshel (1993) · [Frozen density functional approach for ab initio calculations of solvated molecules](https://doi.org/10.1021/j100132a040)

- **Derived result**. Restricting the Coulomb constrained search to active densities plus a fixed environment gives an upper bound on the library’s molecular trial-class infimum. Attainment by a compatible split gives equality. [frozenMolecularEnergy](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Multiscale.ChoosingLayers.FrozenMolecularDensity/FQChem.Multiscale.frozenMolecularEnergy)
- **Special case**. Along a scalar density variation, the derivative of total minus active minus constant frozen energy equals the difference of the first two derivatives. [frozenDensityDerivative](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Multiscale.ChoosingLayers.QuantumEnvironments/FQChem.Multiscale.frozenDensityDerivative)
- **Derived result**. In an additive density model each active-plus-frozen split must be feasible. Restricting the infimum gives an upper bound, with equality if a split realizes the unrestricted infimum. [frozenSearch](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Multiscale.ChoosingLayers.QuantumEnvironments/FQChem.Multiscale.frozenSearch)

## White (1992) · [Density matrix formulation for quantum renormalization groups](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevLett.69.2863)

- **Supporting lemma**. Contracting fixed left and right environments with a free site tensor gives the entries of the explicit linear map to physical states. [environmentMap_apply](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.DMRG/FQChem.DMRG.environmentMap_apply)
- **Supporting lemma**. The compressed Hamiltonian B†HB is Hermitian for Hermitian H. The local quotient using overlap B†B agrees with the physical Rayleigh quotient on the embedded tensor. [effective_symmetric](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.DMRG/FQChem.DMRG.effective_symmetric) · [energy_identity](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.DMRG/FQChem.DMRG.energy_identity)
- **Special case**. With fixed environments an exact minimizing site update cannot raise the energy. For an isometric embedding of a nonzero finite-dimensional site space and Hermitian H, a lowest effective eigenvector attains the local minimum. This concerns a single update. [canonicalUpdate](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.DMRG/FQChem.DMRG.canonicalUpdate) · [oneSiteUpdate](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.DMRG/FQChem.DMRG.oneSiteUpdate)

## White (2005) · [Density matrix renormalization group algorithms with a single center site](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevB.72.180403)

- **Supporting lemma**. Contracting fixed left and right environments with a free site tensor gives the entries of the explicit linear map to physical states. [environmentMap_apply](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.DMRG/FQChem.DMRG.environmentMap_apply)
- **Supporting lemma**. The compressed Hamiltonian B†HB is Hermitian for Hermitian H. The local quotient using overlap B†B agrees with the physical Rayleigh quotient on the embedded tensor. [effective_symmetric](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.DMRG/FQChem.DMRG.effective_symmetric) · [energy_identity](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.DMRG/FQChem.DMRG.energy_identity)
- **Special case**. With fixed environments an exact minimizing site update cannot raise the energy. For an isometric embedding of a nonzero finite-dimensional site space and Hermitian H, a lowest effective eigenvector attains the local minimum. This concerns a single update. [canonicalUpdate](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.DMRG/FQChem.DMRG.canonicalUpdate) · [oneSiteUpdate](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.VariationalOptimization.TensorNetworks.DMRG/FQChem.DMRG.oneSiteUpdate)

## Wick (1950) · [The Evaluation of the Collision Matrix](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRev.80.268)

- **Special case**. For an orthonormal complex occupied frame, the one-body expectation is the density entry and the two-body expectation is its antisymmetrized two-by-two minor. These are number-conserving two- and four-operator cases of Wick contraction on a single determinant. [oneBody](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction.oneBody) · [twoBody](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction/FQChem.Complexity.SlaterContraction.twoBody)

## Zalka (1999) · [Grover’s quantum searching algorithm is optimal](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevA.60.2746)

- **Paper result**. Sections 2–3, equal-prior single-marked phase-oracle search. For every nonempty finite database, normalized initial workspace, oracle-independent gates and projective readout, average Born success up to the first maximum is bounded by the success of the explicit Grover circuit with the same query count. [grover_is_optimal](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality/FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality.grover_is_optimal)
- **Paper result**. Sharp sine-squared success upper bound through the first maximum and its attainment by computational-basis marking, uniform preparation and one common diffusion reflection per call. For at least two items the construction gives the sine law at every iteration. [grover_attains](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality/FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality.grover_attains) · [optimal_average_success](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality/FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality.optimal_average_success)
- **Paper result**. If equal-prior success tends to one, then for every positive ε, all sufficiently large N require at least (π/4−ε)√N queries. The algorithm and workspace may vary with N. [near_certain_queries](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality/FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality.near_certain_queries)
- **Derived result**. The angle of the square root of average success is at most (2T+1) arcsin(1/√N), via a direct sum over candidate labels, the identity-oracle trajectory and a reflection about the readout sectors. [query_angle_bound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality/FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality.query_angle_bound)
- **Supporting lemma**. Hilbert direct-sum geometry, Born mass and reflections used in the upper bound, plus the computational basis, normalized uniform state and explicit Grover iterate used for equality. [angle_formula](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality/FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality.angle_formula) · [angle_map](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality/FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality.angle_map) · [coordinateProjection](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality/FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality.coordinateProjection) · [grover_run](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality/FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality.grover_run) · [reference_angle](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality/FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality.reference_angle) · [reflection_angle](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality/FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality.reflection_angle) · [run_norm](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality/FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality.run_norm) · [success_mem](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality/FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality.success_mem) · [uniform_norm](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality/FQChem.Complexity.SearchOptimality.uniform_norm)

## Zhang & Krakauer (2003) · [Quantum Monte Carlo Method using Phase-Free Random Walks with Slater Determinants](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevLett.90.136401)

- **Special case**. With nonzero individual and total overlaps, importance-weighted local energies reproduce the mixed estimator. A left eigenbra gives its eigenvalue on each overlapping walker. [exactTrialLocalEnergy](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC.exactTrialLocalEnergy) · [mixedEstimator](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC.mixedEstimator)
- **Derived result**. For the paper’s max(0,cos Δθ) weight projection, loss in the product of cosine factors is bounded by half the sum of squared phase increments. The argument fixes every other weight factor. [phaselessWeightLoss](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.AFQMC.phaselessWeightLoss)

## Zwanzig (1954) · [High-Temperature Equation of State by a Perturbation Method. I. Nonpolar Gases](https://doi.org/10.1063/1.1740409)

- **Special case**. For a nonempty finite canonical ensemble and β>0, the difference of free energies is −β⁻¹ log of the reference expectation of exp(−βΔU). [zwanzig](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.Multiscale.StitchingSolutions.Observables/FQChem.Multiscale.zwanzig)

## ten Haaf et al. (1995) · [Proof for an upper bound in fixed-node Monte Carlo for lattice fermions](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevB.51.13039)

- **Special case**. For a real symmetric lattice model and a nowhere-zero guide, the fixed-node quadratic form adds a sum of nonnegative penalties on sign-flip edges. [fixedNodeShift](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.DiffusionMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.DiffusionMonteCarlo.fixedNodeShift) · [penalty_nonneg](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.DiffusionMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.DiffusionMonteCarlo.penalty_nonneg)
- **Paper result**. The finite real lattice fixed-node ground-energy infimum bounds the original one from above for any guide nonzero on every site, by positivity of the sign-flip correction. [fixedNodeUpperBound](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.DiffusionMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.DiffusionMonteCarlo.fixedNodeUpperBound)
- **Special case**. Trial energies agree exactly when the trial-to-guide ratios match on each sign-flip edge. If a normalized minimizing state matches those ratios, the two ground energies coincide, including for an exact nonvanishing guide. [exactFixedNode](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.DiffusionMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.DiffusionMonteCarlo.exactFixedNode) · [exactGuideGroundEnergy](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.DiffusionMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.DiffusionMonteCarlo.exactGuideGroundEnergy) · [fixedNodeEqualityIff](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.DiffusionMonteCarlo/FQChem.StatePrep.ImaginaryTimeProjection.DiffusionMonteCarlo.fixedNodeEqualityIff)

## Čížek (1966) · [On the Correlation Problem in Atomic and Molecular Systems. Calculation of Wavefunction Components in Ursell-Type Expansion Using Quantum-Field Theoretical Methods](https://doi.org/10.1063/1.1727484)

- **Supporting lemma**. The two opposite cluster exponentials are inverse matrices, for any finite index set. This supplies the cancellation in the coupled-cluster similarity transform. [expClusterInverse](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.PerturbationTheory.CoupledCluster/FQChem.VariationalMethods.expClusterInverse)
- **Supporting lemma**. In a finite matrix space e^(−T)He^T has the reference as an eigenvector precisely when H has e^T times the reference as an eigenvector, at the same energy. The result addresses exact similarity before any truncation or amplitude solver. [coupledCluster](https://fqchem.matterlab.ca/#FQChem.StatePrep.PerturbationTheory.CoupledCluster/FQChem.VariationalMethods.coupledCluster)
